# Research provenance

> **Historical note (added 2026-08-31).** Several techniques below turn out to
> have direct precedent in the project author's own 1994 system — IDUR
> (M. Sharpe, N. Ahmed & G. Sutcliffe, "An Intelligent Document Understanding
> & Reproduction System," MVA '94, p. 267,
> [PDF](http://b2.cvl.iis.u-tokyo.ac.jp/mva/proceedings/CommemorativeDVD/1994/papers/1994267.pdf)):
> Hough skew correction via the same Hinds et al. (1990) reference our
> `deskew.hough` cites, RLSA (whose noise sensitivity IDUR already noted),
> text/picture/line-drawing block classification by image features (the
> ancestor of our `imagezones`), and Nagy's X-Y trees. mlws-ocr re-derived
> these independently before the paper resurfaced. The author's directional
> k-NN + SCC segmenter (1995, `layout/knn_scc.py`) grew from the same work.

Every algorithm and approach in mlws-ocr, with its lineage, where it lives
in this codebase, and how our implementation deviates. Rule of the house:
**a new algorithm does not land without an entry here and a docstring
naming its source.**

This file is the provenance and the measurement behind every decision,
in the order the decisions were made. The current numbers live elsewhere:
the scoreboard and its history in `DESIGN.md` §8, the comparison with
Tesseract's two engines in `TESSERACT.md`, the headline table in the
README. The whole-system lineage is `DESIGN.md` §11.

Status: ✅ implemented · 🔬 implemented, superseded or optional · 🗺 planned

## Image cleanup

| Technique | Lineage | Code | Notes / deviations |
|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ Projection-profile deskew | H.S. Baird, "The Skew Angle of Printed Documents" (1987); W. Postl (1986) | `cleanup/deskew.py` `ProjectionDeskew` | Row-sum variance as the alignment energy; coarse-to-fine search on a downsampled Otsu ink mask instead of Baird's connected-component sampling. |
| ✅ Hough-transform deskew | S.N. Srihari & V. Govindaraju, "Analysis of textual images using the Hough transform" (1989); Hinds, Fisher & D'Amato (1990) | `cleanup/deskew.py` `HoughDeskew` | One reference point per CC (bottom-center ≈ baseline); accumulator scored by Σ(bin²), equivalent to profile variance on points. ~9× faster than raster rotation. |
| ✅ Sauvola adaptive binarization | J. Sauvola & M. Pietikäinen, "Adaptive document image binarization", Pattern Recognition 33 (2000) | `cleanup/binarize.py` `SauvolaBinarize` | Via scikit-image. Known behavior: cuts thinner strokes on sharp clean text than a fixed 0.5 gray threshold (measured; see RESEARCH-NOTES in memory of 2026-08-30 sev-0 study). |
| ✅ Otsu global threshold | N. Otsu, "A threshold selection method from gray-level histograms" (1979) | `cleanup/binarize.py` `OtsuBinarize` | Baseline comparator in the registry. |
| ✅ Illumination flattening by background division | Flat-fielding; morphological background estimation à la S. Sternberg's rolling ball (1983) | `cleanup/illumination.py` | Background = heavy median filter on a downsampled copy, then divide. Median replaces the rolling ball. |
| ✅ Despeckle by component area | Standard document-image practice (see O'Gorman & Kasturi, *Document Image Analysis*, 1995) | `cleanup/despeckle.py` | Area threshold declared at 300 dpi, scaled by (dpi/300)². |
| ✅ Deskew: pixels rotated out of the frame are dropped, not piled on the edge row (2026-09-12) | Baird 1987 (the alignment energy must be computed over the same pixels at every angle) | `cleanup/deskew.py` `_InkProjector.variance`; `tests/test_cleanup_stages.py` | The coordinate projector clipped an out-of-frame row index to the edge row. On two of the sixteen first magazine and newspaper pages a large halftone photograph reaching the bottom of the page put thousands of pixels into one row at the extreme angles, the fake spike won the search at the full 5 degrees (5.5 after the unclipped refinement step), and the rotated page lost its text columns to the image-zone stage: newspaper page 8227_030 read 0.7% char, magazine page 8056_030 9.8%. Dropping the pixels, as `ndimage.rotate` does, reads 0.45 and 0.15 degrees there and changes NOTHING on the 105 pages of the four standard sets (every estimate identical to the fine step, so the four-set rows stand without a re-run). A Baird-style projector over text-sized components only was tried in the same probe and did NOT fix the magazine page (4.65 degrees: halftone dots are text-sized). |
| ✅ Scanner-frame clearing in the illumination stage (`frame_dark`) — a Library of Congress page went from 176 lines, 24.1 / 32.9 and five minutes to 19 lines, **96.2 / 86.1** and 19 s; nothing else moved; ADOPTED in every profile (2026-09-22) | The Rumor Project harvest read nothing: the lines overlay showed a black scanner frame binarised as a band of speckle along all four edges | `cleanup/illumination.py` `scanner_frame`: the raw gray's box mean (15 px at 300 dpi) below 0.5 marks dark regions; those touching at least THREE of the image's four edges are the frame (a surround touches four, a lid's strip three), grown by the box size, and set to paper before the division. `tests/test_border.py`. Three forms measured: (1) despeckle-stage clearing of edge-touching binary components — inert, because the median background inside a 75-px frame IS the frame, so division had already turned it into paper-white speckle that touches no edge; (2) illumination-stage clearing of ANY edge-touching dark region — took a newspaper's halftone bleeding off a corner and cost that page 4.7 words (news-8 92.8 → 92.2), and hollowness / thin-band tests still let the patchy halftone through; (3) the three-edge rule | Census of pages with a frame under (3): UNLV 0 of 400, modern 0, business 0, FUNSD 0, SROIE 14 of 60 (receipts on dark tables), Legal Reports 10 of 40, Rumor 100 of 100. Measured, char / word: Rumor page 24.1 / 32.9 → 96.2 / 86.1 (recall 80.9 → 86.7, precision 56.0 → 86.7; 5 min 20 s → 19 s, the 176 junk lines had been the whole decode time); Legal Reports 81.0 / 60.9 → 81.1 / 61.1; SROIE 71.0 / 41.1 identical; news-8 95.5 / 92.8 identical; dev-8, legal-8, broad-30, business identical (their pages carry no frame, so the rule is the identity there by construction and by measurement). Adopted in every profile; the classic profile's dev-8 reproduces. What the exercise taught: the order of the cleanup stages decides what a rule can see — a frame is a gray-level fact and is gone by the time the binary exists; and "dark and touching the edge" needs the topological qualifier (three edges) because magazines and newspapers bleed dark pictures off their corners. |

## Layout analysis

| Technique | Lineage | Code | Notes / deviations |
|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ Recursive XY-cut | G. Nagy & S. Seth, "Hierarchical representation of optically scanned documents" (ICPR 1984) | `layout/blocks.py` | Widest-gap-first axis choice; depth-first traversal order used directly as reading order. |
| ✅ Ruling detection by run-length morphology | Document morphology practice; cf. Wong, Casey & Wahl, "Document Analysis System" (IBM JRD 1982); D. Bloomberg's leptonica writings | `layout/rulings.py` | Opening with 1×L / L×1 elements; rules removed (dilated ×2 — ×1 left stubs) before line finding. Tolerance pass (default on): closing bridges hairline breaks and dilation PERPENDICULAR to the rule direction covers waviness — a 1–2 px rule wandering ±1 px never yields the straight run a strict opening demands. Found via a 400 dpi outlier page that lost its column rules entirely (−8 char); fix took it from 61.0→69.9% and turned the paired 300-vs-400 study into a consistent 4B win (mean 81.2→83.5). Still skew-sensitive ≳1° — noted gap. |
| ✅ Projection-profile line segmentation + baseline estimate | Classic; see survey: R. Cattoni et al., "Geometric layout analysis techniques" (1998) | `layout/lines.py` | Baseline = last row ≥ 25% of peak ink scanning upward (descender-robust heuristic). |
| ✅ Image/halftone zone removal | Text/image segmentation: Wong, Casey & Wahl (IBM JRD 1982); D. Bloomberg, "Multiresolution morphological analysis of document images" (1991); L. Fletcher & R. Kasturi, "A robust algorithm for text string separation from mixed text/graphics images" (PAMI 1988) for the line-art rule | `layout/imagezones.py` | Giant well-filled components + dense coarse-scale regions, union'd, grown, removed from ink before layout. Found via UNLV magazines: photo silhouettes bridge every whitespace channel, XY-cut collapses to one mega-block, and glyphs get union-grouped into photo components (138 groups on a page with ~500 words; 1116 after the stage). Zones must clear a minimum area (min_zone_frac): bold headline glyphs trip the density detector locally and were getting eaten (measured on newsprint). LINE-ART DETECTORS (2026-09-01, from the broad-30 deletion decomposition): (1) large HOLLOW components — big in BOTH dimensions (≥120px each at 300dpi) but sparsely filled, the Fletcher-Kasturi size rule the well-filled test misses (display glyphs and letter-spaced logos are big in one dimension only, so they stay); (2) ZONE ABSORPTION — a component ≥4× median CC dim within 12px of a detected zone is a remnant of the same graphic. Root case (visual session on UNLV 8509): a mailbag illustration's dense half was zoned while its line-art envelope spill stayed, welded two opening paragraphs into one unsplittable "line", and 507 chars of body text vanished into the garbage gate. After: 8509 deletions 507→33 chars; broad-30 char 77.5→78.4 (word flat 52.4, recall +0.8); news-10 75.2 / mag-6 59.8 — both far above their stale baselines, no absorption collateral. Regression fixture: tests/test_imagezones.py::test_line_art_removed_text_kept. |
| ✅ Tall-line adaptive re-split | Profile line finding with adaptive thresholds (cf. projection-profile line segmentation surveys; smoothing per Wong-Casey-Wahl) | `layout/lines.py` (profile impl) | Second deletion-decomposition fix (2026-09-01): on wide blocks the fixed noise floor (0.002×block width) sits BELOW inter-line valleys — descender/ascender overlap plus photocopy speckle keeps 1–2% of a wide row inked — so multiple text lines fuse into one segment whose vertically-stacked glyph groups recognize as i/l/! garbage and get suppressed (UNLV 8718: a 3-line paragraph, valleys 7–38 ink vs floor 3.4). Fix: any line taller than 1.8× the page median height is re-profiled with a threshold adaptive to its own peak (12%), smoothed over 5 rows (raw ascender rows sputter around any fixed cut and shed veto fragments — first attempt failed exactly there), tiny fragments merged into neighbors; a genuinely tall single line (display type, signature scribble) has no deep interior valley and passes unchanged. Results: 8718 deletions 193→0, 8753 169→0, 8509 residual 33→0; broad-30 77.5→78.4 (line art)→78.7 char over the two segmentation legs. |
| ✅ Per-glyph rejection retired as default | Reject-option classification (Chow's rule, 1970: reject when error probability exceeds the rejection cost) — revisited as the classifier improved | `decode/beam.py` `reject_mads=None` | Third deletion-decomposition fix (2026-09-01): the confusion report showed ~600 '?' outputs standing in for common letters. Re-sweep of the adaptive reject threshold (median+k·MAD), which measured FLAT (k 3–10) when introduced, turned strictly monotonic-DOWN with the harvest-strengthened prototypes: dev-8 85.7→88.2 char / 65.2→68.3 word from k=5 to off; broad-30 confirmed +1.6 char/+2.0 word; synthetic +2.7/+3.9/+3.6 char, word sev1 72.3→85.0. Chow's logic inverted: when the classifier was weak, '?' beat its guesses; now every '?' is a guaranteed error while beam+LM's best guess is usually right. Junk-line defense belongs to the garbage-suppression gate (line-level, lexicon+confidence-based), not per-glyph rejection. Mechanism kept as an opt-in parameter; per-char confidences still reported for downstream consumers. |
| ✅ Robust line x-height (caps-line fix) | Bimodal 2-means clustering (same pattern as the gap_band spacing fix); page-level typographic anchoring | `decode/beam.py` `_line_x_height` | Fourth confusion-cluster fix (2026-09-01): case-flip context analysis showed 225 of 519 upper→lower flips inside ALL-CAPS words and most of the 207 word-start flips on caps-heavy lines (letterheads, org names, address lines). Root: per-line x-height = median glyph height, which IS the cap height on such lines, so every capital paid the -1.2 "too-tall lowercase" prior and whole words flipped. Fix: cluster glyph ASCENTS (baseline to top — descenders don't pollute) 2-means per line; bimodal → low mode is the true x-height; unimodal AND ≥1.25× the page's lowercase anchor (median over lines of median ascent — body lines dominate) → caps line, use the anchor. dev-8 +1.3 char/+2.2 word; broad-30 80.3→81.0 char, 54.6→55.9 word; synthetic word +0.9/+1.4/+2.0. Remaining case tail: i→I/b→B word-start flips (pixel-identical twins, Capitalized pattern legal) and sentence-initial caps — needs a sentence-case prior, not size evidence. |
| ✅ Sentence-case repair pass | Orthographic post-correction (case restoration/truecasing lineage, e.g. Lita et al., "tRuEcasIng", ACL 2003) applied ONLY where pixels are silent | `decode/beam.py` `_sentence_case_pass` | Word-level case repair for first letters whose case twins pixels cannot separate (c,o,s,u,v,w,x,z size twins + i/I,b/B,p/P tall pairs): flip UP at a sentence start, flip DOWN a lone Capitalized corpus-frequent word mid-sentence. Three measured hazards shaped the guards: (1) up-flips are DOCUMENT-CALIBRATED — applied only when the document's own unambiguous sentence starts are ≥3:1 capitalized (all-lowercase documents and fixtures must not have style imposed); (2) sentence state across an unpunctuated line break is UNKNOWN, not mid-sentence — no flips there ("Call me Ishmael" at a verse-style line start was flipping down); (3) neighbor-capitalization veto protects proper-noun runs ("San Antonio"), and down-flips require very-frequent lowercase forms (>-10). Net: broad-30 +0.1 char/+0.4 word (81.1/56.3); synthetic word −0.3 avg residual — the irreducible cost is lone ambiguous-initial proper nouns with frequent lowercase twins ("Ishmael"→"ishmael", "Brown"→"brown" when a neighbor misreads); accepted as the real-page/synthetic trade and recorded. |
| ✅ Dehyphenation pass | Standard OCR post-processing (line-break hyphen removal, cf. any dehyphenation heuristic in OCR literature); lexicon-gated | `decode/beam.py` `_dehyphenate_pass` | From the dash-insertion context study (2026-09-01): ground truth reads "indi-\nvidual" as "individual". Two variants: (a) cross-line join (line-final "xxx-", next line of the SAME block starts lowercase, joined core endorsed; the same-block guard correctly refuses paragraph-final period-misread-as-dash + new sentence); (b) token-level (the wrapped halves often arrive already merged as "indi-vidual": strip the internal hyphen when the joined core is a word and neither the hyphenated form nor both halves are — "self-service"/"Michigan-Dearborn" keep theirs). Aggregate-NEUTRAL on broad-30 (cases too rare: precision +0.1, else flat) — kept as a correctness improvement; the rest of the dash cluster is garbled letterhead-region debris (the open letterhead recognition problem), not a dash-specific mechanism. |
| ✅ Word-level case coherence | Word-case consistency as a unit constraint (print words are all-lower / Capitalized / ALL-CAPS); post-hoc repair over pixel-ambiguous letters only | `decode/beam.py` `_word_case_coherence` | Letterhead diagnosis on 8571 (2026-09-01) showed the survivors after the day's fixes were case-jittered caps words: "MiCHIGAN", "ADDREss", "DETRoiT" — the beam's per-transition case_change_penalty (1.5/flip) loses to pixel deltas over a long word. When ≥70% of a word's letters agree on a case, the pixel-ambiguous minority (c,o,s,u,v,w,x,z,i,b,p twins) joins them; first letter exempt from down-flips; unambiguous shapes never touched. broad-30 81.1→81.2 char, 56.3→57.1 word; synthetic sev2 word +0.5, rest unchanged. |
| ✅ Punctuation position prior | Baseline-relative mark disambiguation (same geometry family as the descender prior) | `decode/beam.py` `punct_position_prior` | Corpus report: '.'→'-' ×69, '.'→',' ×51 — at 3×4 pixels the SHAPES of tiny marks cannot be told apart, but their VERTICAL POSITION can: '.' sits on the baseline, ',' hangs below, '-' and quotes float above. Among tiny-punct candidates the position-consistent mark gains 1.2 and the inconsistent ones pay it. broad-30 81.2→81.4 char, 57.1→57.9 word, recall +0.9; synthetic unchanged. |
| ✅ Mixed-alphanumeric repair | Twin-substitution post-correction (extends the digit-mode twin machinery both directions) | `decode/beam.py` `_mixed_alnum_repair` | Corpus report showed strays both ways: "0f" for "Of", "482D2" for "48202". A digit inside an alpha word flips to its letter twin ONLY when the lexicon endorses the result; a letter inside a number flips to its digit twin ONLY when flanked by digits on both sides (leading letter runs are product codes: "CD23021" stays); number+unit tokens (9am, 1st, 35mm) exempt. The unit study caught a would-have-shipped bug — "9am"→"gam" via a dictionary word — now a regression test (tests/test_decode.py::test_mixed_alnum_repair). broad-30 81.4→81.5 char, 57.9→58.3 word, recall/precision +0.4 each. |
| ✅ Dot/mark component-count prior | Multi-component class evidence from the grouping stage (the same i-dot grouping O'Gorman & Kasturi describe); mirrors our descender and tiny-punct priors | `decode/beam.py` `dot_prior` | The vertical-stroke confusion family (l↔i, i→I, i→d, t→l/1 — ~190 errors) survived the GED gate re-sweep because its distinguishing evidence is not in the skeleton at all: it is the PART COUNT the components stage already recorded when it merged a dot with its stem, and the decoder never consulted it. Measured on dev-8 top-1s: 'i' groups are 97% two-part and 't/l/1/T' ~100% one-part, while 22% of 'I' readings and 62% of 'j' readings contradict their class. A soft ±1.0 prior (photocopy breaks can split any stroke, so pixels and the LM keep veto power) favours dotted classes (i, j, !, marks-above accents) in two-part groups and undotted ones in one-part groups. dev-8 90.1→90.3 char / 71.8→72.3 word; broad-30 word 58.3→58.8, recall +0.3, precision +0.3. LESSON: when a rerank sweep measures flat, suspect missing evidence upstream rather than mis-tuned gates. |
| 🔬 Space-insertion repair by lexicon (NEGATIVE — vocabulary, not logic) | Fragment-join repair extended to long+short splits | `decode/beam.py` (`_join_decoded`, extension REVERTED) | Corpus space study (broad-30): 458 inserted spaces = 313 mid-word + 154 at punct/digit boundaries; deletions (232) are almost all tabular ("3\|x", "5\|3.00"). Mid-word splits come from the DEFINITE-gap path, which splits unconditionally — only the uncertain band is lexicon-gated. An extension admitting long-head + short-non-word-tail runs fired ZERO times and measured bit-identical on broad-30. Root cause, measured directly: the joined forms needed ("trademarks", "deadlines", "schoolers", "counseling") are ABSENT from the lexicon (frequency −18 = unknown), while the joins that must be AVOIDED ("fora", "asa" from "for a", "as a") ARE endorsed — the corpus (5 Victorian novels + dict-words) is anti-correlated with modern business vocabulary, so any lexicon-keyed join rule fires on exactly the wrong cases. Extension reverted. This promotes MODERN CORPUS COVERAGE from a nice-to-have to the blocking item for this error class. |
| ✅ Lexicon inflection expansion | Regular English morphology applied to a base-form word list (classic lexicon conditioning; the list is a Webster-derived base-form dictionary) | `scripts/build_langmodel.py` `inflections()` | Root cause found while chasing the space-insertion cluster: `/usr/share/dict/words` holds BASE FORMS ONLY — "trademark" present, "trademarks" absent; a 3000-entry sample found **100% missing their -s plural**. Because the decoder gates every split, join and substitution on lexicon endorsement, essentially every plural noun and third-person verb in a real document was unendorsed unless the corpus happened to contain it. Regular rules only (-s/-es/-ies/-ed/-ing; irregulars left to corpus evidence): lexicon 236k → 814k entries. broad-30 word 58.8→59.3, recall +0.3, precision +0.5 (char flat). Second round (2026-09-04): the inflections applied to the base-form list only, so corpus words gained no forms — 'airline' and 'onboard' were in, 'airlines' and 'onboarding' (the missing-word report's top entries on the modern letters) out; inflecting the corpus words too adds 26k forms (818k → 844k). Measured dev-8 flat, broad-30 flat (recall −0.1), modern +0.3 word / +0.2 recall / +0.2 precision; adopted. Trap recorded: `build_langmodel.py` DEFAULTS to `data/corpus` (a multilingual scratch directory) — the live English lexicon is built from `data/corpus_en_plus`; a rebuild from the default silently lost 'airline'. |
| ✅ Modern public-domain corpus | US federal government works, public domain by 17 U.S.C. §105 (Congressional bills + Federal Register) | `scripts/fetch_modern_corpus.sh`, `data/corpus_en_modern/` (gitignored) | The corpus behind both the lexicon frequencies and the char LM was five Victorian novels (667k words). Ordinary business vocabulary was either UNKNOWN or pinned at the flat dictionary floor, where it cannot win a decode contest: "email" −18→−8.3, "fax" −18→−10.4, "program" −15→−7.1, "software" −15→−10.2, "membership" −15→−9.7. Federal sources chosen over Wikipedia/EDGAR so a public repo carries no licensing entanglement; 12MB added, corpus 667k→2.4M words. Measured: dev-8 90.2→90.7 char / 72.2→74.7 word; broad-30 81.9→82.4 char / 59.3→60.8 word (first time over 60), recall +0.9, precision +0.9. Largest single leg since the harvest flywheel, and it works by letting mechanisms that ALREADY existed act on words they previously had no evidence for. |
| ✅ Char-GRU retrained on the enlarged corpus | Our own numpy char-GRU (see the language-model entry); retrained, not redesigned | `scripts/train_charlm.py --corpus data/corpus_en_plus` → `data/gru_en.npz` | The character model had learned its statistics from the same five Victorian novels as the old lexicon. Retraining on novels + modern federal text (2.4M words) improved the model on its own terms — validation perplexity 3.27 → 2.59 — and compounded with the lexicon leg rather than overlapping it: dev-8 90.7→91.1 char / 74.7→75.2 word; broad-30 82.4→83.1 char / 60.8→62.1 word, recall +1.4, precision +1.8. Synthetic held (96.3/96.8/95.9 char; word 85.9/87.9/85.4, sev1/2 +1.5/+1.4). Combined corpus leg (inflections + modern text + retrained GRU): broad-30 **81.9→83.1 char, 58.8→62.1 word, recall +2.6, precision +3.2**. |
| ✅ Broken-character merge lattice (associator) | Tesseract's legacy "chop and associate" — fragment association over a segmentation search; the inverse of our existing split lattice | `glyph/components.py` (`merge_*`), `recognize/stage.py`, `decode/beam.py` (`max_merge_variants`) | Found by the paired legacy comparison (`scripts/compare_legacy.py`), which showed the gap is CONSISTENCY, not peak quality: legacy's worst page of 30 is 80.8% char, ours was 29.9%, and on 9 pages we were already within 5 points. Diagnosing the worst page (UNLV 8718, an American Express letter in a fine old-style serif) at pixel level: the scan is clean and legible, but the thin top/bottom hairlines of round letters have dropped out in print, so every 'o' arrives as TWO disconnected arcs — "company" read as "c(21III)any", a 25-char line yielding 32 groups. We had a split lattice for touching characters and nothing to put broken pieces back together. Mirror mechanism added: components proposes unions of sub-glyph fragments, recognize scores the union crop, decode enumerates merge variants alongside split variants (adjacent merges conflict and are excluded). TWO CORRECTIONS during the work, both measured: (1) the size reference must be median glyph HEIGHT, not width — on a page where letters break apart the median WIDTH is itself halved, so a width-based rule refuses exactly the merges it exists to propose (fired 0 times before the fix, 464 after); (2) merge_char_bonus = 0.0, NOT the mirror of split_char_bonus — a split ADDS a character and must offset the extra glyph+LM terms it accumulates, whereas a merge REMOVES one and its shorter path is already favoured, so a bonus double-counts (swept on dev-8: 0.0 → 91.1 char, 2.2 → 90.8). RESULTS: page 8718 char 29.9→57.7; broad-30 83.1→83.9 char, 62.1→62.8 word, recall +0.9, precision +0.7; clean pages unchanged (8582 97.8, 20 merge hypotheses vs 464 on the broken page — the rule is selective); synthetic held (sev2 word +0.5). LESSON: the gap to a mature classical engine is a catalogue of failure-mode mechanisms, and each one shows the same signature — flat on clean pages, large recovery where the system was catastrophic. |
| 📊 Legacy-gap error decomposition | Paired error-type analysis against a common ground truth (standard OCR evaluation practice; cf. the ISRI/UNLV accuracy reports) | `scripts/compare_legacy_errors.py` | Both engines aligned to the same truth, every error bucketed. RESULT (2026-09-01): excluding the three catastrophic pages we score **86.8 vs legacy 95.5 — still 8.7 points behind on ordinary pages**; the catastrophic tail explains only ~¼ of the 11.4-point gap, the rest is SYSTEMATIC. Composition of the systematic excess (27 pages, 39,952 truth chars): shape substitutions +1397 (40%; ours 1667 vs legacy 270 — SIX times as many, the classifier itself), character deletions +716 (20%), case flips +391 (11%; legacy makes **10** case errors in 40k chars, we make 401), digit substitutions +373 (11%), punctuation +337 (10%), spaces +257 (7%). We are BETTER than legacy on spurious insertions (−27) — the suppression/scrub work paid off. CONSEQUENCES for the roadmap: (1) the classifier is the largest single reason legacy wins on clean pages, promoting "a classifier that can absorb the 120k harvest" to the top of the gap program (feature-based and self-trained, so inside the project's constraints); (2) case handling has a bounded, near-zero reference to chase; (3) deletions and digit/punct priors are the cheap remainder. DELETION ATTRIBUTION (same day, 30 pages): of 21 deleted runs ≥10 chars, only ONE was suppressed — the rest were never segmented, so the excess deletions are a LAYOUT failure, not the garbage gate. Families: display/letter-spaced text, letterhead blocks, small print, signature/closing lines, and the deferred price table. |
| ✅ Adaptation pins assert shape, not case | Reject-free adaptive classification; the pin comes from document clustering, the case from line geometry | `decode/beam.py` `pin_case_geometry` | The case share of the legacy gap traced to a mechanism, not a prior: case twins share a SHAPE, so the adapt stage clusters C with c, the majority vote pins every member lowercase, and the 2.5 pin bonus outweighs the 2.0 height swing — the existing comment claiming the height prior "retains veto power" was arithmetically false. Geometry itself was healthy (measured on dev-8: lowercase size-twins sit at 0.99–1.10 of x-height, capitals at 1.33–1.60). Fix: a pin asserts shape and, for the pure SIZE twins only, confident geometry directs its case (>1.35 upper, <1.15 lower, cluster vote in between). THREE ATTEMPTS, the first two measured and rejected: (a) fully case-neutral pins traded 25 fixed errors for 33 new ones — case-blind pins had been silently repairing pixel-driven uppercase misreads; (b) geometry over ALL twins TRIPLED lower→UPPER errors, because ascender lowercase (b, d, l, t) legitimately reaches ~1.4 x-height and got pinned as capitals. Restricting to size twins: dev-8 91.1→91.3 char / 75.1→76.5 word, Capitalized→lowercase 68→41 with the opposite direction flat (37→40); broad-30 83.9→84.1 char, 62.8→63.7 word; synthetic +0.2/+0.4/0.0 word. |
| 📊 Classifier ceiling study (offline, on the stored harvest) | Held-out evaluation of condensed vs. exhaustive nearest-neighbour and parametric alternatives (Hart 1968 condensation; Duda–Hart–Stork ch. 4) | `scripts/classifier_ceiling.py` | QUESTION (2026-09-01): the shape-substitution share of the legacy gap is 40% and the 1-NN pool uses ~3.6k of ~120k harvested real glyphs — is the rest recoverable? PROTOCOL: page-disjoint split of each harvest file (contiguous last 20%, 24k test glyphs, 57 classes), synthetic exemplars recovered from prototypes.npz and added to every training set, scored on top-1 / top-3 / truth-in-top-14 (the decoder's candidate list). Caveat stated in the output: test labels are self-labels, so agreement favours the incumbent. RESULT — top-1 on held-out real glyphs: incumbent cap-80 1-NN **91.9**; cap 250 95.1; cap 1000 96.2; 1-NN over all 120k **98.7**; 5-NN vote 99.1; harvest-only 1-NN 98.8; k-means 20/class 97.0, 60/class 97.5; regularized QDA 98.7; numpy MLP 95-256-C 99.0. Top-14 recall is ≥99.8 everywhere (the decoder already sees the truth; it is top-1 that moves). Macro (per-class mean) stays 82–84 for every method: the rare-class floor is label noise and coverage, not the model. So ~7 top-1 points sit in the harvest that the incumbent cannot reach — the 40% share IS recoverable in principle. |
| ✅ Per-class k-means condensation of the exemplar pool | P.E. Hart, "The condensed nearest neighbor rule" (IEEE Trans. IT 1968) realized with k-means; the same design as Tesseract's legacy classifier, which clusters training samples into per-class prototypes (Smith, ICDAR 2007) | `recognize/condense.py`, `scripts/build_prototypes.py --condense N`, `scripts/build_kmeans_prototypes.py` | THE PIPELINE TEST OF THE CEILING. Plain 1-NN over all 144k exemplars (synthetic + whole harvest) measured WORSE in the pipeline despite +7 offline: dev-8 91.3→90.1 char / 76.5→74.7 word. Confusion report names the mechanism — COVERAGE IMBALANCE: the harvest holds no digits at all and few capitals (O: 6, S: 77 against e: 15,429), so once lowercase grows dense every real digit finds a real letter first ('1'→'l' 3→17, '9'→'e' 0→8, '9'→'g' 0→6, '5'/'3'→'S', ','→'s' 0→7). Equal per-class prototype budgets restore the balance while keeping the real glyph modes: 60/class (6,562 prototypes, 4× smaller than the live pool) — broad-30 84.1→**84.5** char / 63.7→**65.0** word, recall +0.4 / precision +0.5; synthetic 96.5/96.9/96.1→**97.5/97.7/97.1** char, word 86.4/88.3/85.9→**91.3/90.3/87.9**; dev-8 −0.4 char / +0.2 word (91.3→90.9 / 76.5→76.7). Two of three sets up, the headline and the clean-page set strongly, so adopted. Budget bracket on broad-30: 40/class 84.4/65.2, **60/class 84.5/65.0**, 120/class 84.3/64.4 — flat between 40 and 60, falling by 120 (the harvest's lowercase mass starts to crowd again). The chunked matmul matcher (`nearest.py`, \|q\|²−2q·x+\|x\|² in float64, partial sort) landed alongside: identical rankings to the broadcast form (ties at 1e-13) and 20× faster, and it is what makes a 100k pool affordable at all. NEXT: coverage, not pool size — a format-endorsed DIGIT harvest (`harvest_glyphs.py --digits`) so digits get real exemplars too. |
| 📊 Deletion attribution, corrected; reading-order share measured | Zone-ordered scoring (ISRI practice) as a control | `scripts/eval_unlv.py --zone-order`, scratch layout renders | The earlier claim that 20 of 21 long deleted runs were "never segmented" was WRONG for the largest ones. Rendering the layout of 8514 (the 159-char run "EXECUTIVE DIRECTOR Estelle Murphy, MPA …") shows every block and line segmented and the text present in our output — after the body, where the ground truth lists the sidebar first; edit-distance alignment books a reorder as a deletion plus an insertion. Others were suppressed misreads the probe's word-match missed ("Business Liaison/Consultant" → 'Doe!ness L!a!eon/ConsuMana', "Denny Weddle" → 'D e my Se d d I e', letter-spaced "D~E~D~I~C~A~T~E~D" → 'o c o 1 c a T …'). Address lines were suppressed whole: 'PAssAIc, Na 07055', 'ltasoa, IL 60143'. MEASURED reading-order share: broad-30 with words re-sorted into ground-truth zones = 85.5 char vs 84.1 — **1.4 char points** of the 11.2 gap is order convention on letters (word accuracy drops 63.7→61.8 under the re-sort because zone sorting splits lines that cross zones). Real deletion families after correction: (1) suppressed address/data lines — fixed by format endorsement (below); (2) letter-spaced display text read as letter salad — a recognition problem (small caps) plus grouping; (3) logo lines inside image zones ("Wayne State University"); (4) the deferred price table. |
| ✅ Format endorsement for numeric tokens | Forms-OCR field validation (numeric fields validated by pattern, cf. Casey & Lecolinet 1996 survey; ISRI/UNLV forms evaluations) | `decode/formats.py` `numeric_endorsed`, used by `decode/output.py` and `scripts/harvest_glyphs.py --digits` | A rigid whole-token format (ZIP, ZIP+4, phone parts, years, dates, dollar/decimal amounts, thousands separators) is the digit analogue of a lexicon hit: '0'→'O' breaks the pattern and self-filters. Two consumers. (a) Suppression exemption: a line holding a format-endorsed token is data, not a signature scribble, even when the words beside it misread ('PAssAIc, Na 07055' was 16 deletions; kept, it is 2 substitutions). (b) Digit HARVEST: the lexicon word gate admitted no digits at all (harvest histogram: zero digit exemplars), which is the coverage hole behind the all-harvest dilution. Numeric tokens carry word confidence ≈0.05 (the confidence is lexicon-driven), so the digit harvest runs with `--min-conf 0` — the format is the endorsement. Yield ≈20 digits per business letter. MEASURED (suppression exemption alone): dev-8 91.3/76.5→91.3/76.8, recall +0.2, precision +0.3; broad-30 on the km60 model 84.5/65.0→84.5/65.1; synthetic unchanged (no numerics). Small, as expected for two address lines in thirty pages; kept because it is the right rule and the harvest side is the real payload. |
| ✅ Punctuation position prior, widened; comma/apostrophe position twins | Typographic-position disambiguation of small marks (classic OCR practice; cf. the baseline-relative features in Tesseract's legacy classifier) | `decode/beam.py` `punct_small_frac`, `punct_position_prior` | PROBE (dev-8, ground-truth-aligned glyph geometry): correct '.' stand 0.19–0.36 x-heights tall with bottoms within 0.09 of the baseline; correct ',' stand **0.46–0.62** tall, bottoms 0.22–0.38 below; ':' ≈1.0 tall, 2 parts, on the baseline; ';' 1.14 tall, 2 parts, hanging 0.27; apostrophes 0.46–0.75 tall with bottoms 0.65–0.93 ABOVE the baseline. The position prior was gated at h < 0.4 x-height, so it fired for periods and hyphens only — the top punctuation confusions (','→'.', "'"→',', ':'→';') were all outside its reach. Three changes: (1) band widened to 0.85 x-height (letters are ≥1 by construction); (2) hang threshold 0.10→0.15, the midpoint between the measured populations; (3) a colon/semicolon rule for two-part marks near x-height. And a SECOND mechanism the probe exposed: for real apostrophes the classifier offers only ',' ("I,ll", "Burgess,s" — no "'" in the candidate list; synthetic apostrophes do not look like real ones) — so a comma shape floating above the baseline now injects "'" as a POSITION TWIN with the comma's score, the same move as case twins. Measured on the condensed model: dev-8 90.9/76.7→91.1/77.3; broad-30 84.5/65.1→**84.8/65.8**, recall 76.3→76.9, precision 74.0→74.6; synthetic unchanged. Residual: eroded commas (h 0.3, bottom 0.09) are geometrically periods — unfixable by position. FOLLOW-UP (same evening): "you'll" decoded as "you-ll" — hyphen and apostrophe were both 'floating' and tied on position. Measured populations separate them: hyphen bottoms 0.37–0.50 x-heights above the baseline and wider than tall; apostrophes 0.65–0.93 and taller than wide. A floating mark that is high OR tall now favours the apostrophe/quote and disfavours the hyphen, and the apostrophe twin is injected from either ',' or '-'. dev-8 91.7/77.9→91.8/78.0; broad-30 86.4/67.8→86.5/67.9. |
| ✅ Digit harvest through format endorsement; live model condensed | Self-labeled real exemplars for the classes the lexicon gate could never admit (the numeric analogue of Ho & Nagy's self-training) | `scripts/harvest_glyphs.py --digits --min-conf 0`, `data/harvest_digits.npz`, `scripts/build_prototypes.py --condense 60` (live build) | 120 non-evaluation business letters yielded 964 real digit exemplars (10 classes; 9/1/0 most common — years, ZIPs, phones). Condensed together with the letter harvest into the live model (6,600 prototypes, k-means++ seeding with best-of-3 restarts after a single random seeding measured ±0.3 char / ±1 word of pure build noise on the synthetic suite). FIRST EFFECT was a FLIP, not a fix: the dev-8 confusion report went from digits-read-as-letters ('0'→'O' 18, '1'→'l' 11, '9'→'D' 7) to letters-read-as-digits ('l'→'1' 13, 'I'→'1' 12, 'o'→'0' 10, 's'→'5' 10), total errors 786→762 but word accuracy down (dev-8 77.3→76.4, broad-30 65.8→65.0 before the ++ rebuild). The probe below found the mechanism in the decoder, not the classifier. |
| ✅ Digit mode as a deferred decision | Late commitment (this project's own design principle: emit alternatives, decide with the most evidence); lexicon-gated mode selection | `decode/beam.py` `_beam_word` / `_beam_word_mode` | PROBE (dev-8, GT-aligned): in every letter→digit error the glyph's rank-1 candidate AND its adaptation pin were the correct letter ('so'→'50', 'is'→'15', 'tool'→'t001', 'allows'→'a110w5', "I'll"→"1'11"). The digit came from DIGIT MODE: the graded trigger counts a digit at rank 2-3 as 0.6 evidence, and with real digit prototypes a digit twin sits at rank 2-3 of most l/I/o/s glyphs, so ordinary short words crossed the 0.5 line, muted the LM and took the +1.2 digit boost. A first attempt — an 'alpha mode' mirror penalty for digits in words — measured exactly zero effect (the words never reached alpha scoring; they were already in digit mode). FIX: top-1 digits decide digit mode outright; graded evidence only earns a digit-mode decode when the WORD-mode reading is not a lexicon word; a single glyph never enters digit mode on graded evidence alone (a lone 'I' vs '1'); and a standalone 'l' token becomes 'I' in the repair pass (the only one-letter English words are 'a' and 'I'; adaptation clusters I with l and pins the majority). MEASURED (condensed digit model): dev-8 91.1/76.4→**91.7/77.9**, recall 84.0→85.4, precision 82.1→83.3 — the best dev-8 of the project; confusions 791→746, 'o'→'0' 11→3, 's'→'5' 11→3, 'I'→'1' 13→4; broad-30 84.8/66.2→**85.1/67.4**, recall 77.4, precision 76.0; synthetic 97.3/89.8→97.4/90.8, 97.3/88.3→97.6/90.3, 96.6/85.9→96.8/87.4; legal-8 (other domain, same decoder) old pool 85.4/64.0 → new live model 86.1/66.7. Session total (2026-09-01 evening): broad-30 84.1/63.7→85.1/67.4. |
| ✅ Row alignment of column blocks (unruled tables) | T. Kieninger & A. Dengel, "A paper-to-HTML table converting system" (T-Recs, DAS 1998) — unruled tables recognized from word/line geometry and read out by row | `layout/rows.py`, `decode/output.py` `align_columns` | The deferred price-table page (8588) rendered with its layout showed the mechanism exactly: the table is TWO blocks (a names column, an item+price column) whose lines share baselines; output emitted column after column while the ground truth reads rows, so edit distance booked half the page as deletions and insertions. Groups of horizontally disjoint, vertically overlapping blocks with ≥3 lines each, ≥60% of one block's baselines matched within 0.5 line-heights in the other, are re-emitted as rows (baseline clustering, left-to-right within a row). GUARD against the obvious failure (two columns of running text set on one leading grid ALSO share baselines): the cell-length prior — a block whose median words-per-line exceeds 4 is text, never a column of cells; plus newspaper/magazine doc_type opts out. MEASURED: page 8588 char 50.6→**82.2**, word 34.8→46.0; broad-30 85.1→**86.2** char / 67.4→67.7 word (the whole gain is that page — a one-page catastrophe was a quarter of the legacy-gap tail); dev-8 unchanged; synthetic unchanged; magazine-6 zone-ordered identical with and without (guard holds). Remaining on the page: cell-internal spacing ('3 x 5'→'3x5'), '(LINED)' misread, small-caps 'HOF PLAQUE'. |
| ✅ Column-first XY-cut for tall gutters (single-column doc types) | Nagy & Seth's X-Y tree (1984) with a column-preference rule; reading-order conventions of the ISRI zoning | `layout/blocks.py` `column_first_frac` | The 8514 sidebar diagnosis: the widest-gap rule cut ROWS first wherever a horizontal gap spanned the page, interleaving the sidebar's sections (BOARD OF TRUSTEES, OFFICERS, MEMBERS…) with the body's paragraphs, while the ground truth reads the sidebar as one column. Rule: inside a region at least half the page tall, any full-height gutter is cut before any row gap. MEASURED: 8514 char 69.0→**74.7**, word 42.5→44.4; dev-8 unchanged; BUT magazines −2.6 char (48.6→46.0 on mag-6 unordered) — their gutters are genuine columns whose order the widest-gap rule already gets right, and forcing columns first inside sub-regions broke it. So the rule is a single-column-document PRIOR, applied with the existing letter/legal/book gutter widening only; magazines/newspapers keep the classic rule. broad-30 86.2→**86.4** char / 67.7→67.8 word (the reading-order share was measured at 1.4 points; this recovers a seventh of it on one page family). |
| ✅ Word-case coherence: body-majority rule, possessive exemption | Same orthographic convention as before (all-lower / Capitalized / ALL-CAPS), applied to the word BODY | `decode/beam.py` `_word_case_coherence` | Case-context probe after the evening's fixes (broad-30): UPPER→lower 179 (wordstart 79, capsword 64), lower→UPPER 171 (wordstart 80, **midword 67**). Midword examples: "SaVe", "yoU'll", "Sus[s]man" — a Capitalized word's legitimate initial was voting AGAINST its own lowercase body, so a 2:1 body never reached the 70% majority. The lowercase majority is now judged on the letters after the first (≥3 of them, ≥60%); and the possessive s after an apostrophe is exempt from up-flips ("TADC's" had been becoming "TADC'S" inside a caps word). dev-8 91.8/78.0→91.8/78.1; broad-30 86.5/67.9→86.5/68.0; synthetic unchanged. Small, kept as the correct rule. |
| 📊 Harvest round 3 under the improved pipeline (capitals coverage) | Self-training iteration (Ho & Nagy 2000) — the flywheel's third turn, now consumed by condensation instead of a capped 1-NN pool | `data/harvest_en_r3.npz` (100 non-eval letters), `scripts/build_prototypes.py --harvest` for variants | Motivation: the classifier-ceiling work moved the consumption mechanism, and the pin-case fix changed the labels (round 2 had labeled most capitals lowercase: S 77, C 47, O 6). Round 3 yielded 61,547 exemplars from 100 pages with capitals 1,747→2,112 (S 207, C 126, P 92). Two variants measured against the live model under identical code: (a) r3 REPLACING round 2 — dev-8 91.8/78.1→92.3/79.5, but synthetic sev2 word 87.4→85.0 and broad-30 word 68.0→67.7; (b) r3 ADDED to round 2 — dev-8 92.1/78.6, synthetic 97.6/91.3 · 97.8/91.3 · 96.6/87.4 (all ≥ live), broad-30 86.5/68.0 with recall 77.4→**78.1** and precision unchanged. Adopted (b): the union is the live model (`--condense 60` over harvest_en, harvest_en_r3, harvest_legal, harvest_digits). Verdict on the flywheel stands — headline char is flat on the third turn — but coverage still buys recall and the clean-page sets, which is what the condensation mechanism was built to let through. |
| ✅ Thin-wide veto for image zones | Text/graphics discrimination by component aspect (Fletcher & Kasturi 1988 use elongation to separate rules from text; the same cue separates an underlined line from art) | `layout/imagezones.py` `max_aspect` | Zone crops from the deletion families showed page 8571's zones 1 and 2 were UNDERLINED TEXT LINES ("Gifts are tax deductible…", "your receipt for income tax purposes."): the underline welds every glyph into one 880×32 px component, which passes the giant-well-filled rule and is erased as art. A giant component longer than 6× its thickness is a rule or an underlined line, never art — skipped, left to rulings and text. MEASURED: dev-8 92.1/78.6→**92.5/79.5**, recall 86.2→87.1; broad-30 86.5/68.0→86.5/**68.3**; magazine-6 zone-ordered 54.9→55.4 char; synthetic unchanged. (Page 8571's own char accuracy does not move because the recovered lines sit out of ground-truth order and edit distance books them as insertions regardless of content — the recall and the other sets carry the evidence.) |
| 🔬 Stroke-preserving rule removal — NEGATIVE, opt-in | Line removal with intersection recovery (Yu & Jain, "A generic system for form dropout", PAMI 1996) | `layout/rulings.py` `preserve_strokes` (default False) | After the veto, the underlined line decoded as "nift- Ara tax M.d.j.fihla": the rule band (rule dilated by 2) erased the glyph bottoms resting on the underline. Restoring band pixels that continue text ink within a few rows above/below made the line read "Gifts arm tax Hedlletible as provided by law. This is" — and measured dev-8 +0.2 word, broad-30 −0.2 word, **legal-8 86.3→85.8 char / 66.2→65.6 word**: pleading pages carry long rules beside line-numbered margins, and the restored stubs decode as junk. Kept as an opt-in for underlined business text; default off. The right fix is probably to restore only where ink exists on BOTH sides (true crossings) plus glyph bottoms only when the run above is glyph-sized — not done. |
| ✅ Self-trained MLP as a second opinion on the candidate list | Multilayer perceptron over hand-designed features (Rumelhart–Hinton–Williams 1986; LeCun et al.'s early zip-code work used the same shape of model on extracted features) — self-trained on our own exemplars, inside the no-pre-trained-nets constraint; combined with the prototype matcher by additive re-costing (classifier combination, Kittler et al. 1998) | `recognize/mlp.py`, `scripts/train_mlp.py`, `recognize/stage.py` `mlp_path` / `mlp_weight` / `mlp_inject` | The ceiling study's best offline method (99.0 top-1 vs 97.5 for condensation) brought into the pipeline WITHOUT replacing the prototype distances: cost' = distance + w·(nll_mlp(c) − min nll over the list), so the MLP's favourite among the candidates keeps its prototype distance (the scale graphic detection and adaptation calibrate against is untouched) and every other candidate pays its disagreement in nats; the MLP's top-3 classes are inserted when the prototypes missed them. One hidden layer of 256, numpy Adam, cosine schedule, inverse-sqrt class weights; trained in **12 seconds** on 197k exemplars (synthetic renders + all harvests, `build_prototypes.py --cap 1e9 --inlier 100` → `train_mlp.py`), held-out 5% top-1 97.7 (self-labels). MEASURED (w=1): synthetic 97.6/91.3 · 97.8/91.3 · 96.6/87.4 → **98.2/93.7 · 98.6/95.1 · 97.4/90.8**; dev-8 92.5/79.5→92.4/80.2, precision 84.2→84.9; broad-30 86.5/68.3→**86.8/68.3**, recall 78.2→78.9, precision 76.1→76.8; legal-8 86.3/66.2→86.4/66.3. WEIGHT SWEEP: w=0.5 dev-8 92.4/80.4; w=1 as above; **w=2** broad-30 **87.0/68.6**, dev-8 92.7/80.8, legal-8 86.5/66.5, synthetic 98.2/93.7 · 98.4/94.7 · 97.4/90.8; w=4 broad-30 86.9/68.5, dev-8 92.7/81.1, legal 86.6/66.7, synthetic sev0 slips to 98.1/93.2 while sev2 rises to 97.7/91.3. Default w=2 (the headline optimum). Adopted (`data/mlp.npz`, gitignored like the other models; README lists the build). The 'overnight bigger network' item is now a 12-second job; what is left overnight is data, not training time. Day total (2026-09-01): broad-30 77.3/52.4 → **87.0/68.6**; legacy reference 95.3/90.5. |
| 📊 Legacy-gap decomposition, end of day (2026-09-01) | Same paired error-type analysis as the morning's | `scripts/compare_legacy_errors.py` (run with `TESSDATA_PREFIX` pointing at a legacy-capable `eng.traineddata`; Homebrew's ships LSTM-only) | All 30 pages, 44,668 truth chars: ours **87.0** vs legacy 95.3; excess errors 3,861 (morning, 27 clean pages: ~3,470 excess at 86.8 vs 95.5 — the catastrophic tail is now inside the count and the total still fell). Shares of the remaining gap: shape substitutions **43%** (2,036 vs 368 — still 5.5× legacy), character deletions 25% (1,411 vs 459), case 9% (350 vs 14), punctuation 7% (490 vs 227), digits 6% (351 vs 136), spaces 8%, insertions 4%. What moved today: digits +373→+215, punctuation +337→+263, case +391→+336 (on a harder page set), deletions per clean page down (the table and underline families). What did NOT move: shape substitutions (+1,397→+1,668 with the three hard pages included), even though the classifier's offline top-1 on real glyphs went 91.9→~99. CONCLUSION: the residual shape errors are not single-glyph classification errors — they sit where glyphs are broken, merged or eroded on degraded pages, i.e. in segmentation and in features that are not robust to breaks. Next levers: (1) a TRUTH-labeled real glyph set from GT-aligned pages (the harvest is self-labeled, so it cannot show the classifier its own systematic mistakes); (2) merge/split lattice quality on degraded pages; (3) reading order (1.4 points) and display-type wordmarks for the deletion share. |
| 📊 Shape-residual labeling (GT-aligned glyph probe) | Error taxonomy by glyph condition, in the spirit of the ISRI reports' failure analyses | scratch `shape_probe.py` (crop grids in the session scratchpad) | Every letter→letter substitution on aligned lines classified by the glyph that reached the classifier. dev-8 (clean): 152 errors — whole 59, broken 8, merged 8, bloated 7, eroded 1, unaligned 69. broad-30: whole 463, broken 47, bloated 22, merged 16, eroded 14, **unaligned 1,299** — 'unaligned' means the line used split/merge variants or dash scrubs, so 70% of degraded-page shape errors sit on lines where segmentation was already active. CROP GRIDS overturn the 'degraded glyph' story for the aligned part: the whole-glyph errors are mostly LEGIBLE — heavy sans capitals from letterhead faces (F→r, Y→o, N→c, A→s, P→F, D→G: a bold grotesque not in our 23-face stock), 'fi'/'ffi'/'fl' LIGATURES (single components with no class: 'f'→'i', 'f'→'h'), italic serif merges ('rve', 'rvi'), and filled/eroded bowls in body text (e→o 7, a→e 5, e→a 5, c→s 5, s→e 4). Consequences: (1) the outline channel below targets the bowl family and the unseen-bold family; (2) ligature classes are a missing piece with a clear signature; (3) the 'unaligned' 70% says the segmentation lattice, not the classifier, is the biggest single lever left on degraded pages — the same conclusion the decomposition reached from the other side. |
| ✅ Outline-segment features, many-to-one matched (Tesseract §5 re-derived) | R. Smith, "An Overview of the Tesseract OCR Engine", ICDAR 2007, §5.1–5.2 and §6; source read: `src/classify/intfx.cpp` (feature extraction: normalization by centroid and second moments to a 256-unit space, features as fixed-length outline pieces of 64/5 units with position and direction), `intmatcher.cpp` (evidence per feature–proto pair from a Gaussian-like table of point-to-line distance and angle difference; per-config feature evidence = best proto; per-proto evidence = best L matches for a proto of length L; `NormalizeSums` divides by features + proto lengths), `intproto.h` (protos as (A,B,C,angle) lines with configs). PDF fetched to `docs/papers/refs/` (not committed). | `recognize/outline.py`, `scripts/build_outline_protos.py`, `recognize/stage.py` `outline_path` / `outline_weight` / `outline_top`; test `tests/test_outline.py` reproduces Smith's Fig. 6 (a cut 'h' still rates 'h' above 'b', 'n', 'k') | Readable numpy re-derivation: skimage contours → moment normalization → fixed-length oriented pieces (features) and polygon-approximated segments (prototypes, one configuration per clean font render, 3,080 configurations over 110 classes from 28 fonts — clean renders only, as Tesseract trained, §5.3). Evidence e = exp(−(d/σd)²−(Δθ/σθ)²) with d the distance to the SEGMENT (Tesseract bounds its infinite-line distance with the proto pruner's position buckets); ratings vectorized over all configurations of a class with `reduceat`. σ study on the cut-'h' case: tight widths (10, 0.45 rad) rated everything ~0.2 and let 'b' win; (35, 0.7) rates the cut 'h' 0.75 as 'h' vs 0.71 as 'b', a clean cross-font 'h' 0.81 vs 0.73. Used as a THIRD opinion in the same additive form as the MLP: cost' = cost + w·(oc − min oc over the top candidates); scale measured on a real page — prototype candidate gaps ~20 units vs outline gaps ~0.07, so w=100 ≈ half a vote. On real glyphs its top-1 agrees with the prototype top-1 79% of the time. MEASURED: synthetic 98.2/93.7 · 98.4/94.7 · 97.4/90.8 → **98.3/94.2 · 98.6/95.6 · 97.5/91.3** (w=100; w=200 loses sev2); dev-8 92.7/80.8 → 92.8/80.5 (w=100), 92.5/79.7 (w=200). Cost ~48 ms/glyph at 14 candidates, capped to the top 6. WEIGHT SWEEP on the pipeline: w=50 dev-8 **92.8/81.2** (recall 88.0, precision 85.3), broad-30 **87.1/68.7**, recall 78.9, precision 77.1 — every column up; w=100 broad-30 87.1/68.6, dev-8 92.8/80.5; w=200 dev-8 92.5/79.7. Synthetic at w=50: 98.3/94.2 · 98.5/95.1 · 97.4/90.8. Adopted as default at w=50 (`data/outline_protos.npz`, ~2 s per page at six candidates). Day-2 scoreboard: broad-30 87.1/68.7 against legacy 95.3/90.5. |
| 🔬 Ligature classes (fi, fl, ff, ffi) — NEGATIVE for now | Ligature modelling as in Tesseract's unicharset (which carries fi/fl classes and expands them) | `factory/stock.py` `LIGATURES` (defined, not in CHARSET), `factory/synth.py` `glyph_available`, NFKC expansion in `decode/beam.py` `_core` and `decode/output.py` | Motivated by the shape-residual crops (single-component 'fi'/'ffi' decoding as 'i'/'h' with the 'f' deleted). Built as classes from the 28-face stock (fonts lacking the glyph render a fallback box, detected by comparing the ligature's ink width with its NFKC expansion; fi/fl available in ~all faces, ff/ffi in about half): 114 classes, variant prototypes + MLP. MEASURED on dev-8: 20 glyphs decoded as ligatures, of which THREE were ligatures — the rest signature scribbles, a blackletter initial, merged fragments ('Dese', '00'): the widest classes became a junk magnet; dev-8 92.7/80.8→92.5/80.3, synthetic sev0 98.2/93.7→98.0/92.7. Not adopted; the expansion plumbing stays so a gated version (ligature admitted only when the expanded word is lexicon-endorsed, or only from the outline channel's rating) can be tried cheaply. |
| 🔬 Adaptation-pin strength sweep — NEGATIVE (pins stay as they are) | Adaptive classifier confidence gating (cf. Tesseract's adaptive classifier and its 'good word' criteria, Smith 2007 §7) | `adapt/cluster_refit.py` `member_gate` (new, default off), `min_purity`, `decode/beam.py` `pin_bonus` | A truth-labeled probe on three non-evaluation pages (3,600 glyphs, 71 pipeline errors) found 73% of the residual errors carried an adaptation pin to the wrong class, while the recognizer's raw top-14 held the truth for 73% of them. Three ways of trusting pins less were measured on dev-8 (baseline 92.8/81.2): pin only members whose OWN candidate list contains the voted class → 92.8/81.1 (flat); pin bonus 2.5→1.5 → 92.5/80.5; cluster purity 0.7→0.85 → 92.6/80.8. Pins are present in most residual errors because pinning is what repairs the majority; every way of weakening them loses more than it saves. The residual truth sits at ranks 7–14 of the candidate list, so the lever is a better ORDERING of that list, not a weaker pin. |
| 🔬 One-base dot association — NEGATIVE; the 'i' errors are touching pairs | Diacritic/base association by overlap (Smith 2007 §3.1) | `glyph/components.py` `_group_overlapping` (reverted to the single overlap rule) | The provenance-based shape probe on broad-30 (1,885 aligned shape errors: 73% whole single components, 21% broken, 2% merged-by-aspect) put truth 'i' at the top of the confusion list — read as d, A, s, n, e, N over 120 times — with accented letters among the candidates, which suggested the i-dot welding its stem to the next letter through the union-find. Restricting small marks to attach to ONE base (most overlap) was measured three ways: 'any overlap' glued punctuation under letter overhangs (dev-8 92.8/81.2→92.3/79.0); with the half-width requirement, separating small marks from each other broke colons, quotes and broken small letters (same numbers); with small–small unions restored, dev-8 92.8/80.8 and **broad-30 87.1/68.7→86.9/68.0**. Reverted. The crop grid then showed the true mechanism: the 'i' errors are TOUCHING PAIRS in a bold serif face — 'ti', 'li', 'fi', 'ri', 'is', 'in', 'wi' as single connected components that the split lattice never cut (their width is only ~1.2–1.5× the line's median, under the suspect threshold), so a two-letter blob decodes as one letter. This is the chopper (Smith §4.1), not association; see the split-threshold work that follows. |
| ✅ Touching-pair split suspects: threshold 1.5→1.3 | Chopping of joined characters (Smith 2007 §4.1: chops proposed generously, undone when confidence does not improve) | `glyph/components.py` `split_width_factor` | The provenance probe's top confusion was a bold serif face's touching pairs ('ti', 'li', 'fi', 'ri', 'is', 'in', 'wi') read as one letter: at 1.2–1.5× the line's median width they never became split suspects. A suspect only ADDS a hypothesis — the decoder still chooses, with the split bonus — so the threshold can be generous. Sweep: dev-8 1.5 → 92.8/81.2, **1.3 → 92.8/81.3** (recall 88.3, precision 85.5), 1.15 → 92.7/80.9; broad-30 1.5 → 87.1/68.7, **1.3 → 87.3/69.3**, recall 78.9→79.7, precision 77.1→77.8. A second, targeted proposal (`split_under_dot`): a dot-sized part over one side of a body wider than 1.1× median marks an 'i' or 'j' touching its neighbour, and the cut is looked for at the dot's edge — alone broad-30 87.4/69.3; together with the threshold **87.4/69.5**, recall 80.0, precision 78.1 (dev-8 92.8/81.1–81.3, synthetic unchanged). Both adopted. |
| ✅ Chopper: ranked cut options, three-piece splits, fixed-pitch guard | Chopping joined characters with several candidate chops tried in priority order (Smith 2007 §4.1: up to three chop pairs, undone when confidence does not improve; our cuts are ink-profile minima rather than concave outline vertices) | `glyph/components.py` `_cut_candidates`, `split_cuts`, `split_triple`; `recognize/stage.py` scores every piece of every option; `decode/beam.py` `_decode_word` enumerates one option per suspect, best-first, under `max_split_variants` | Before: one cut per suspect at the global ink minimum, two pieces. In a touching pair the kiss is often the second-lowest column (the lowest sits inside a bowl or under a serif), and three touching letters ('rti') cannot be read at all. Now up to three local minima (at least a piece apart) each give a two-piece option, and the best cut's still-wide side gets a second cut for a three-piece option; the split bonus counts the extra characters actually produced. MEASURED (all with threshold 1.3): three cuts + triples → dev-8 92.9/81.2, broad-30 87.2/69.8 (recall +0.7), legal-8 85.9/65.5; ablation: one cut + triples → **broad-30 87.5/69.9**, recall 80.6, precision 78.5, **dev-8 93.0/81.7**, recall 88.8, precision 85.9 (the best dev-8 and broad-30 yet); three cuts without triples → broad-30 87.2/69.4. So the THREE-PIECE option carries the gain and extra cut positions add nothing; defaults `split_cuts=1`, `split_triple=True`. The legal-8 loss (86.5→85.9) traced NOT to the chopper but to the earlier 1.5→1.3 suspect threshold: typewriter pages are fixed-pitch, every wide letter is 1.3× the median, and legal had not been re-measured after that change. Fixed-pitch detection (page median of per-line component width CV: legal 0.17 vs letters 0.29, threshold 0.22 — Smith §3.3 separates fixed-pitch text the same way, by pitch) keeps the 1.5 threshold on such pages. On the legal EVALUATION pages, though, the width statistic read 0.22–0.47 and fired on two of eight — raw component widths mix punctuation and broken pieces — so the `doc_type` hint (legal filings are typewriter pages) is the surer trigger, and either may fire. Legal-8 isolation: dot-guided cut off → 85.8/65.1 (no change), triples off → 85.9/65.0 (no change), fixed-pitch threshold via doc_type → **86.1/65.5**. dev-8 93.0/81.7 and broad-30 87.5/69.8 unchanged by the guard. The last 0.4 of legal's drift from 86.5 predates the chopper: the outline channel, adopted on letter and synthetic evidence, was never measured on legal until now — without it legal-8 reads 86.3/66.5, with it 86.1/65.5. A 0.2 char / 1.0 word domain trade for +0.1/+0.4 on dev-8 and the synthetic gains; kept, recorded, and a candidate for a doc_type-aware weight. |
| ✅ Per-blob confidence-driven chopping — neutral at first, adopted after the ensemble sharpened | Smith 2007 §4.1: "while the result from a word is unsatisfactory, Tesseract attempts to improve the result by chopping the blob with worst confidence"; "any chop that fails to improve the confidence of the result is undone" | `recognize/stage.py` `_chop_on_confidence` (`chop_on_confidence`, `chop_distance_factor`, `chop_min_width_frac`) | Truth-set analysis of the 41% of errors whose truth lies OUTSIDE the candidate list: whole-read glyphs of truth 'i' (293), 't' (99), 'l' (85), 'r' (62), 'f' (50) decoded as d/a/n/h/u/m — two narrow letters touching are no wider than an 'm', so no width threshold can flag them (the 1.3 rule already caught the wide pairs). Second pass in the recognizer: a group with no split hypothesis whose top-1 distance exceeds 1.3× the page median and whose width is at least 0.9× the line median gets one cut at the ink minimum; its pieces are scored by every channel and the decoder chooses. UNGATED, the pass cut clean glyphs apart on the synthetic suite (sev0 char 98.8→98.1); with Smith's second clause — the hypothesis survives only if BOTH pieces match better than the whole did — synthetic is fully restored. MEASURED (gated): dev-8 93.0/81.7→92.9/81.6, broad-30 87.5/69.9→87.6/69.8 (recall +0.3), legal-8 86.1/65.5→85.9/64.8; ungated far worse everywhere (broad-30 86.6/68.5). Why it cannot work as a per-blob rule: a touching 'li' matches 'u' or 'h' WELL, so its distance never exceeds the page median — the blobs that need chopping are exactly the confident ones. Smith's trigger is at the WORD level ("while the result from a word is unsatisfactory"), which needs the lexicon verdict; that version is the chop stage that follows. Kept as an opt-in. RE-MEASURED 2026-09-04, after font-stock widening and the outline channel: modern 66.2→66.8 word (+0.7 recall/precision; 'Depatment:' → 'Department:' on every payslip — the touching 'rt' in Helvetica is a 3-px bridge the width rule cannot see), broad-30 88.3/72.7→88.5/73.1, legal-8 86.9/68.5→87.0/68.8, dev-8 94.1/84.8→93.9/84.7. Three sets up, one −0.1: ON by default. Lesson: a chopper is only as good as the distances that trigger it — the same rule that was inert against the old prototypes trips correctly once the channels separate 'rt' from 'd'. |
| 🔬 Word-level chop stage (Tesseract's trigger, faithfully) — INERT, opt-in | Smith 2007 §4.1: chop the worst blob while the WORD is unsatisfactory; undo chops that do not improve confidence | `recognize/chop.py` (slot `chop`, impl `unendorsed`; not in the default pipeline), `recognize/stage.py` `score_crops` | Between the two decode passes: every word the lexicon did not endorse, or endorsed below decode confidence 0.15 (the lexicon pass endorses near-misses — on one page 'mIl', 'Wea', 'SCOtch', 'Spar' all under 0.12 while right words sit at p50 0.28), has its worst-matched whole-read groups cut at the ink minimum, the pieces scored through every channel, and the hypothesis kept only if both pieces beat the whole. RESULT: dev-8, broad-30 and legal-8 IDENTICAL to the reference to the decimal (broad-30 87.5/69.9). Counts across dev-8 with the gate OFF: 296 unsatisfactory words, 397 hypotheses, **9 chosen by the decoder**. The trigger is now right and the cut position is not the blocker: the PIECES are — a cut piece carries a cut edge the whole-glyph prototypes never saw, its candidate list is wrong, and the split reading loses the beam to the whole. Consistent with the truth set (cut pieces err 13.9% vs 2.7%). The lever is piece-aware scoring: outline evidence over the un-cut sides only, or prototypes rendered with cut edges. Stage kept as an opt-in so that work has its harness. |
| 🔬 Piece-aware scoring — NEGATIVE, opt-in: cut-edge masking and all-class outline rating for chopped pieces | Partial-shape matching — the outline features' original purpose (Smith §5.1: unmatched features and prototypes cost only their own share) applied to a deliberately incomplete glyph | `recognize/outline.py` `outline_features(cut_edges=…)`, `OutlineMatcher.costs_all`; `recognize/stage.py` `_piece_outline` (`piece_outline`, `piece_inject`) | A chopped piece has a straight cut edge no prototype has, and its whole-glyph 95-vector candidates are unreliable (cut pieces err 13.9%; the word-level chop stage saw 9 of 397 hypotheses accepted). Now every split piece drops the outline features within two pixels of its cut side(s) and is rated by the outline channel against ALL classes; the outline's top three join its candidate list and the additive re-cost applies. Cost ~350 ms per piece (pieces only). Synthetic unchanged. MEASURED: dev-8 93.0/81.7→92.6/80.7, broad-30 87.5/69.9→87.1/69.2, legal-8 86.1/65.5→86.3/65.0. Rating a small piece against all 110 classes produces confident wrong classes (a fragment of 'm' rates as 'n' or 'r' better than anything), and injecting them at the outline weight wrecks pieces that the prototypes had right. Off by default. The chopper series' honest sum: three-piece splits (+0.4 word broad-30) are the only positive; per-blob trigger, word-level trigger and piece-aware scoring all measured inert or negative. Cut pieces remain the weakest population, and the next attempt should change what a piece IS (cut at concave outline vertices so the edge falls in a natural neck, or prototypes rendered from cut synthetic pairs) rather than how a piece is scored. |
| 🔬 Concave-vertex cut placement — NEGATIVE on real scans, opt-in | Smith 2007 §4.1: chop points are concave vertices of the polygonal outline approximation, paired with a concave vertex opposite | `glyph/components.py` `_concave_cuts` (`cut_method`, `cut_tol`), ink minimum as fallback | The outer outline (largest enclosed area) is polygon-approximated; a vertex is concave when its turn opposes the outline's winding; pairs of concave vertices one above the other within a quarter of the blob height mark a NECK, scored by neck length plus horizontal offset, and the cut goes through the neck's midpoint. On real width-flagged suspects (6 pages, 375 blobs) a concave pair exists for 83%, and the chosen column differs from the ink minimum by 7 px median (17 px at p90) — the two rules genuinely disagree. Synthetic: tolerance 1.5 px hurt the degraded severities (sev1 word 96.6→94.7: noise vertices form spurious pairs); tolerance 3.5 recovers it (98.9/95.6 · 99.1/96.6 · 98.1/91.7) but does not beat the ink minimum (98.8/95.6 · 99.3/96.6 · 98.3/92.2). REAL PAGES, both tolerances: dev-8 93.0/81.7→91.6/78.7 (1.5) and 91.9/79.3 (3.5); **broad-30 87.5/69.9→86.0/66.2 and 86.0/66.4**; legal-8 86.1/65.5→84.4/62.9 and 84.9/63.0. Decisively worse. Smith's chop points come from outlines of clean 300 dpi originals traced by Tesseract's own edge tracker; on our bitonal photocopy scans the polygonal outline is ragged and a 'neck' between two concave vertices is far more often a pair of noise pits than the kiss between letters — the ink-minimum column, which integrates the whole column, is the robust statistic here. Default back to "ink"; the rule stays as `cut_method="concave"` for cleaner sources. This closes the chopper series: three-piece splits positive; per-blob and word-level triggers inert; piece-aware scoring and concave placement negative. The remaining piece-quality idea is prototypes rendered from cut synthetic pairs, so cut pieces have neighbours; everything else on the chopper has now been measured. |
| 📊 Modern test set: first numbers, and a rasterization lesson | Test-set construction from born-digital documents; print simulation (dot gain) as in document-degradation modelling (Baird 1992; Kanungo et al. 2000 model the scanner, not the printer — the printer mattered here) | `scripts/make_modern_set.py`, `data/modern/` | FIRST RUN (35 govinfo PDF pages rasterized straight to 300 dpi + 24 templated invoices/payslips/letters in Helvetica Neue, Avenir, Arial Narrow, Helvetica; clean): ours 57.5 char / 28.7 word / 55.6 recall / 51.7 precision vs legacy Tesseract 67.0 / 61.9 / 85.7 / 92.9. Modern letters read at 95–97 char in all four faces — the modern-font fear is not borne out on sans letters. The congressional BILLS were catastrophic (recall 12–22%): their face (a Century-like high-contrast serif) has hairlines thinner than one 300-dpi pixel, which poppler anti-aliases to light grey or nothing, so 'and for other purposes.' (20 glyphs) binarizes to 78 components at Sauvola k=0.2, 110 at a plain 0.5 threshold, 114 unantialiased — every letter shredded before any stage of ours ran. Not a split-lattice fault (all split toggles ±1 point), not a stroke-normalization fault (top-1 garbage with it off too), not fixable by the threshold alone (k 0.05–0.1: 30–45 char). Real print does not do this: toner dot gain spreads ink about a 600-dpi pixel. PRINT MODEL adopted for the PDF pages: render at 600 dpi, one-pixel grey minimum filter, area-downsample to 300 → 19 components for those 20 glyphs, median stroke 2 px, like a real scan of the page. The bill page then reads 18.2→68.4 char, recall 22.5→54.4, body text intact; residue is letter-spaced headings and marginalia. TWO LESSONS: (1) a born-digital raster is not a scan — a test set needs a printer model as much as a scanner model; (2) legacy Tesseract read the SHREDDED pages at 86% recall, so fragment association (Smith §4.2) is a real gap of ours regardless of the set's fidelity — the merge lattice joins adjacent pairs only. Real-page numbers on the rebuilt set follow. |
| ✅ Fragment associator: merge runs and k-best segmentation search | Smith 2007 §4.2: the associator searches the graph of combinations of maximally chopped blobs (A*, best-first); here a k-best dynamic program over the piece sequence | `glyph/components.py` `merge_max_pieces` (runs of 2–3 pieces as one hypothesis), `recognize/stage.py` (scores every run), `decode/beam.py` `_merge_paths` | Before: one hypothesis per piece ('this plus its right neighbour'), and the decoder tried SUBSETS of those under a cap of eight — a word shredded into ten fragments could never be reassembled, and the modern bills (hairline serif) and UNLV's broken-hairline page are exactly that. Now every run of 2..3 consecutive pieces whose union is one character wide (≤1.05× median height), whose gaps are small and which contains a sub-glyph piece is scored; the decoder finds the k best segmentations of the word into singles and runs by summed best-candidate cost, keeps the all-singles path first (ranking is for truncation, the beam decides), and combines them with the split variants as before. MEASURED: broad-30 87.5/69.9→**87.7/70.1**, recall 80.6→80.9, precision 78.5→78.7; dev-8 93.0/81.7 unchanged; legal-8 86.1/65.5→86.3/65.7; synthetic unchanged. Adopted. On the SHREDDED bill render (35 pieces for 20 glyphs) it does not help (recall 22% either way) although twelve merge hypotheses exist on the test line: a letter in three pieces merged two at a time still scores badly against whole-glyph prototypes, and fragments that overlap horizontally (negative gaps) were never grouped — the same piece-scoring wall the chopper series hit, now from the merge side. Legacy reads that render at 86% recall, so the wall is real; the print model makes it rare in the modern set. |
| ✅ Outline-channel confidence gate (2.4x faster, accuracy up on the headline) | Cascade evaluation: spend the expensive opinion only where the cheap one is unsure (the same gate the GED rerank already used) | `recognize/stage.py` `outline_margin` | The outline channel costs ~30 ms per glyph and dominated runtime: a three-column Federal Register page took ~5 minutes, and a 59-page set two hours per severity. But the prototype channel is already sure for most glyphs: on a real page, 80% have (d2-d1)/d1 > 0.3. Rating only the rest measured, on real pages, NOT worse but slightly better: broad-30 87.7/70.1 → **87.7/70.4** (recall 80.9→81.0, precision 78.7→78.9), legal-8 86.3/65.7 → **86.5/66.2**; dev-8 93.0/81.7 → 92.9/81.3 and synthetic sev0/sev1 word −0.5 (the tuning set and the clean renders lose a little). Margin 0.6 (76% skipped) measured identical to 0.3 on all three real sets, so the faster gate wins. Whole-suite timing: synthetic evaluation 94 s → 40 s. Default 0.3. Plausible mechanism for the gain: on confident glyphs the outline opinion could only add noise, and its cost re-ranking occasionally displaced a correct top-1. |
| 🔬 Self-trained glyph CNN as a fourth opinion — NEGATIVE in the pipeline, off by default | Convolutional classification of glyph images (LeCun et al. 1989 on hand-designed inputs; here trained from our own renders and our own truth-labeled scans, ~30k parameters of numpy, no framework) | `recognize/cnn.py`, `scripts/train_cnn.py`, `recognize/stage.py` `cnn_path` / `cnn_weight` / `cnn_inject` | Motivation: convolution is local, so a break costs a few filter responses rather than every feature — the property the outline channel has without its fragility on ragged outlines. Trained in 11 min on 151k crops (24.6k renders through the degradation stack + 134.6k truth-labeled real crops); **95.4% top-1 on a PAGE-DISJOINT holdout** (a random 5% split flatters it: crops from the same page are near-duplicates). On the glyphs the pipeline reads right it agrees 99.1%; on the glyphs it reads wrong it was 62% right — but those crops were in its training set, so that number is memorization-inflated. IN THE PIPELINE: with class injection (its top-3 added to the candidate list, as the MLP does) it was catastrophic at EVERY weight — dev-8 92.9/81.3 → 86.7/71.6 at w=2, 88.4/73.0 at w=0.1 — because a CNN reading an image proposes classes the prototype channel rejected for good reasons (junk crops, split pieces), and they enter near the best cost; the MLP's injections are safe only because it reads the same 95 features and proposes the same neighbourhood. As a pure re-coster (`cnn_inject=0`): dev-8 93.0/81.6 (+0.1/+0.3), synthetic flat, but **broad-30 87.7/70.4 → 87.6/70.0 and legal-8 86.5/66.2 → 86.3/65.2**. Not adopted. THE LESSON IS ABOUT THE ENSEMBLE, NOT THE MODEL: a fourth opinion on the same crop cannot help when the residual errors are decided elsewhere — the truth set says 68% of them carry a wrong adaptation pin, 52% sit in a lexicon-endorsed word, and the truth is outside the candidate list for 41%, i.e. the glyph was mis-segmented before any classifier saw it. Kept selectable for the day the segmentation frontier moves. |
| 📊 Modern set, measured: we beat legacy on characters, lose on recall | Paired evaluation on today's documents (`data/modern`, print model per the entry above) | `scripts/eval_unlv.py data/modern/sev0`, `scripts/eval_tesseract.py` (now prints per page, so both engines parse alike) | 59 clean pages: ours **73.8 char** vs legacy 67.4, but word 47.6 vs 62.2, recall 70.1 vs 85.7, precision 69.4 vs 93.1. BY FAMILY (ours / legacy, char and recall): modern LETTERS 95.7/87.8 vs 99.7/98.5 — modern sans is not the problem; congressional BILLS 73.0/62.2 vs 76.2/69.2; FEDERAL REGISTER 63.8/79.5 vs 67.7/94.3; INVOICES **78.5**/63.2 vs 41.9/95.7; PAYSLIPS **64.8**/68.0 vs 34.7/97.8. The inversion on invoices and payslips is the whole story of this set: legacy reads nearly every word (96–98% recall) and emits them in an order that destroys edit distance, while we order sparse column layouts well and MISS a third of the words. So on business forms our reading order beats legacy's and our recall is far behind. |
| ✅ Short numeric cells are data; margin line numbers are apparatus | Table-cell vs. page-apparatus discrimination (line-number columns are a standard special case in legal-document OCR) | `decode/output.py` `keep_short_numeric`, `_line_number_column`, `line_number_doc_types` | Diagnosing the invoice recall gap: every quantity cell was being deleted. A one-character line ('9') trivially repeats 100% of itself, so the garbage rule's shape test (one character supplying ≥40% of the line) fires on every short cell. FIRST FIX, a blanket 4-character floor on that test: modern recall +1.1 but −0.2 char on all three scan sets (short junk on photocopies is real: 'u5', 'x', 'c'). SECOND, narrower: exempt only lines of ≤3 characters that are ALL DIGITS — modern recall 70.1→71.4, broad-30 unchanged at 87.7/70.4, but legal-8 86.5→84.6 char, because a pleading numbers every line down the margin and UNLV's ground truth omits those numerals. THIRD: detect the numbering column structurally (≥6 single-token numeric lines in one 40 px x band, ≥70% ascending) and suppress it. That fixed legal (86.7/66.7, its best) but cost the modern set 2.6 recall — because a congressional bill's line numbers ARE in its ground truth. The conflict is a consumer convention, not a fact about documents, so it is a parameter: `line_number_doc_types="legal"`. FINAL, all three at their best: modern 74.2 char / **71.4 recall**, legal-8 **86.7/66.7**, broad-30 87.7/70.4, dev-8 92.8/81.3. |
| ✅ Font-stock widening (adopted): six modern sans faces, all channels rebuilt | Re-running the widening experiment under the CURRENT consumers (per-class condensation + MLP + outline), as the roadmap predicted | `scripts/build_prototypes.py --add-fonts` | Every earlier widening failed under the capped 1-NN pool (a pure append of six faces cost letters −1.3 char). Repeated now with six modern sans faces that the modern set does NOT use (Trebuchet MS ×3, Geneva, Avenir Next ×2 — Helvetica Neue, Avenir, Helvetica and Arial Narrow stay held out): modern 73.8→**75.5** char, recall 70.1→**72.8**; dev-8 92.9→**93.4** char, recall +0.6; at 90 prototypes per class instead of 60, modern **75.8 char / 73.9 recall / 71.3 precision** (its best) — the first widening in the project's history to help. BUT broad-30 word −0.6 / precision −0.9 and **legal-8 86.5→85.1 char (−3.4 word)**, only partly recovered by the larger budget (85.4/64.2). Font ROUTING is not the cause: legal pages still vote mono with higher confidence and their distances IMPROVE (10.25→8.54). The cost is elsewhere — a denser pool compresses the distance scale that the graphic gate, the softmax spread and the adapt calibration are tuned against. THE TRADE WAS AN ARTEFACT OF A MISMATCHED ENSEMBLE. Those measurements swapped the prototype file while leaving the MLP and the outline bank built from the OLD stock: three channels disagreeing about what a face looks like. Rebuilding all three together (`build_prototypes --condense 90`, `pool_all` → `train_mlp`, `build_outline_protos`) removes the loss entirely and every real set improves: modern 74.2/47.3/71.4/69.3 → **76.0/50.0/74.6/71.7**; broad-30 87.7/70.4 → **88.2/71.0** (recall +0.5); legal-8 86.7/66.7 → **87.0/67.0** (recall +1.8); dev-8 92.8/81.3 → **93.6/82.1**. Synthetic, whose face is held out, loses ~1 word point at the two lighter severities and gains 0.5 at the heaviest.
