Adopted: the six faces are in `factory/stock.py` and the live build is 90 prototypes per class. LESSON: the three recognition channels are one model — rebuild them together or measure a chimera. |
| ✅ Sparse column layouts: numbers split at separators, and the 2-means gap band on short lines | Word segmentation from gap statistics (the classic bimodal-gap method) and its failure on lines with a single word gap; numeric-format endorsement as lexicon | `decode/beam.py` `numeric_join` (an uncertain gap the variant search reads both ways, scored with `numeric_endorsed`), `digit_mode_separators`, and the gap-band sanity check in `_segment_line` | Word-by-word diagnosis of an invoice after the stock widening left 18 missing words in four families: MONEY split at the thousands comma ('$7,165.00' → '$7,1' + '65.00'); DATE slashes read as '1' ('09/01/2024' → '0910112024', because digit mode boosted '1' and not '/'); WORD FRAGMENTATION ('Project management' → 'Proj act management'); and '@', which is not a class at all. (1) A FORCED join across a wide gap after a digit-separator-digit pattern recovered the money on the invoice (82.2→88.1 char, ~74→81.7 recall) but cost 0.4 word on every scan set, and a width guard on the separator recovered only 0.1 of that. Rewritten as the pipeline's own idiom — the gap becomes UNCERTAIN and the variant search reads it both ways, with a whole-shape numeric format counting as lexicon quality — it keeps the invoice gain. (2) Number separators (/ - . , : $ %) share the digit-mode boost. (3) THE BIG ONE: on 'Project management' the gaps are 0–4 px with one 14 px word gap; 2-means with a single outlier splits the letter gaps among themselves and calls 3 px a word space on a 21 px x-height line. A band whose 'word space' is narrower than the minimum plausible one (space_lo × x-height) is spurious and falls back to the x-height ratios. Invoice recall 81.7→**88.2**, payslip 67.1→**76.7** on the spot-check pages. FULL SETS, all three changes (separator boost; numeric join as an uncertain gap, context-gated to data lines — '$'/'%' or two digit-separator-digit triplets — because ungated it cost dev-8 0.5 word even when deferred; gap-band sanity check): dev-8 93.6/82.1/88.9/85.8 → **94.1/84.7/89.3/88.3**; legal-8 87.0/67.0/82.4/79.3 → 86.9/**68.5**/81.8/**80.3**; broad-30 88.2/71.0/81.5/78.7 → **88.3/72.5**/80.7/**80.9**; modern 76.0/50.0/74.6/71.7 → **77.9/62.2/78.0/82.3** — word accuracy +12 and precision +10.6 on today's documents, because money amounts stopped shattering into fragments. Synthetic unchanged. The recall dip on broad-30 (81.5→80.7) against precision +2.2 is the gap-band fallback merging a few true word pairs on scans; word accuracy, which counts both, is up 1.5. |
| 🔬 '@' as a class — NEGATIVE (gate it, don't add it) | Charset design: every added class competes with its lookalikes on every glyph | `factory/stock.py` (not in CHARSET), `factory/synth.py` `glyph_available` (now rejects fallback boxes for '@' too) | E-mail addresses read as 'billing0example.com' because '@' was never a class. Added (13 of 29 stock faces draw it), all three channels rebuilt (111 classes): the address reads, and dev-8 loses 0.9 word (94.1/84.7→94.1/83.8), legal-8 −0.5 char, and the sample invoice 4.3 RECALL (88.2→83.9) — a large round glyph with an inner loop is a plausible neighbour for a, e, 0, O and ©-like junk on every page, and it wins some of those contests, while there is one address per document to gain. Same lesson as the ligatures: a rare class must be admitted by CONTEXT (inside an e-mail pattern: token contains letters, a '.', and the candidate sits between them), not offered everywhere. Not adopted. |
| ✅ Digit-run continuation and the e→3 twin | Same numeric-continuation idea; confusion twins for digit mode (as l→1, o→0 already) | `decode/beam.py` `numeric_join` (digit\|digit gap inside a token that already holds a separator), `DIGIT_TWINS` e→3 | Two residues on the payslip: '06/15/2025' split into '06/1' + '5/2025' — a narrow '1' leaves a 10 px kerning gap that lands just above the band's 9.8 px word threshold, and the join rule only fired after a separator; and every '3' in Avenir read as 'e' ('$116.36' → '$116.e6', '36.25' → 'e6.25'), a geometric-sans shape with no twin in the table. Fixes: a digit\|digit wide gap inside a token that already carries a numeric separator continues the token (data lines only); 'e' joins the digit twins so digit mode can yield to '3'. Spot checks: payslip recall 76.7→**87.7**, invoice 88.2→**90.3**. FULL SETS: dev-8 and legal-8 unchanged to the decimal, broad-30 88.3/72.5→88.3/72.6 (precision 80.9→81.0), modern 77.9/62.2/78.0/82.3→**78.0/62.8/78.5/82.7** (old scorer). Small in aggregate because only the 16 business pages carry dates and amounts in these faces; on those pages it is the difference between a readable payslip and not. |
| ✅ Scorer folds typographic punctuation | Evaluation hygiene: score conventions the same for both engines (ISRI's accuracy tools likewise normalize before alignment) | `scripts/eval_unlv.py` `normalize` (shared by `eval_tesseract.py`, `harvest_truth.py`) | The modern set's truth comes from PDF text layers and carries curly quotes (‘‘ ’’ “ ” ’), en/em dashes and non-breaking spaces; UNLV truth is ASCII. Neither engine has classes for the typographic forms, so every one was scored as a substitution against both. Folded to ASCII before alignment — quote pairs first, then single characters. UNLV numbers are unchanged (no such characters in that truth, verified); modern numbers rise slightly for both engines: legacy 67.4/62.2/85.7/93.1 → 67.7/63.1/86.8/94.3; ours: see the follow-up entry once rescored. |
| 📊 Federal Register family: the gap is small type and footnotes, not columns | Diagnosis by word-set difference on one page | scratch `missing.py` | On FR page 9 (three columns, 8-pt body) we emit 1,198 words for 1,254 in the truth with all three columns segmented (79/67/70 lines), so nothing is lost as a block; recall 79.5 against legacy's 94.3 is (a) misreads of 8-pt body type and 6-pt footnotes at 300 dpi (x-height ≈ 12 px; the suppressed lines are footnote gibberish: 'precipation in overvinorisma'), (b) URLs and legal citations ('234.3(a)(17)(vii)', 'https://www.federalreserve.gov/…'), and (c) the truth itself: the PDF text layer includes the vertical marginal slug ('VerDate Sep<11>2014 … E:\\FR\\FM\\15MRR1.SGM') that no OCR of the page image can read, plus '§' which is not a class. Small type at 300 dpi is the same frontier the newspaper sets showed (RESEARCH, resolution study); the honest lever is 400 dpi input or the print-and-scan loop, not decoding. |
| ✅ Modern-set truth cleaned: production slug and glued line numbers | Ground-truth hygiene (ISRI zoning practice excludes page apparatus the OCR is not asked to read) | `scripts/make_modern_set.py` `clean_truth` (unit-tested) | The PDF text layer of every govinfo page carries a 4-pt VERTICAL production slug along the margin ('VerDate Sep<11>2014 03:20 Jan 14, 2023 Jkt 039200 PO 00000 Frm 00002 Fmt 6652 Sfmt 6201 E:\\BILLS\\H21.IH') — in the text, unreadable in any 300-dpi image — and bills' right-margin line numbers come out glued to the last word of each line ('jurisdic-17'), which on the page are two tokens. Both engines had been scored against that. Cleaned (slug lines dropped, trailing 1–2-digit numbers un-glued): one bill page 81.1/59.0/76.7 → 90.7/67.1/88.1 for us with no pipeline change; **legacy on the modern set 67.7/63.1/86.8/94.3 → 72.0/67.5/92.0/95.7**. Ours: **82.8/66.8/84.4/84.9** (from 78.0/62.8/78.5/82.7 under the dirty truth and old scorer). Against legacy on the cleaned set we lead by 10.8 char, trail word accuracy by 0.7, recall by 7.6 and precision by 10.8 — the modern gap is now mostly PRECISION (spurious tokens), no longer recall. A test set is only as honest as its truth; this is the second fidelity defect found in this one (the first was the print model), and both moved the numbers by more than most algorithm changes do. |
| 📊 Modern set after the sparse-layout work: where the remaining errors are | Bag-of-words difference aggregated per family (`scripts/missing_words.py` over every page) | scratch aggregation | BUSINESS PAGES (24 invoices/payslips/letters, cleaned truth): char 86.9 / recall 89.4 / precision 88.6. The 308 spurious tokens: 211 word-like misreads — almost all in the two HELD-OUT faces (Avenir: 'priya'→'priva', 'overtime'→'advedime', 'training'→'lraining'; Helvetica Neue: 'department'→'depatment') — 45 numeric, 42 fragments, 10 mixed. Top missing: 'priya' 9, '(8.25%)' 8, 'billing@example.com' 8, 'qty' 8, 'airlines' 8 (split 'air'+'lines'), 'department:' 8, '(415)' 8, '99.95%' 8, 'onboarding,' 8 (split 'onboard'+'ing'). Mechanisms, ranked: held-out-face glyph shapes (coverage, by design of the test); the '%' glyph (3 parts, a split suspect) losing to its own split into two boosted digits and ')' losing to a boosted '0' — fixed below; word splits where the variant search prefers a lexicon word plus a fragment because the whole ('onboarding', 'airlines') is not in the lexicon although its stem is; '@'; a bold 'Qty' header. BILLS (23 pages): char 88.3 / recall 80.6 / precision 82.1. Top missing 'of' 23, 'shall' 9, 'the' 9 against spurious 'd', 'o', 'sham', 're' 20, 's' 10 — the italic 'Be it enacted' lines whose letters touch ('of' arrives as one blob, 'll' reads as 'm'): the chopper's territory, not a class or lexicon problem; plus the page-foot slug '•HR 21 IH' (the bullet is not a class) and the ‘‘(i) item markers whose doubled quote reads as 'u'. |
| 📊 Bills: the missing 'f' of 'first' is italic touching letters, not a ligature | Diagnosis by group geometry | `scripts/missing_words.py` + a group-gap probe | 'first' → 'irst' twice on one bill page looked like the fi-ligature failure (RESEARCH, ligature classes). It is not: the first glyph of each 'irst' is a whole 's'- or 'b'-shaped group with no ligature candidate, and the line's groups are 52–70 px wide with 52 px word gaps — the italic 'Be it enacted…' lines are set in a face whose letters TOUCH, so each word arrives as one or two blobs, the split lattice cuts them, and the 'f' (whose hook overhangs the 'i') is lost in the cut. Chopper territory (the closed series), not a class gap. The gated ligature classes, rebuilt for this, therefore measured on the scan sets only as a safety check: dev-8 94.1/84.7→94.2/84.4, legal-8 86.9/68.5→86.4/67.8, broad-30 88.3/72.6→88.0/72.2. Negative again, gate or no gate; reverted, plumbing kept. |
| 📊 Truth-labeled real glyph set and channel attribution at scale | Ground-truth-aligned error analysis (ISRI practice), now per glyph | `scripts/harvest_truth.py` → `data/truth_en.npz` (134,838 glyphs from 120 non-evaluation letters, 3,035 line matches), `scripts/classifier_truth_eval.py` | Each decoded line matched to its truth line and aligned character by character through the new per-character provenance. RESULT: pipeline right on 96.8% of aligned glyphs (4,324 wrong). On ALL glyphs: prototype top-1 95.1, MLP 96.6, outline-among-top-6 95.4, truth within prototype top-14 98.5. On the WRONG subset: prototype 34.3, MLP 36.3, **outline 41.1**, truth within top-14 only **59%** — two errors in five have the truth outside the candidate list entirely. Attribution: 67.7% carry an adaptation pin to the wrong class (but every way of weakening pins measured worse — pins repair far more than they break), 52% sit in a word the lexicon endorsed, and only 6.2% are cases where the recognizer's top-1 was right and got overridden. BY READ KIND: whole glyphs err 2.7%, split pieces **13.9%** (4,678 pieces, ~650 errors — 3.5% of glyphs, 15% of errors), merges 42.7% (386). Top truth→decoded: ','→'.' 76, 'i'→'d' 73, 'i'→'a' 67, 'a'→'e' 61, "'"→'-' 56, 'i'→'l' 49, 'c'→'e' 47, '0'→'o' 44, '$'→'9' 34, '"'→'n' 32. CONSEQUENCES: (1) the 'i' family is the touching-pair chopper (fixed above, after this harvest ran); (2) split pieces need their own treatment — a cut piece carries a cut edge the whole-glyph prototypes never saw; (3) the truth labels cover punctuation, digits and capitals that the self-labeled harvest's lexicon gate never admits, so they are training data for every channel (`build_prototypes.py --truth`). |
| 📊 Truth-labeled exemplars as training data (prototypes + MLP) | Supervised training on aligned ground truth vs. self-training (the classic contrast; cf. Nagy's 'no shape training' line and its limits) | `scripts/build_prototypes.py --truth`, `data/prototypes_t.npz`, `data/mlp_t.npz`; `recognize/stage.py` `_family_mask` ("truth" tag = routing wildcard) | 134k truth-labeled glyphs (charset only; the ground truth's '~' unreadable marker and stray symbols dropped) merged with the synthetic renders and the self-labeled harvests, condensed to 60/class; MLP retrained on 325k exemplars (20 s). MEASURED with both truth models: dev-8 92.8/81.2→**93.3**/80.3 (recall 88.4, precision 85.5→84.2); broad-30 87.4/69.5→**87.8**/69.5, recall 80.0→80.7, precision 78.1→77.4; legal-8 86.5/66.5→85.5/65.2; synthetic 98.9/95.1 · 99.3/96.6 · 98.2/91.7 (sev0 word −0.5). Characters up on letters, words and precision down, another domain down: the truth glyphs are all business letters, and denser real exemplars lower every distance, including the junk lines the graphic gate used to catch. ABLATION: truth PROTOTYPES alone → dev-8 93.3/80.2 (precision 84.6), legal-8 85.8/64.0 — they carry both the character gain and the word/precision loss; truth MLP alone → dev-8 92.7/80.9, legal-8 86.8/66.8 — neutral to slightly positive. MECHANISM (dev-8 emission counts): with truth prototypes the graphic-suspect lines fall 37→24 and the suppressed lines 28→20, emitted words 1,628→1,649 against 1,583 true — denser real exemplars give junk lines (scribbles, rules) closer matches too, and the RELATIVE graphic gate (line median distance > 1.7× page median) no longer fires. Tightening the gate (1.7→1.5→1.35) with the truth prototypes: dev-8 93.1/80.0 → 93.0/80.0, legal 85.9/64.5 → 85.9/64.8 — precision does not come back and characters slip, so the gate is not the whole mechanism; the remaining word loss is unexplained (candidate-distance calibration is the suspect: denser pools compress distances and shift the pixel/LM balance). VERDICT: truth prototypes NOT adopted (character gain on letters, word and cross-domain loss); truth MLP neutral, not adopted. The truth set's value so far is measurement; its split-piece subset was tested next: adding only the 4,678 truth-labeled split pieces and 386 merges as exemplars (`--truth-kinds split,merge`, prototypes + MLP) — dev-8 92.3/80.2, legal-8 86.6/66.4, broad-30 87.5/69.0 (char +0.1, word −0.5): NEGATIVE. Cut pieces do not become a learnable population by adding a few thousand of them to a 60-prototype budget; the 13.9% error rate of split pieces is a chopper problem (where to cut, and whether), not an exemplar problem. |
| 🔬 Lexicon coverage hole (bug, fixed) | — | `scripts/build_langmodel.py` | Exposed by the larger corpus: dictionary words were excluded whenever they appeared in the corpus at all, but the weighted lexicon keeps only the top 30k — so a word occurring in the corpus yet outside that cut fell out of BOTH paths and vanished. Invisible with five novels (fewer than 30k distinct words); with 2.4M words "trademarks" disappeared entirely. Exclusion is now against the words actually kept. |
| ✅ Digit-heavy suppression exemption | Data-line recognition (numeric strings are unvalidatable by lexicon, by construction) | `decode/output.py` | The deletion study showed price-table rows ("3 x 5 (LINED) 3.00"), phone numbers and receipt codes being garbage-suppressed: no lexicon can endorse them and their confidences run low, so they met every gate. Lines whose alnum content is ≥40% digits (≥4 alnum) are now exempt from suppression including the graphic-suspect branch — junk that decodes digit-heavy is rare. dev-8 +0.6 char/+1.3 word (90.1/71.8, first dev char >90); broad-30 81.6 char, recall +0.1. |
| ✅ Dash-run scrub | Output sanitation; complements the punctuation position prior | `decode/output.py` | Follow-up dash study (2026-09-01): the position prior fixed '.'→'-' (69→~5), but '-' INSERTIONS rose 133→192 — signature scribbles and underline fragments now decode as dash runs (rejection retired) and dodge suppression via accidental short words ("no", "of"). Printed text never contains '-{3,}': such runs are deleted from tokens at output, emptied tokens dropped, hyphens and '--' untouched. broad-30 81.6→81.9 char; synthetic unchanged. |
| 🔬 GED gate re-sweep (negative) | Riesen-Bunke GED rerank gate calibration, revisited after the classifier legs | `recognize/stage.py` | The fresh confusion report ranked a vertical-stroke family (~190: l↔i, t→l/1, h→r, i→d, u→i) — exactly GED's territory — so the gates were re-swept on dev-8: margin 0.25→0.40, roughness 0.72→0.85, top 6→8, alone and combined. ALL FLAT (90.1/71.8 → 90.2/72.0 at best, noise-level). The gates are not the binding constraint: these confusions either never present the right candidate to rerank or lose their distinguishing evidence upstream (i-dots merging, t-crossbars eroding at small bitonal sizes). Next lever for this family is evidence-side (component/dot handling, feature sensitivity), not rerank tuning. Defaults unchanged. |
| 🔬 knn_scc edge-distance variant | Author's 2026 refinement of his 1995 algorithm | `layout/knn_scc.py` `distance_mode="edge"` | Link length = minimum distance between the two boxes' edge-center points (sector classification stays centroid-based). Measured: ties centroid mode on the fixture (IoU 1.00) and letters; on photo-heavy news it INVERTS the big-component bias rather than removing it — a large remnant's edges are near everything, so it merges INTO text (12 mixed blocks, 54.5% vs centroid's 47/77.4%). Kept as an option; the two biases trade places by domain. Same session: spread-based prune thresholds (mean+kσ, MAD) measured collapsing newspapers (heavy-tailed lengths from photo fragments) — default reverted to the 1995 ratio rule; paper amended with the cross-domain caveat. |
| 🔬 knn_scc per-axis pruning (granularity study) | Docstrum's separate within-line/between-line spacing estimates (O'Gorman, PAMI 1993) motivated splitting the prune threshold by link axis | `layout/knn_scc.py` `prune_scope="per_axis"` / `"per_axis_nn"` | Question (user-raised TWICE, paper Fig. 5): why does a letter's body weld into ONE block instead of per-paragraph blocks? TRUE RESOLUTION (2026-09-01, via the segmentation lab): it DOES weld at the default — the intermediate "11 blocks at the default" claim recorded here earlier was an ARTIFACT of a diagnostic script feeding the binarizer raw 0–255 gray instead of load_gray()'s [0,1]; Sauvola binarizes differently and the CC/link statistics shift. Correct-scale measurements on bus.3B 8760_001: default 1.5×mean (cutoff 137px) = 5 blocks, body welded (its paragraph gaps ≈90–135px sit just under the cutoff); 1.2×mean (110px) = 10 blocks, the classical letter decomposition; per_axis_nn = 47 blocks, mostly paragraphs but sparse lines shatter into word boxes (the earlier "103-block word confetti" figure was also mis-scaled). GRANULARITY DIAL conclusion stands with corrected level labels: 1.5×mean = region, ~1.2×mean = paragraph, nearest-link = below paragraph. Default unchanged; Fig. 5 rebuilt as a two-panel (a) default/(b) 1.2×mean comparison with the retraction noted. LESSONS (process): (1) regenerate figures after any default change; (2) ad-hoc scripts MUST load pages through core.imgio.load_gray — mis-scaled gray silently changes every downstream stage; (3) the lab exists precisely so segmentation claims are checked against the algorithm's live inner state. |
| ✅ knn_scc pooled-k link collection (author's refinement) | **Proposed by M. Sharpe (2026)** for his 1995 algorithm, from the lab's single-line evidence: per-sector quotas force every char into long north/south links however far the next line is | `layout/knn_scc.py` `k_total` (default None = 1995 spec) | Per-sector picks are pooled and only the k_total shortest links survive per node — a mid-line char keeps left/right/2nd-left instead of forced verticals. Two sub-refinements found by iterating in the lab: (1) DISPLAY-SIZE chars are exempt from pooling (they sit far from everything; a pooled top-k measured against body text starves headline links — first attempt shattered a news headline); (2) the "large" reference must be the median GLYPH size with specks excluded (newsprint's raw median CC is a 5px dot; 2×5px classified every char as large, exempted the whole page, and welded columns to 1 block). MEASURED: on the Fig-5 letter the LAYOUT quality is the best of any configuration — 19 blocks matching the human decomposition (every bullet, paragraph and letterhead field separate, vs 5 with the body welded at the spec default); synthetic fixture IoU 1.00 at every setting; news parity (columns each one clean block both ways; the headline shatters both ways — pre-existing display issue). END-TO-END OCR on dev-8 with knn_scc blocks: spec 89.2/70.6 vs pooled kt=5 88.6/70.7 — char −0.6 because finer blocks fragment reading order. VERDICT: the granularity lesson again — the OCR pipeline wants regions (spec default unchanged), but for layout-analysis consumers the pooled variant is the better segmenter. A second dial: k_total sets granularity structurally (at kt=3 the threshold stops mattering — connectivity, not pruning, is the constraint). USER CONFIRMED the refinement (2026-09-01) and supplied the IDUR lineage for the bullet question: strictly by whitespace each bullet is its own block, and COMPOSING them into a list is a logical operation — IDUR (1994) detected bullets geometrically and used a DEFINITE-CLAUSE GRAMMAR to recognize a bullet list as one-or-more bullets. That layering (whitespace-honest geometry below a grammar-based logical composer) is the design direction for a future logical-layout stage. Paper §4 gained the pooled-k section + Figure 8. |
| 🔬 knn_scc component conditioning (author's suggestions, optional) | **Proposed by M. Sharpe (2026)**: filter the CC list before graph building — tiny specks, oversized image components, components nested inside others, optional overlap-merge; kinship: Fletcher-Kasturi size filtering, Docstrum preprocessing | `layout/knn_scc.py` `cc_min_px` / `cc_max_factor` / `cc_drop_nested` / `cc_merge_overlap` (all off by default) | Nested rule refined during design: containment alone would delete BOXED TEXT (the Fig-5 letterhead address lives inside a drawn box), so a container must be SOLID (fill ≥0.35) or oversized — hollow frames keep their contents. MEASURED, full pipeline (despeckle+imagezones upstream): near-noop on letters; speck filter HARMFUL on news (44→4 blocks) — the thousands of short speck links were holding the length mean down, and without them the 1.5×mean cutoff climbs above the column gutter: the pruning statistics are COUPLED to the node population (future refinement: compute threshold stats over glyph-sized links only). MEASURED, STANDALONE (raw binary, no upstream cleanup): big+nested filters do imagezones' job on an illustration page (1 welded block → photo/text separated); letters clean; newsprint remains beyond size filters (a halftone FIELD's mid-size blobs survive speck removal — density detection is irreducible there). Overlap-merge: mild and safe on Manhattan layouts (21 unions, blocks unchanged). VERDICT: valuable for the algorithm's standalone story, redundant-to-harmful inside the full pipeline — all four ship as documented options, defaults off. ADDENDUM (author's point, same day): a component dropped for being BIG is not noise — it is a photo/illustration/boxed region the filter has effectively DETECTED. Dropped-big boxes now rejoin the final segmentation as `image_blocks` (merged among themselves only — never with text blocks, the measured photo-weld hazard — and interleaved in reading order). Standalone mailbag page: illustration returns as its own image block alongside the text blocks, a complete accounting of the page; the lab draws image blocks in orange. |
| ✅ Whitespace maximal empty rectangles (experimental) | H.S. Baird, "Background structure in document images" (1994); T.M. Breuel, "Two geometric algorithms for layout analysis" (2002) | `layout/whitespace.py` (`blocks.whitespace`; default remains `xycut`) | Branch-and-bound maximal empty rects over RLSA-smeared line segments (glyph-level obstacles fragment spanning headlines — measured); gutters validated by SIDE SUPPORT (ink hugging both flanks ≥0.30 of gutter height; real gutter measured 0.31/0.44, false margins/word-gaps ≤0.15 — an unvalidated version produced 22 false gutters on one newspaper page); banded column-major reading order (spanning blocks close bands). Status: passes the synthetic multicolumn suite, parity with xycut on newspapers (42.1 vs 41.3 char); the recall its better segmentation gains (+9 measured pre-validation) is not yet retained through validation. Trim-to-supported-extent measured WORSE (recall 20.5). A visual-debugging session (2026-08-31) then mapped the full failure space on one newspaper page and banked four findings for a redesign: (1) RIVERS AT GUTTER SCALE — obstacle smear must exceed word gaps (15–25px body, 50–70px display) yet stay under gutter width (~30px): no single length works, so spans need their own long smear; (2) the long smear welds body lines across narrow gutters, so span candidates must also be TALL — and the height median must be computed over line-sized obstacles only (tiny fragments drove the median to 4px and neutered the gate); (3) gutter validation must be flank-ASYMMETRIC — the next column's left edges align under any alignment mode, but the left column's right edges are ragged unless justified; (4) removing span-band obstacles from column bookkeeping deleted body text wholesale when a false span band overlapped columns (aggregate 42→25 char). All variants reverted to the parity implementation; the redesign should be built against SYNTHETIC NEWSPAPER FIXTURES (known gutters/spans/rivers as unit tests) rather than live-page iteration. |
| ✅ Table cell grid from rule intersections | Ruling-based table recognition; R. Zanibbi, D. Blostein & J. Cordy, "A survey of table recognition" (IJDAR 2004) | `layout/tables.py`, `decode/output.py` | Union-find over touching rules groups a frame; clustered rule coordinates give row/column levels; cells are the inter-level rectangles (≥2×2 required — lone separators are not tables). Output stage places decoded words into cells by box center → meta.tables_text grid. Side discovery: language detection on a near-empty page (9 isolated words) confidently picked Italian — detection now requires ≥60 chars of pseudo-text and otherwise falls back to a default language. |
| ✅ XY-cut newspaper/magazine prior: half gutter, tall-gutter rule, narrower row gap for newspapers (2026-09-12) | Nagy & Seth 1984 (XY-cut); the known weakness — a spanning headline blocks the column cut until a row gap frees it — and the tab-stop answer to it in R. Smith, "Hybrid page layout analysis via tab-stop detection" (ICDAR 2009) | `layout/blocks.py` `XYCutBlocks` (`min_column_h_300dpi`, doc_type branch) | First numbers on UNLV mag.3B and news.3B (eight pages each, seed 1, neural): 59.2/44.2 and 62.3/52.1 char/word against legacy Tesseract's 87.4/85.0 and 96.4/93.6. Two of the sixteen pages were lost to the deskew edge pile-up (cleanup row); with that fixed, 66.5/48.6 and 73.1/61.7. Two two-column newspaper pages still read 28 char: their gutter is 0.06", under the letter's 36-px minimum, and the second headline line sat 20–26 px above the byline (the letter's row break needs 30), so the body block kept the headline, whose letters crossed the gutter and blocked the column cut; the columns were then read line by line, fused. Variants on both sets: gutter 18 px alone — news 84.5/74.1, mag 68.2/50.8; plus a tall-gutter rule (a vertical cut needs a region at least 150 px tall, because a display headline's WORD gaps are wider than a newspaper's gutter and had cut 'Joyful patrons of all races' into four blocks) — news 84.6/74.3, mag 68.3/50.7; plus row gap 18 px — news **92.8/83.1** (the two pages 92.9/82.4 and 94.0/84.9), mag 65.8/50.2 (magazine leading is heavier; blocks shred). The whitespace-rectangle blocks stage on the same pages: news 56.1/43.4 with a crash, mag 58.9/38.9 — worse than XY-cut either way (its crash, a cut span past the mask in `components._cut_column`, is clamped). Adopted as a `doc_type` prior in the pattern of the letter/legal one: newspaper gets all three, magazine the gutter and the tall-gutter rule. Classic on the same pages: news 92.1/79.0, mag 65.3/40.6. Zone-ordered (`--zone-order`, recognition without reading order): news 92.9/82.7 — the order is now right — and mag 74.9/53.4 against 68.3/50.7 plain, so a third of the magazine gap is still reading order (a page at recall 92.7 reads 65.7 char plain, 91.6 zoned). The four standard sets carry doc_type letter, legal or none and are untouched by construction. |
| 🔬 Tall-gutter rule as a GENERAL default — NEGATIVE, stays a newspaper/magazine prior (2026-09-12) | As above | `blocks.min_column_h_300dpi=150` on every document type, classic profile | dev-8 95.2/89.2 → 95.4/89.6, broad-30 91.8/82.0 → 92.0/81.9, legal-8 91.7/81.2 → **89.9/76.3**, modern 89.6/80.8 → **84.2/59.5**. The rule forbids a vertical cut through any region under 150 px tall, and that is exactly what a pleading's line-number column beside a short block, and an invoice's or payslip's cell row, need: their columns are one or two lines tall by construction. A letter's headline word gaps were never the problem there. Kept at 0 by default; newspapers and magazines, whose short regions are display headlines, set it through the `doc_type` branch. |

## Glyph features (`glyph/features.py`)

| Feature | Lineage | Notes / deviations | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ Zoning densities (8×8) | Classic hand-print OCR feature; surveyed in Ø.D. Trier, A.K. Jain & T. Taxt, "Feature extraction methods for character recognition — a survey", Pattern Recognition 29 (1996) | Our single strongest feature family (measured +4–5 pts top-1 over 4×4). |
| ✅ Hu invariant moments | M.-K. Hu, "Visual pattern recognition by moment invariants" (1962) | Log-compressed to tame dynamic range. |
| ✅ Crossing counts | Classic; Trier et al. (1996) | 4 rows + 4 columns of ink-run counts. |
| ✅ Skeleton endpoints/junctions | Thinning: T.Y. Zhang & C.Y. Suen (1984) via scikit-image `skeletonize` | Counts only in the feature vector; the full structural representation lives in `glyph/skeleton.py` (attributed graphs: endpoint/junction nodes with positions and degrees, stroke edges with length/angle/straightness, explicit loop count; rings like 'o' become a self-loop node). |
| ✅ Gated GED reranking | K. Riesen & H. Bunke, "Approximate graph edit distance computation by means of bipartite graph matching" (IVC 2009); structural OCR lineage per Kahan-Pavlidis-Baird | `recognize/ged.py`, `recognize/stage.py`, `scripts/build_skeletons.py` | Bipartite-assignment GED over skeleton graphs as a SECOND OPINION on the feature matcher's top-6, active only behind two gates, both measured necessary: (1) roughness < 0.72 — flip-noise corrupts skeletons (ungated: −5 on heavy isolated glyphs; fixes 7, breaks 12); (2) feature top-1/2 margin < 25% — blur corrupts skeletons while SMOOTHING roughness, and ungated GED overrode confident correct calls (synthetic sev2 −13 word). Three further lessons completed it: (3) bank-uncovered candidates need the NEUTRAL mean GED — rescoring only covered classes penalized them relatively, and accent classes with no skeletons flooded top-1 ("the"→"tnü"); (4) the bank must be OPERATOR-CONSISTENT — clean 48px prototypes mismatch Sauvola-thinned 32px pipeline crops (an 'e' whose bar vanished needs a matching degraded prototype), so the bank carries 48px/32px/blurred-32px variants; (5) scale swept to 10 (25 taxed synthetic). FINAL: broad 30-page letters 75.7 char/44.2 word (+1.1/+2.1), dev-8 84.2/55.5, synthetic at parity — a clean net win, largest in the clean-to-light regime where customer grayscale PDFs live. Language detection gained a challenger margin (default holds unless beaten by 0.08) after noisy top-1 text flipped borderline en/de. |
| ✅ Persistence-graded hole counts | Simplified from persistent homology (H. Edelsbrunner, J. Harer, *Computational Topology*, 2010) | Holes counted after closing at radii {0,1,2}: a hairline-broken 'o' keeps its hole at r≥1. Poor man's persistence — no filtration diagram. |
| ✅ Moment-based deslanting | Slant normalization from handwriting recognition; cf. Bozinovic & Srihari (1989) | Shear by μ11/μ02, clipped to ±0.6. Lets one prototype cover roman + italic (+3 pts shape-class top-1). |
| ✅ Stroke-width normalization | Cf. Tesseract's character normalization (R. Smith, "An overview of the Tesseract OCR engine", ICDAR 2007) | Erode/dilate toward width = 0.16·max(h,w), estimated from the distance transform median. |
| ✅ Side profiles (left/right first-ink distance) | Profile features; Trier et al. (1996) | Separates b/d, p/q. |

## Recognition, decoding, language

| Technique | Lineage | Code | Notes / deviations |
|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ Nearest-prototype (1-NN, z-scored) | T. Cover & P. Hart, "Nearest neighbor pattern classification" (1967) | `recognize/nearest.py` | Std floored at 5% of mean std — a raw ε amplified near-constant features into the dominant distance term (measured catastrophic). Candidate depth matters far more than expected: top-k 5→8→10 gained +2 then +4 char on real scans (the truth for unseen fonts often sits at rank 6–10, where the beam+LM+adaptation stack can still find it); 12 plateaus. Beam width does NOT follow: 16/24 measured slightly worse than 8 even with 10 candidates — wider beams surface LM-favored wrong paths. |
| ✅ Synthetic training data via font rendering + degradation | H.S. Baird, "Document image defect models" (1992); T. Kanungo et al., "A statistical, nonparametric methodology for document degradation model validation" (PAMI 2000) | `factory/synth.py` | Kanungo edge-distance-decaying flips + blur + skew + illumination field + mirrored-page bleed-through. Every sample keeps its θ. Bitonal variant added 2026-08-31 (blur → hard threshold, the fax/bitonal-scanner pipeline per Baird's defect model): prototype variants at threshold 0.4/0.55 improved BOTH real scans (+2 char) and synthetic (+2–6 word) — a general augmentation, not just domain matching. |
| ✅ Over-segmentation + LM-scored variant choice | Segmentation survey: R.G. Casey & E. Lecolinet, "A survey of methods and strategies in character segmentation" (PAMI 1996); document decoding: G. Kopec & P. Chou, "Document image decoding" (PAMI 1994) | `glyph/components.py`, `decode/beam.py` | Wide groups (>1.5× median) get an ink-minimum cut hypothesis; decoder enumerates ≤8 variants/word. Full DID-style lattice not implemented. |
| ✅ Character n-gram beam decoding | C.E. Shannon (1948) n-grams; beam search standard practice | `decode/beam.py` | Trigram over prefix context; add-one smoothing. |
| ✅ Corpus-frequency lexicon + correction | OCR post-correction practice; cf. K. Kukich, "Techniques for automatically correcting words in text" (ACM Surveys 1992) | `lang/model.py`, `decode/beam.py` | Tiered: corpus top-30k weighted + dict-words (≥3 chars) at a floor. Flat word lists measurably hurt (junk 2-letter entries). |
| ✅ Dictionary-endorsed word segmentation | Word-boundary DP practice (cf. Kukich 1992 §segmentation) | `decode/beam.py` `_best_gap_variant` | Key invariant found the hard way: beam score can never be the primary key for split choice — every extra word resets the n-gram context, so shredding always wins locally. Lexicon endorsement is primary; scores break ties. |
| ✅ Bimodal gap clustering for spaces | Standard bimodality assumption on inter-word vs inter-letter gaps (layout surveys, op. cit.) | `decode/beam.py` `gap_band` | Per-line 1-D 2-means; x-height ratio only as fallback (median-of-heights x-height is ascender-inflated — measured twice). |
| ✅ Case disambiguation from line geometry | Typographic-context practice in classic OCR | `decode/beam.py` `_height_prior` | Plus orthographic prior: capital-after-lowercase penalty. Dotted i/j are ascender-tall — missing this cost ~10 char pts (bug of 2026-08-30). |
| ✅ Shape-confusion twin injection | Classic OCR confusion-pair handling (n/h, c/e); same mechanism as case-twin injection | `decode/beam.py` CONFUSION_PAIRS | When one of a confusion pair is a candidate, the other joins at penalty 1.6 so language context arbitrates. Motivating diagnosis: a typewriter letter where 'h' ranked below top-14 while 'n' led — "the" (19 page occurrences) decoded as "LEe"/"CLe", unreachable by any LM because 'h' was never offered. Worst page 75.3→79.5 char; letters set +1.5 char/+4.0 word. Penalty calibrated two-objective (1.0 cost degraded synthetic −3.9 word; 1.6 keeps both). Digit-mode fix in same leg: a letter pin must not override digits ("1993"→"l993" via an 'l' pin). e↔o added next leg after a sans-font page decoded "president"→"prosidonl" throughout ('e' bar fills/vanishes): letters +1.6 char/+5.5 word AND synthetic up — the strongest single pair so far. e↔s and 9↔s followed ("Drivs", "5ss5"=5995): letters +0.3 char/+1.3 word, word acc crossing 50%. Same leg fixed a plain coverage hole: '&', '$', '%', '/', '#' were absent from the class set entirely — "&" decoded as 'a' forever in a corpus full of business text. |
| ✅ Digit-mode decoding (rank-graded, categorical twins) | Numeric-field handling practice in forms OCR | `decode/beam.py` `_beam_word` | Digit evidence graded over candidate ranks (rank-1 = 1.0, ranks 2–3 = 0.6); in digit mode the letter LM is muted, lexicon substitution off, digit candidates boosted, letter pins do not apply, and DIGIT_TWINS (l/I→1, o→0, s→5, z→2, B→8, g→9…) are injected. Boost arithmetic alone lost when adaptation left short sharp candidate lists ('l' beat '1' by 1.5 nats on 5 entries — unit-reproduced), so the rule is categorical: a top-scoring letter with a digit twin in contention yields to the twin. "l993"→"1993", "357l"→"3571", "92l23"→"92123". Letters +1.5 word; ~0.4 synthetic char tax accepted. |
| ✅ doc_type layout priors | Original plan scope (optional hint, never required) | `cli.py --doc-type`, `layout/blocks.py` | Hint flows via page.meta. Single-column prior (letter/book/legal): column-split gap requirement rises 2.5×. Measured +0.4 char on letters — and +6.0 char / +9.6 word on legal pleadings, where double-spaced monospace text is full of vertical whitespace RIVERS (aligned character cells stack spaces into full-height channels) that shredded pages into ~187 single-line blocks. Auto river-defense added: a page shredding past 80 blocks retries the cut at the wide gap — legal unhinted recovers 67.2→70.5 char (hint still best at 73.2). |
| ✅ Corpus confusion methodology | ISRI-style error analysis at character-alignment level | `scripts/confusion_report.py` | Edit-distance backtrace over the full 30-page sample aggregates substitutions/insertions/deletions — the scaled version of the worst-page play; statistics nominate fixes. First run surfaced: ~550 case flips, p→D ×144, punctuation→letters ×280, and net ~700 spacing errors as the largest op category. |
| ✅ Baseline-descender prior | Baseline-relative typographic context (classic zone analysis: ascender/x-height/descender bands) | `decode/beam.py` | A glyph whose box crosses the line baseline is a descender letter (p/y/g/q, not P/Y/D/9), and vice versa — geometry always computed (lines stage baselines) but never consulted at decode. With the tiny-glyph punctuation prior (a mark under 40% of x-height prefers .,'- over letters), broad 30-page letters +1.4 char / +2.7 word and synthetic sev-1 +3.4 word. |
| ✅ Case-pattern grammar | Orthographic convention (words are all-lower, Capitalized, or ALL-CAPS); cf. truecasing literature (Lita et al. 2003) | `decode/beam.py` | Any case transition inside a word pays a penalty except upper→lower right after the first letter. Found via error decomposition on UNLV: case-folding alone moved word acc 16.6→28.6% on the worst page — random per-glyph flips inside caps words were the top word killer. |
| ✅ Garbage-line suppression (shape-gated) | Reject-class practice; cf. handwriting/signature discrimination in letter processing | `decode/output.py` | A line is suppressed only when THREE tests agree: zero lexicon words, mean confidence <0.15, AND degenerate shape (one character ≥40% of the line — "IIIIIIxIIIII"). The original two-test version deleted misread-but-real lines on hard fonts ("ADril 5, 19s31" = April 5, 1991; addresses; "Sincerely"), silently costing whole lines of recall; the shape gate returned them (+1.7 char on letters). |
| 🔬 Graphic-line suppression (distance-based, partial) | Non-text discrimination via recognition confidence | `decode/beam.py`, `decode/output.py`, `adapt/cluster_refit.py` | Attempted: flag lines whose pass-1 median top-1 distance ≫ page median (logo shapes match no prototype), suppress unless a ≥4-char endorsed word survives; graphic-suspect lines excluded from adaptation. KEY DISCOVERY en route: adaptation PINS logos — repeated identical strokes are the most self-consistent shapes on a page and rescored to distance ~0.0 (measured), the purest error-amplification case; the pass-1-only rule and adaptation exclusion are kept as guards. But the relative test fails exactly where needed: stylized-font pages inflate the page median so logo lines (1.2–1.5×) hide below real-line ratios elsewhere (1.3–1.4×). Open: needs absolute distance calibration or shape-level non-text features (density/periodicity), not a page-relative ratio. |
| ✅ Dotted-rule filtering | Perforation/dotted-ruling handling; complements solid-rule morphology (Wong-Casey-Wahl) | `glyph/components.py`, `decode/output.py` | A "line" of ≥20 dot-sized groups (≤8px median) is a perforation or dotted rule, not text — one receipt tear-off line decoded its dots as 313 junk words, costing a page −54% word accuracy; solid-line morphological opening cannot see dotted rules. Groups dropped at the components stage; a single-char-flood backstop (≥80% one-char tokens) added to output suppression. One fix moved the ENTIRE 30-page letters sample +1.6 char / +5.1 word. Found via broad-sample honesty check: the 8-page dev set had been ~10 points rosier than a 30-page sample — tuning-set optimism made measurable. |
| ✅ Fragment-join repair (group-level re-decode) | Mirror of dictionary word segmentation (Kukich 1992) | `decode/beam.py` `_join_decoded` | Letter-spaced caps make letter gaps as wide as word gaps (unimodal-large distribution defeats gap_band), shredding "National" into "Nat i ous l". Text-level concatenation is insufficient — the fragments are usually also misread — so candidate runs of ≤3-char fragments are RE-DECODED as one word over their combined glyph groups, letting the lexicon repair the reading; the merge is kept only if the result is an endorsed word and most fragments weren't words on their own ("on to" never becomes "onto"). |
| ✅ Adaptive rejection (median + k·MAD) | Robust statistics: F. Hampel's MAD outlier rule | `decode/beam.py` | Replaced 3×median (multiplicative rule was tightest exactly on clean pages). Reject exempts adapt-pinned glyphs. |
| ✅ Per-document adaptation (cluster → vote → refit) | Tesseract's adaptive classifier (R. Smith 2007); T.K. Ho & G. Nagy, "OCR with no shape training" (ICPR 2000); decipherment view of OCR | `adapt/cluster_refit.py` | Average-linkage clustering (measured 100% purity at cut ≤8 on synthetic pages); purity-gated cluster votes; labeled glyphs pinned as hints; unlabeled glyphs re-scored against document prototypes with cross-calibrated distances. Replacing all candidates amplified errors (measured) — blast-radius control is the design. Negative result (2026-08-30): a second adapt+decode round gains ≤0.5 pts — adaptation converges in one pass; iteration not worth the cost. |
| ✅ Calibration sheet with corner fiducials | Fiducial-marker practice (cf. ArUco, Garrido-Jurado et al. 2014); classic registration | `factory/sheet.py`, `decode_sheet.py` | 3 large + 1 small square; TR/BL disambiguated by requiring det>0 (a reflection fits all 4 marks!). Labels from pure geometry. |
| ✅ Degradation-parameter fitting by distribution matching | Simulation-based calibration; Kanungo et al. (2000) model validation | `factory/fit_theta.py`, `scripts/fit_theta_unlv.py` | Per-feature 1-D Wasserstein on glyph summary stats; Nelder-Mead over (blur, flip rates). Works label-free on harvested real crops. Negative result (2026-08-31): against UNLV bus.3B the fit only moved the distance 3% and the fitted theta gained ~nothing — those TIFFs are ALREADY BITONAL, so the dominant degradation is the scanner's own thresholding of low-res glyphs, which a gray-blur+flip model cannot mimic. build_prototypes now gates theta inclusion on fit quality (final < 0.8×initial). The calibration sheet (gray scan, known device) remains the intended use. |
| ✅ Font-family routing | Font recognition: A. Zramdini & R. Ingold, "Optical font recognition using typographical features" (PAMI 1998); also this plan's "runtime document router" idea | `recognize/stage.py`, `factory/fonts.py` | Exemplars tagged serif/sans/mono by face name; page's dominant family detected by a dominance vote (top ≥1.4× runner-up) among the CONFIDENT HALF of a glyph sample — absolute shares were too diffuse because degraded glyphs vote randomly. Matching then restricted to that family. Prototype-set study (2026-08-31, 8 UNLV pages + 220-word synthetic): 14 accidental-sans fonts = best synthetic, worst real; 26 curated (serif+mono+sans incl. Courier/Times/Georgia) = best real, −5-10 synthetic without routing; routing recovers most of it; 40 fonts (incl. SF UI faces) = noise-level mixed. Locked at 26 curated. Per-BLOCK routing added later (letterhead diagnosis: display-type logo lines decoded as word salad after inheriting the body's family): blocks with ≥12 glyphs vote their own family (+0.3 char/+0.4 word letters; synthetic unchanged). A "display" family (Academy/Herculanum/Luminari — the very faces once excluded as prototype poison) is now stocked FOR ROUTING ONLY: within the routed architecture they help letterheads instead of diluting (letters 79.5→81.0 char, +3.2 word; synthetic unharmed). The same faces unrouted had measurably hurt — the mechanism, not the fonts, was the problem. Composition is now PINNED BY NAME (scripts/build_prototypes.py BODY_NAMES): alphabetical-limit selection reshuffled the stock on every scanner change, and a .ttc-scan experiment that widened body to 30 with new serifs crashed synthetic sev0 92.7→75.4 (reverted). The .ttc scan's keepers: Impact and Copperplate as display faces (routing-quarantined, zero cost, both letter sets at baseline). The deliberate widening experiment then ran: appending 6 classified .ttc faces (AmericanTypewriter/Athelas/Baskerville/Charter/Cochin/Didot) to the pinned base with nothing displaced STILL cost letters −1.3 char/−3.6 word and sev0 −2.2 char — dilution is intrinsic to a bigger 1-NN pool even within routed families. The 23-face body is a genuine measured local optimum; per-document adaptation, not stock width, is how new fonts get covered. |
| ✅ Directional k-NN + SCC block segmentation (experimental) | **Original algorithm by M. Sharpe** (designed pre-project); kinship: L. O'Gorman's Docstrum (PAMI 1993) also clusters connected components by k-NN, via angle/distance histograms | `layout/knn_scc.py` (`blocks.knn_scc`) | Per character: 3 nearest neighbors in each of 8 compass sectors (directed edges); prune long edges; STRONGLY connected components = blocks; overlapping boxes merged. Merit test verdict: REAL MERIT — synthetic multicolumn fixture 3/3 blocks at IoU 1.00 untuned; on a real newspaper page it produced the best block structure of any implementation (coherent headline + three clean columns, no rivers); letters 79.7 char vs 81.2 for the hand-tuned incumbent, zero tuning. Refinement found: the spec's global 1.5×mean prune fragments display type (body spacing dominates the mean) — a HYBRID rule (global + extra links between mutually large-but-not-huge characters) fixes headlines without welding columns (pure relative pruning welds them: 2× an ascender exceeds a news gutter). Open hardening: photo-heavy aggregate pages lose recall (suspect: merge_overlapping absorbing text into photo-remnant boxes); needs the fixture-driven pass every stage gets. |
| ✅ Own character GRU language model | Recurrent char LMs: Mikolov et al. (2010), Graves (2013); GRU cell: Cho et al. (2014); user's proposal, self-trained per the project's founding constraint | `lang/gru.py`, `scripts/train_charlm.py`, `lang/textprep.py` | The project's own trained model: 258k-param single-layer GRU (hidden 256, embed 48, vocab 78) in PURE NUMPY — documented forward pass, longhand BPTT verified by finite-difference gradient checks, numpy Adam. Trains on the boilerplate-stripped Gutenberg corpus in 18 min on a laptop (24 epochs, 45 s each). Held-out perplexity 3.27 vs the trigram's 10.03 (same stream). Chosen over a transformer because beam search wants an O(1) per-hypothesis state: `GruLM` threads a hidden state through each beam, one batched step per glyph yields the full next-char distribution. Trained on RUNNING text (spaces included), so it models cross-word context the word-internal trigram never saw; lexicon/frequency stay with the corpus model; trigram remains the fallback when no weights exist for the locked language. lm_weight recalibrated 0.7→0.5 (sharper log-probs). RESULTS: broad 30-page letters 77.1 char / 47.3 word / 63.2 recall (+1.4/+3.1/+3.6 — largest LM-side gain of the project); dev-8 85.8/59.7; synthetic ≈ parity (pangram vocabulary is atypical for the corpus — accepted). Cost: decode slows measurably (per-word batched GRU steps). |
| ✅ Accented glyph classes (Latin multilingual) | Diacritic handling practice; components-level grouping reuses the i-dot mechanism | `scripts/build_prototypes.py` (105 classes), `decode/beam.py` | Accented letters are whole classes (the overlap grouper already merges mark+base into one glyph group). Three compensations, all measured: marks-above are TALL and ç is a DESCENDER for the height prior; accents outside the locked language's alphabet are penalized (LANG_ACCENTS); and top_k rose 10→14 because accent variants crowd their base letter's neighborhood and evict true competitors from shorter candidate lists (−2 char on English until compensated). Residual multilingual tax on English letters: ~0.7 char pt. |
| ✅ Language detection, multilingual corpora | n-gram language ID: W. Cavnar & J. Trenkle, "N-gram-based text categorization" (1994) | `decode/beam.py` `_detect_language`, `scripts/build_langmodel.py` | Five models (en/de/fr/es/it from Gutenberg corpora; alphabet extended to accented Latin); the page's pixel top-1 text is trigram-scored per language, best wins, locked per document. Three measured pitfalls fixed: (1) unequal corpus sizes make the smallest model flattest so it wins on any noisy text — counts are scaled to a common per-million rate before add-one smoothing; (2) each model's score is calibrated against its own-corpus baseline (likelihood ratio); (3) the pseudo-text must be split at word gaps — whole-line concatenation destroys the ^xx/xx$ boundary trigrams that carry much of a language's signature. Glyph classes are still ASCII; accented prototypes are the remaining piece for full multilingual decoding. |
| ✅ Multi-part sign grouping by nesting ('%') | Connected-component grouping of multi-part glyphs (Smith 2007 §3.1 on diacritic/base association; classic CC-to-character assembly) | `glyph/components.py` `_group_overlapping` (`nested_overlap`, `nested_min_h_factor`) | The stacked rule (x-overlap ≥ 0.5 of the narrower part) merges dots and accents but not the lower circle of '%', which overlaps the slash's foot by 5 of 11 px: '99.95%' read '99.95-40' on every modern letter page. Second rule: a part at least 1.5 dot-sizes tall whose whole y-span lies INSIDE another part's joins at 0.4 overlap — Latin print puts no separate blob beside a letter within its own height except the two-part signs and scanner-broken letters (both want merging); the height floor keeps a kerned period out. Unit-tested on the measured boxes. Whole-set effect is small (the sign is rare: modern word +0.1) but the confusion was systematic. |
| ✅ Vertical bar named by geometry | Position-based punctuation disambiguation (this project's punct-position priors; cf. Tesseract's baseline/x-height normalisation) | `decode/beam.py` `_is_bar` (post-pass) | '\|' is the field separator of modern letterheads ("Tel … \| www…") and read as 'I' on every modern letter. It is NOT a trained class: adding it would only steal from l/I/1. Instead the standalone-letter post-pass names a lone stroke taller than 1.5 x-heights whose foot drops 0.12 x-heights below the baseline — '\|' spans ascender to descender, l/I/1/! sit on the baseline. The recognized-lines' x-height is now published on the line for the geometric post-passes. Unit-tested. |
| ✅ Numeric formats admit a space cut | Format grammars as a lexicon (cf. Tesseract's number-pattern DAWG, `number-dawg`) | `decode/beam.py` `_best_gap_variant`, `decode/formats.py` | The strict admissibility rule let a cut through an uncertain gap only when BOTH parts were dictionary words, so '(415) 555-0190' was glued on every letterhead. A whole-shape numeric format (phone parts, amounts, dates, ZIP, and now percents) endorses a part as a word does. dev-8 recall +0.1 / precision +0.1, modern up on the letters. |
| ✅ Space before a comma or period joins | Typographic rule (no word starts with , or .) used as a segmentation constraint | `decode/beam.py` `_segment_line` (`_tiny_mark`) | Tracked figures on the invoices open a gap wider than the space threshold before the thousands comma: '$1 ,200.00'. A mark under `punct_small_frac` x-heights whose top reading is ',' or '.' joins the word before it whatever the gap. Merely making the gap uncertain LOST: the variant scorer counts "$1" and "200.00" as two endorsements against the whole's one — the join has to be a rule. |
| ✅ Numeric endorsement split from the digit-mode gate | Deferred decision principle (this project's design rule 4) | `decode/beam.py` `_beam_word` (`numeric_format` in word meta), `_decode_word` | A format hit was first made to count as `in_lexicon` so '(8.25%)' could answer the split reading's bonus. That flag ALSO gates the deferred digit-mode re-read ("graded evidence only if the word is not in the lexicon"), and "$35.55" is as valid an amount as "$39.99" — so the re-read that fixes 5/9 was skipped and the modern invoices lost 3.4 word (payslips −0.9) while every other kind was flat. The endorsement now lives only where it belongs: as a +2.0 term among the segmentation variants. Invoices restored; the percent line still reads. General lesson: a boolean that means two things will be wrong for one of them. |
| ✅ Merge charge (−1.0 per removed character) | Segmentation-search cost balance (Tesseract's chop/associate search uses a per-segment cost; Smith 2007 §4.2) | `decode/beam.py` `merge_char_bonus` | The first sweep compared 0 and +2.2 only; the negative side was never tried. At 0, three disconnected letters of 'tailspin' and 'Priya' merged into one confident wrong letter ('ttspin', 'PAya'). −1.0 gained on ALL FOUR sets (dev-8 char +0.2, broad-30 +0.3/+0.2, legal-8 +0.3/+0.5 with precision +0.8, modern +0.1/+0.2); −2.2 over-shreds ('Priva Date'). |
| ✅ Digit mode: a digit spawns no letter twin | Confusion-pair injection (this project) meeting Tesseract-style numeric context (Smith 2007 §4.4 on number patterns) | `decode/beam.py` `_beam_word_mode` (CONFUSION_PAIRS loop) | Every Avenir 9 on the modern invoices read as 5 ('$495.00' → '$455.00') although the classifier ranked '9' first by 47 distance units. Bisecting the word decoder per prior found the term: the ('9','s') confusion pair injects 's' at the 9's score minus 0.3; on an all-figure line the estimated x-height IS the digit height, so the lowercase height prior paid 's' +0.8×scale, 's' outscored its own source, and the categorical digit-twin rule ("a letter with a digit twin in contention yields to the twin") promoted 's'→'5'. In digit mode a digit now spawns no letter twin. Measured: modern 68.0→68.4 word (+0.5 recall/precision; the Avenir invoices' amounts all correct, 'SOTO:' → 'Bill To:'), legal-8 68.8→69.1 (+0.4 recall), dev-8 and broad-30 flat. Method note: the per-glyph bisection probe (decode one glyph with each prior zeroed in turn) found in four runs what reading the code did not. |
| 🔬 Segmentation lattice over ranked cuts — NEGATIVE, opt-in | The plan's "lattice" stage; Tesseract's chop-and-associate search over a segmentation graph (Smith 2007 §4.1–4.2); classic over-segmentation + DP | `glyph/components.py` `lattice_cuts`, `recognize/stage.py` `_lattice_options`, `lattice_paths` | Motivation: bill titles in a large serif (Century-like) set letters touching through hairline serifs — 'Administrator' arrives as 7 blobs for 13 letters, a 106-px five-letter blob read WHOLE as a confident 'e'. The fixed 2/3-piece options cannot reach it; a widest-first recursive chop (tried first, `split_max_pieces`) cuts every 'm' and 'w' in half because a wide letter exceeds the letter-width cap — page result mixed, removed. The lattice: components stores up to k ranked ink-minimum cut columns per suspect; the recognizer scores EVERY span between boundaries (≤ 27 crops for k=6) and ranks whole paths by summed top-1 distance minus the page median (a plain sum favours few pieces however badly they read); the k best paths become the `alts` the decoder already consumes. Page probe on the worst bill: 7 words gained ('payment', 'Illinois', 'Introduced', 'awarding'), 6 lost ('JANUARY' → 'JerrY', 'Services' → 'Fleeces'); bag-of-words neutral. Sets (k=5): dev-8 94.1/84.7 → 93.7/83.9, modern 83.1/68.4 → 82.6/67.7. Why: pieces cut between serif-touching letters carry the neighbour's serif stub, and the distances that rank the paths are calibrated for whole glyphs — the same lesson as piece-aware outline scoring. 'Advstrator' persisted: the true path was in the lattice but its pieces did not read as d-m-i-n-i. What would make it work is a partial-shape classifier (Tesseract's answer) or synthetic training on cut pieces; both deferred. Kept opt-in at `lattice_cuts=0`. |
| ✅ Class-aspect prior, gated off on fixed pitch | Width/aspect expectation per class (Tesseract's blob-width and x-height normalisation feeds its classifier; Smith 2007 §4.3) | `recognize/nearest.py` `class_geometry`, `decode/beam.py` `aspect_prior`/`aspect_tol` | The classifier sees a normalised crop: an 'ail' blob three letters wide reads as a confident 't'. The recognize stage now publishes each class's trained median height/width (de-standardised from the exemplar features) and the decoder can charge a candidate per log-unit its box departs from it beyond a ±40% band. Page probes (weight 1–2): fixes 'resting'→'hosting', breaks 'Tail shin'→'TaiLOR', 'Elm'→'nIm', 'Client'→'Event' — narrow-set faces (Arial Narrow, Avenir) sit outside the band of the training stock, so the prior punishes the correct class. Whole-set measurement at weight 1.0 told a different story from the page probes: dev-8 94.1/84.7→94.2/85.0, broad-30 88.6/73.1→88.7/73.4, modern 83.0/68.0→83.1/68.1, and legal-8 87.0→86.3 char — the typewriter set. Fixed-pitch faces stretch every glyph toward one advance width, so the stock's proportional aspects are wrong there; the components stage now publishes its fixed-pitch verdict on the layout and the decoder drops the prior on such pages: legal-8 restored exactly (87.1/69.1), dev-8 keeps its gain. Adopted at 1.0; 2.0 over-punished narrow faces ('Elm' → 'nIm') on the old features. Re-swept after the feature and calibration fixes (2026-09-07): 2.0 gains dev-8 87.5 → 88.0 word, legal-8 79.2 → 79.5 (precision 87.8 → 88.9), broad-30 80.6 → 81.0, modern −0.1; 3.0 no better — 2.0 adopted. Lesson repeated: three-page probes show the failure modes, only the four-set run shows the sign. |
| ✅ Word and line renders with controllable letter spacing (2026-09-09) | Synthetic word images for sequence recognizers: Jaderberg et al. (2014) random kerning and fonts; Shi, Bai & Yao (2017) CRNN on synthetic words; line renders per Breuel et al. (2013); degradation per Baird (1992) via `synth.degrade` | `factory/words.py` (`render_line`, `touching_pairs`, `render_word_window`, `sample_theta`, `sample_tracking`), `scripts/make_seq_data.py` | Characters are placed one at a time (PIL has no tracking control) so every box is exact; negative tracking pulls neighbours into contact, and an OWNER MAP drawn from each glyph's thresholded mask says which adjacent characters share a connected component after rendering — the touching-pair label itself. Two defects found by looking: drawing the owner map with `fill=index` anti-aliased the index (an edge pixel of character 5 read '2', every pair looked touching); and tracking applied before a space left one pixel between italic words. Measured on Arial: letters meet below −0.10 em (0 pairs at −0.10, 5 of 6 at −0.12), so the tight range is [−0.22, −0.06] em. Blur is a fraction of the type size, not a pixel count: a 0.9-px blur that softens 26-px type erased 12-px type entirely (looked at), so sigma ≤ 6% of the x-height. Windows are degraded at NATIVE size, then normalized to the 32-row strip (x-height 13 px, baseline row 22; `glyph/strip.py`). 250k windows, 43% with a touching pair, 8 min on 10 workers. |
| ✅ Truth-labeled real word strips (2026-09-09) | The truth-labeled glyph harvest (above), at word level | `scripts/harvest_lines.py` | Per decoded line matched to its truth line, one window per word cut at the gap midpoints (as the decoder cuts) and labeled with the TRUTH substring between two word boundaries the alignment matched — whatever the pipeline read inside, so a word decoded 'Depatment' yields its strip labeled 'Department'; the harvest is richest exactly where the pipeline fails. Same contamination guard as every harvest. Pitfall: `align()` yields its operations from the line's END backwards; walked forward it found no boundaries at all (18 words from 3 pages; 677 once sorted). bus.3B 170 non-eval pages and legal.3B 240. |
| 🔬 Word-strip sequence scorer, CRNN + CTC, as a rerank term (2026-09-09, under measurement) | Graves, Fernández, Gomez & Schmidhuber (2006) CTC; Shi, Bai & Yao (2017) CRNN; Breuel et al. (2013) and Smith (2016, Tesseract 4) line normalization by x-height and baseline; Cho et al. (2014) GRU cell in torch's gate convention | `recognize/ctc.py`, `recognize/seq.py` (numpy reference: forward, masked BiGRU BPTT, CTC gradient), `recognize/seq_torch.py` (optional mirror, `[train]` extra), `glyph/strip.py`, `scripts/train_seq.py`, `decode/beam.py` `seq_path`/`seq_weight`/`seq_gate`/`_seq_rescore` | The neural profile's first term, aimed at the 53% of excess errors that are touching pairs: each word's segmentation variants (split × merge, as enumerated by `_decode_word`) are rescored by −log P(text \| word strip) under CTC, relative to the variant's best, so 'rt' and 't' are judged by ONE model over ONE image with no cut placed — the decision four chopper series could not make with whole-glyph distances. Never injects a class or a string by default (the glyph CNN's injection was catastrophic). 32 × W strip, conv 16/32/64/64 with vertical-only pools after the first (column stride 2 px so 'll' keeps a blank frame between), BiGRU 96, 285k parameters. Activations past each strip's width are zeroed after every layer so a strip's posteriors do not depend on batch padding (measured 0.1-nat drift on the last frames before). Trained on 250k synthetic windows + real truth strips ×2, page-disjoint holdout. Throughput on this M4 Pro: torch/MPS 310–620 samples/s (the GRU backward launches a kernel per frame), CPU torch 430, numpy 250 — the plan's 4–8k/s estimate was wrong by an order of magnitude; an epoch over 420k windows is 12 min on MPS, so a 12-epoch model is 2.5 h and numpy is the overnight backend. Numpy/torch forward parity to 1e-4 is a test. |

## Evaluation

| Technique | Lineage | Code | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ Levenshtein char/word accuracy | V. Levenshtein (1966); ISRI accuracy-report conventions (Rice & Nartker) | `scripts/eval_pages.py`, `scripts/eval_unlv.py` |
| ✅ Order-independent word recall/precision | Bag-of-words evaluation practice | `scripts/eval_unlv.py` | Added after magazine measurements: edit distance conflates reading-order mismatch with recognition error (one page: 15.8% char "accuracy" yet 53% of words correctly read). Recall vs word-acc gap = order problem; low recall = recognition/coverage problem. |
| ✅ GT-zone-ordered scoring | ISRI evaluation practice (manual zoning; Rice & Nartker) | `scripts/eval_unlv.py --zone-order` | Output words re-sorted by (.uzn zone, line baseline, x) before scoring — sort must use line baselines, not word centers (word centers interleave within a line; measured). Order convention is worth ~4-5 char pts on mag/news. |
| 🔬 Small-size prototype variants (16 px) | Size-matched exemplar practice | — (tried, reverted) | Negative result (2026-08-31): flat on newsprint. Small bitonal type (~15 px x-height) loses topology in the source scan itself; no exemplar coverage recovers information that binarization destroyed upstream. The news/mag gap needs layout/reading-order and possibly higher-resolution source sets (4B), not more prototypes. |
| ✅ Held-out-font evaluation at graded severities | Standard generalization protocol | `scripts/eval_glyphs.py`, `scripts/eval_pages.py` |
| ✅ UNLV/ISRI corpus measurement | S.V. Rice, F.R. Jenkins & T.A. Nartker, ISRI annual tests of OCR accuracy (1992–96) | `scripts/eval_unlv.py`; sets in data/unlv (bus.3B/3A, mag.3B, news.3B/4B) | Resolution study (2026-08-31, paired same-document news 300 vs 400 dpi; after the wavy-rule fix): 4B wins 5/6 pages, mean +2.3 char — resolution helps consistently; the one former −8.2 outlier was a rule-detection bug, now fixed. Clean originals (bus.3A) vs photocopies (bus.3B): +1.8 char. Rejection-threshold sweep (3–10 MADs): flat — adaptation pins already exempt most would-be rejects. |
| ✅ Real-glyph harvest (corpus-scale self-labeling) | Self-training/adaptive-classifier lineage (Ho & Nagy 2000; Smith 2007) taken corpus-wide; motivated directly by the external benchmark's gap decomposition | `scripts/harvest_glyphs.py`, merge in `scripts/build_prototypes.py` | The pipeline runs over real pages and keeps (features, label, family) for glyphs inside lexicon-endorsed, confident, ≥4-char words on clean lines — 52,571 exemplars from 163 UNLV letters. CONTAMINATION GUARD: the harvester reproduces the evaluation samples' exact shuffles and excludes every evaluated page. Merge guards: per-class cap 80 (250 measured diluting — the pool-size law holds for real data too) and inlier-core filtering vs the class medoid (a mislabeled exemplar is a decoy). RESULTS: the largest word-accuracy leg of the project — broad 30-page word 47.3→52.3 (+5.0, crossing 50%), precision +4.0; dev-8 word +6.2; synthetic char 96.0/91.9/93.5 (+4.2/+1.7/+4.7) — real glyphs generalize better than synthetic degradation. ROUND 2 (flywheel iteration, 2026-09-01): re-harvest with the round-1-improved model grew the pool 52,571→75,395 (+43%). Word-level metrics compound — dev-8 word 59.7→63.2 (+3.5), broad recall +2.4 / precision +4.7, synthetic word sev1 63.1→71.8 / sev2 63.6→79.6 — but headline broad char/word barely moved (77.1→77.3 / 52.3→52.4) and dev-8 char dipped 85.8→83.9: the flywheel now polishes glyphs it already reads while the residual errors live in segmentation/layout, not classification. Diminishing returns reached in 2 rounds; further harvest breadth (legal.3B) queued, further letter re-harvests not. CROSS-DOMAIN MERGE STUDY (2026-09-01, before merging legal.3B): the hypothesis that the class-level medoid/cap discards a minority family's mode motivated three family-aware merge variants — ALL measured worse than the plain pooled cap-80 on broad-30 (pooled 77.3 char): (a) equal per-family budget shares starved the dominant family (76.6, −0.7); (b) full cap-80 per family flooded the pool with real English exemplars until distance calibration shifted and ACCENTED classes started rejecting (French decode test went red — accent classes have no harvested exemplars to balance them); (c) per-family inlier filter + pooled cap gave the best dev-8 of the project (85.1/65.0) but still lost broad (76.6/52.0) and still broke the accents test. Reverted to pooled; lesson: the pooled class medoid IS the balance point between synthetic and harvested mass, and dev-8 optima that lose broad-30 are overfits to the 8 tuning pages. LEGAL.3B HARVEST (2026-09-01): 44,537 exemplars from 120 non-eval pages (contamination guard widened to seed-1[:30]+seed-2[:30] first — legal evals default to 10 seed-1 pages). Through the pooled merge: legal-10 FLAT (79.6/54.3 vs 79.9/54.9 — legal pages were already well-read without their own glyphs), broad-30 flat (77.5/52.2), dev-8 85.6/66.0. Root cause of the flatness: POOL SATURATION — at cap 80/class, 120k harvested exemplars compete for ~4.7k merged slots, so a 44k-exemplar harvest nets ~100 merged additions. Harvest breadth via this merge is exhausted; more real data cannot reach the model without a different consumption mechanism (bigger model, per-domain prototype sets, or self-training beyond 1-NN). The error frontier stays segmentation/layout. |
| ✅ Citation labels take no digit-mode bias | Numeric-context decoding vs legal citation grammar ("§ 234.3(a)(17)(ix)"); the deferred-decision rule | `decode/beam.py` `_beam_word_mode` (`flanked`) | Every Federal Register citation read "(a)" as "(3)" and "807(b)" as "807(6)". The candidate dump showed 'a' top-1 at 19 with 'e' second at 39 and NO '3' in the list: digit mode injected '3' as the digit twin of the rank-2 'e' with the boost attached, and it edged the 'a'. A lone glyph whose neighbours carry '(' and ')' in their top three is a label — letter or digit equally — and is decoded on pixel evidence alone: no digit boost, no twin injection. FR page: every citation label restored, 16 lines changed, none for the worse. Sets: modern 68.7 → 68.8 word, dev-8 / legal-8 / broad-30 exactly flat — the rule fires only where labels are, which is what a rule should do. Also measured: parentheses without the digit-mode boost (`digit_mode_parens=false`) — modern 68.7 → 68.8 word, neutral; the boost stays. |
| ✅ Alternative cuts may sit close: 'the' read 'he' | Chopping with several candidate chop points (Smith 2007 §4.1); this project's ranked cut options | `glyph/components.py` `split_cuts` (2), separation `piece // 2` | broad-30 error census (30 pages, 1,423 missing / 1,314 spurious words): the largest single class was 'the' missing 41 times against 'he' spurious 26, with 'with', 'will', 'this', 'that' behind it — the 't' of a touching 'th' vanishing. Candidate dump: the 35-px 'th' blob reads 'h' at distance 18 (and adaptation pins it 'h'); its ONE split option cut at 22 px — inside the 'h', between stem and leg, where the ink minimum is — and the pieces read 'f' + '1'. The t\|h kiss 9 px away had been excluded by the ranked-cut separation, which equalled the minimum piece width: right for simultaneous cuts, wrong for alternatives. Alternatives now need only half a piece apart and two are offered: census page 8726 reads 14 of 14 'the' (was 4), zero 'he'. Sets (with the word-split gate below): dev-8 94.2/85.0 → 94.3/85.3, broad-30 88.7/73.4 → 88.6/74.3 (recall +0.4, precision +0.8), legal-8 87.1/69.1 → 87.0/69.8, modern 83.3/68.8 → 83.5/70.0 (recall +0.7, precision +1.3). The census pointed straight at it: one hour from the frequency table to the fix, against days of guessing before. Three cuts (2026-09-06): the remaining 'he' page (8531, a thicker t-h bridge) reads 7 of 14 'the' with three cuts against 5, but the sets say no — dev-8 85.2 → 84.6 word, legal-8 flat, broad-30 75.3 → 75.2, modern 70.5 → 70.3; more alternatives feed the split bonus more wrong pieces. Two stays. |
| ✅ Word split gated on gap width | Missing-space repair vs compound names; gap statistics of inter-letter vs inter-word spacing | `decode/beam.py` `split_min_gap` (0.18 x-height) | The lexicon-driven split of an unknown 7+ letter word into two endorsed words cut 'Mathcad' into 'Math cad' 15 times on one page (and 'TurboCAD'). A lost space leaves a gap wider than a letter pair's (~0.1 x-height); a compound name does not. The split now requires the gap at the cut to reach 0.18 x-height: page 8753 keeps 'Mathcad' (7 of 19 read whole; the rest are other errors), no stray 'Math'. |
| ✅ Everything inside an art zone is art | Layout practice: text and graphics are exclusive regions (Wahl, Wong & Casey 1982 block classification; Tesseract's image-region masking) | `layout/imagezones.py` (inside-box absorption after zone boxes are final) | Census: the biggest spurious class on broad-30 was 331 tokens of one or two characters, and the two worst pages shed 41 and 27 lone letters — from a letterhead emblem and a church logo (an open book), not from text. The zone detector had caught each emblem's thick strokes but absorbed only neighbours larger than 4× the median glyph, so the emblem's glyph-sized crumbs (edge speckle, the gaps between strokes) stayed 'text' and read as 'x', 'u', 'J', '-u.--'. Now every component whose box lies inside a zone box (grown by the absorb gap) joins the zone: page 8519 lone-letter lines 10 → 4, spurious words 325 → 313 against 298 truth; the two survivors are page-edge scraps outside any zone. First version absorbed inside EVERY zone box and ate the typed 'Sincerely' under a signature zone on dev-8 (fill 0.08 of its box; the emblem fills 0.28): now only zones filling ≥ 0.15 of their box absorb. Sets (with the pin fix below): broad-30 88.6/74.3 → 88.9/75.0 (precision +0.6), dev-8 recall +0.2 / word −0.2, legal-8 and modern exactly flat. |
| ✅ Adaptation pins follow position for the comma/apostrophe pair | Per-document adaptation (Smith 2007 adaptive classifier) meeting this project's position priors; the case-twin pin geometry already there | `decode/beam.py` (pin block, `_baseline`) | Census: "you'll" → "you,ll" 6 times. The apostrophe glyph (13×18 px, 0.76 x-heights) had ',' at rank 1 with "'" second AND a comma pin: adaptation clusters the two marks (one shape, two heights) and pins the cluster's majority — commas — onto every member, and the pin bonus outweighed the position prior. The pin now re-targets by position as the case pins do by height: a pinned ',' whose box floats above the baseline becomes an apostrophe pin, a pinned "'" that hangs becomes a comma pin (thresholds shared with the position prior). Census page 8542 restored; unit-tested both ways. Measured together with the zone absorption above (the pair is inseparable in the runs): broad-30 word +0.7. |
| ✅ Quotes and brackets are language-model transparent | Character LMs over a restricted alphabet; punctuation as out-of-vocabulary (cf. Tesseract's separate punctuation DAWG, kept apart from the word DAWG for the same reason) | `decode/beam.py` `WRAPPERS`, `wrapper_lm_logp` (−4.0); merged/split pseudo-glyphs now carry `_baseline` and `parts`; floating short glyphs penalize alphanumerics; `''`/`""` fold to `"` | Bills census: 70 of 629 missed words were quoted citation labels (`‘‘(i)’’`, folded to `"(i)` by the scorer), read "u(i)" or "(((ii)"; broad-30 has 23 more. Four causes, found one at a time with the per-glyph score table: (1) the curly opening quote is two 9×18 tick blobs that merge into one glyph, and the merged pseudo-glyph carried no baseline, so no position prior ever judged it; (2) it carried no part count either, so the multi-part '"' class paid the dot prior; (3) letters do not float — a short glyph a quarter x-height above the baseline now penalizes alphanumerics; (4) decisive: the trigram alphabet is letters + apostrophe, so a quote at word start scored the floor (−13.8 vs −4.2 for 'u') and poisoned the next transition to the floor too, and the GRU was worse (−23.4). Quotes and brackets now cost a flat −4.0 and are stripped from the trigram context. Bill page hr500-p005: 5 of 5 quoted labels correct (was 0). Sets: modern 83.5/70.0 → 83.6/70.5 (recall +0.5, precision +0.5), broad-30 88.9/75.0 → 89.0/75.3, dev-8 word +0.1, legal-8 char −0.1. Lesson: a language model over a restricted alphabet is not neutral about what it cannot spell — it is maximally hostile, and the hostility hides until a letter competes with the punctuation. |
| ✅ Deslant at 3x with block-OR; stroke target from the glyph, not the canvas | Shear normalization by second moments (classic slant correction, cf. Bozinovic & Srihari 1989); stroke-width normalization (Tesseract's outline normalization does the same job differently) | `glyph/features.py` `_deslant`, `extract_features` | Bills census: the italic enacting clause on every first page read 'Remote' for 'Senate', every glyph 70–100 units from its nearest prototype, with the roman on the same page at 20. Widening the stock with the open TeX Gyre italics (Schola is the bills' Century face) did NOT help — and Times New Roman Italic's own 'e' at 28 px, a face in the stock, read as '-'. Tracing the extractor: (1) the order-0 shear on the binary mask dropped a quarter of a hairline's ink (52 → 38 px) wherever the nearest-neighbour sample fell beside a 1-px stroke; a linear resample thresholded at 0.5 lost the same; shearing at 3x and returning by block-OR keeps every pixel (a 24-px leaning bar comes back vertical at 24 rows). (2) The stroke normalizer's target width is a fraction of the glyph's size — measured on the PADDED deslanted array for italics and on the render CANVAS for synthetic glyphs, so an italic dilated to 3.8x its ink and lost its counter, and every render was normalized to a fatter stroke than the tight page crop it must match. Cropping to ink before the normalizer fixes both: TNR Italic 'e' 52 → 68 px, one counter (was 148 px, none). Measurement had to be done carefully: the harvest files store FEATURE VECTORS from the old extractor (137k exemplars, a third of the live pool), so a rebuild that merged them was confounded and measured negative (modern 70.5 → 69.2 word). Isolating the features — old against new, both built from renders alone, no harvests — is decisive: dev-8 73.0 → 83.5 word, broad-30 64.6 → 73.8, within 1.5 points of the live model WITH its harvests. So the harvests had largely been compensating for the render/crop stroke mismatch: real crops matched real crops while the synthetic prototypes stood systematically off. The harvests were regenerated under the new extractor (letters 200 pages → 86,730 exemplars, legal 45,594, digits 1,919 — twice the old digit count) and the truth file re-featured from its stored crops; the live prototypes, pool and MLP rebuilt. Four sets, old live → new live: dev-8 94.3/85.2 → 94.3/86.0 (recall 89.6 → 90.4, precision 88.8 → 89.5), broad-30 89.0/75.3 → 89.6/76.5 (82.1 → 82.9, 83.6 → 84.6) — the best headline yet — modern 83.6/70.5 → 83.8/70.5, legal-8 86.9/69.8 → 86.4/69.3 with recall 82.1 → 83.4 and precision 81.5 → 83.4.
