Also learned: the 'o'-hole shape gate was right to reject TeX Gyre Termes Italic — its 'e' renders as junk at every size — so stocked names get ordering priority but no gate bypass. |
| ✅ Page x-height anchor from mixed-case lines | Case decision by size (this project's height prior); x-height estimation from bimodal ascent distributions (cf. Tesseract's per-line x-height fitting with a page-level fallback) | `decode/beam.py` `_low_mode`, `_page_x_height`, `_line_x_height` | The rebuilt live model lost 4.3 word on one legal-8 page while gaining precision everywhere else; that page read 'CONSENT' as 'consENT' with the classifier putting every letter upper at rank 1 by wide margins. The decoder's size twins (c/C, o/O, s/S) go by the line's x-height, and an all-caps line's own median IS its cap height; the per-line caps rule falls back to a PAGE anchor — which was the median of per-line median ascents. On a pleading, mostly capitals and typewriter lowercase full of ascenders, that median is a cap height too (27.5, caps at 29–31, x-height 20): the rule could never fire. The anchor is now the median of the true x-heights of the page's MIXED-CASE lines (each line's low 2-means mode, exposed by `_low_mode`; caps lines contribute nothing). Page 9462: recall 67.2 → 73.3, precision 70.3 → 76.6; dev-8 letter 8530 also up 0.8 recall. Sets (anchor alone): legal-8 86.4/69.3 → 88.4/72.6, broad-30 89.6/76.5 → 89.7/76.6, dev-8 flat, modern 70.5 → 69.7 word (invoices/payslips: their numeric lines now anchor on the page x-height too, and the smaller scale exposed the calibration defect below — fixed there, not here). Two companions tried and reverted: gap thresholds scaled by the line's median glyph height, and a tall reward for capitals in the height prior — together dev-8 and broad-30 −1.5 word with legal-8 flat. |
| ✅ Pixel-evidence temperature from the top-1 distance | Softmax calibration of distance-based classifiers (Platt-style scaling; Tesseract converts its rating to a confidence with a fixed scale, not a per-list one) | `decode/beam.py` `_glyph_logprobs`, `evidence_temp_frac` | Chasing 'Elm' → 'blm' on an invoice (the 'E' at distance 8, 'b' at 25.5, a 3x ratio): the per-glyph softmax used the candidate list's STANDARD DEVIATION as its temperature, and a top-14 list always carries far junk at its tail (125, 140…), so the std was ~50 and the two leaders came out 0.3 nats apart — a margin the language model overturns at will. Every pixel decision in the system had been flattened this way. Temperature = 0.5 x the top-1 distance instead (floor 1.0): the same pair is 4.4 nats apart. Three-page probe: invoice-helvetica-0 recall 85.8 → 91.5, legal 9462 74.1 → 87.1 (precision 78.2 → 91.8), letter 8530 87.7 → 90.0; the 'the' page 8726 and the logotype page 8557 also up. Sets, together with the x-height anchor above: dev-8 94.3/86.0 → 94.7/87.2, legal-8 86.4/69.3 → 90.3/78.5 (recall 83.4 → 88.1, precision 83.4 → 87.7), broad-30 89.6/76.5 → 90.8/79.8 (85.1 / 87.1), modern 83.8/70.5 → 84.2/70.7 (88.4 / 89.0). The largest single-change gain in the project's record. Sweeps at the new scale: temperature fraction 0.7 worse everywhere, 0.35 better than 0.5 on all four sets (dev-8 87.2 → 87.3 word, legal-8 78.5 → 79.2, broad-30 79.8 → 80.3, modern 70.7 → 70.8), 0.25 no better than 0.35 — adopted 0.35; lm_weight 0.35 and 0.7 both within 0.3 word of 0.5 in either direction across three sets — kept at 0.5. split_char_bonus 1.5 / 3.0 against 2.2: 1.5 dev-8 +0.2 word (precision +0.4), legal-8 and broad-30 flat; 3.0 slightly worse — kept at 2.2 (a lean, not a win). pin_bonus 1.5 / 4.0 against 2.5: 4.0 dev-8 +0.2, broad-30 +0.2, legal-8 −0.8; 1.5 worse everywhere — kept. digit_mode_boost 0.7 / 2.0 against 1.2: 0.7 leans +0.1..+0.2 word on three sets, 2.0 leans down; merge_char_bonus −2.0 / −0.5 against −1.0: inert to 0.1. All kept. The weights tuned on the flattened scale sit near their optima on the sharp one: the temperature was the lever, not the weights around it. |
| 🔬 Serif italics from the TeX Gyre stock — mixed, not stocked | Stock widening (this project's font-stock rows); the open TeX Gyre faces are metric clones of Century Schoolbook, Times, Palatino and Bookman with true italics | `factory/stock.py` BODY_NAMES (+6), `factory/fonts.py` (.otf discovery, stocked names first), `~/Library/Fonts/mlws-ocr-stock/` | Motivated by the bills' italic clause; first measured on the OLD features and did nothing, because the extractor was the problem. Re-measured on the fixed features: renders-only variant dev-8 83.5 → 84.3 word, modern 68.8 → 69.7, broad-30 −0.2; on the live recipe with regenerated harvests: dev-8 86.0 → 86.2, legal-8 69.3 → 69.1, broad-30 76.5 → 76.7, modern 70.5 → 70.8 (char mixed within 0.4). Small and consistent on word accuracy under the OLD evidence calibration; rebuilt into the live models after the temperature fix it turned char-positive and word-negative — dev-8 94.7/87.2 → 94.8/87.6, legal-8 90.3/78.5 → 90.8/78.1, broad-30 90.8/79.8 → 91.0/79.3, modern 84.2/70.7 → 84.0/70.2 (recall +0.4). Word accuracy is the headline, so the six faces are not stocked; the pre-italic models are restored and the fonts stay installed for a later `--add-fonts` try (the bills' italic clause is now a decoder matter, since the same glyphs read 'Senate' and 'assembled' with the fixed features). Two of the eight fetched faces were out from the start: TeX Gyre Termes Italic renders junk 'e' at every size and Schola Bold Italic fails the same 'o'-hole gate — the gate was right. |
| ✅ Cut candidates one per valley | Chop-point selection at profile minima (classic projection-profile segmentation; Smith 2007 §4.1 ranks candidate chops) | `glyph/components.py` `_cut_candidates` | The census after the calibration fix still showed 'the' → 'he' on page 8531 (8 of 14). Its 41-px 'th' blob's ink profile: the h's arch is a plateau of four columns at 4 px, the t\|h kiss two columns at 5 px. Ranking raw columns put BOTH offered cuts in the plateau (25 and 28, three columns apart) and never reached the kiss. Candidates are now one per valley — a maximal run of equal profile values no higher than its flanks, represented by its centre — ranked by depth. Page 8531: 'the' 5 → 7 of 14, 'he' 5 → 2, recall +0.5; page 8726 keeps 14 of 14. Unit-tested on a synthetic profile. Sets (at temperature 0.5): modern 70.7 → 71.1 word (recall +0.6, precision +0.5), broad-30 79.8 → 79.9, dev-8 flat, legal-8 word flat with recall −0.5 — adopted. |
| ✅ Spacing scales with the line's own size, case with the page's x-height | Word-gap thresholds relative to type size (classic inter-word gap statistics, e.g. Wong, Casey & Wahl 1982) kept apart from the x-height that decides case | `decode/beam.py` `space_scale` ("line") | After the page x-height anchor (correct for CASE), the modern payslips lost 6 word: their digits-only lines now took the page's x-height as their scale and the gap thresholds shrank, splitting '$150.90' into '$1 50.90' and '11/09/2025' into '1 1/09/2025'. Two scales tried: the line's median glyph height ('size') restores the payslip and invoice but wrecks prose (letter recall 93.7 → 77.6: a line rich in ascenders puts the median on the tall mode); the line's own x-height estimate WITHOUT the page-anchor substitution ('line': low mode when bimodal, glyph height when uniform) restores both — payslip-avenir-1 recall 84.9 → 89.0, precision 78.5 → 85.5; invoice-helvetica-0 85.8 → 91.5 / 82.0 → 92.4; the letter unchanged. Lesson: one estimate served two consumers with different needs; the anchor fix helped case and silently broke spacing until the census-by-kind showed the payslips falling. Sets: modern 70.8 → 71.7 word (recall 88.6 → 89.1, precision 89.2 → 90.1), broad-30 80.3 → 80.4, dev-8 87.3 → 87.2, legal-8 79.2 → 78.7 — the typewriter pages' uniform caps lines take their glyph height and the wider thresholds fuse words, so fixed-pitch pages keep the anchored scale: legal-8 back to 90.5/79.2 exactly, dev-8 unchanged at 94.4/87.2. Adopted. |
| 📊 Reading order re-measured: closed | Zone-order scoring (UNLV .uzn zones) as the upper bound on ordering | `scripts/eval_unlv.py --zone-order` | The roadmap carried 1.4 word points on letters as the reading-order gap. On the adopted model, dev-8 scored in the truth's zone order is 94.5 / 86.4 / 91.2 / 89.1 against 94.4 / 87.2 / 91.1 / 90.6 in our own order — the truth ordering is no better (slightly worse on word: zone boundaries split words the layout keeps whole). Column-first XY-cut and row alignment of column blocks closed the gap; item retired. |
| ✅ Bullets named by geometry | Position/size-based naming of untrained marks (this project's '\|' rule); UNLV's '~' bullet convention | `decode/beam.py` `_is_bullet` (standalone post-pass), `scripts/eval_unlv.py` fold '•' → '~' | Census after the calibration fix: the top spurious token was '.' (23) — on page 8522 a column of 15×15-px squares, the list bullets of a business letter, each read as a confident period (distance 6). UNLV writes bullets '~' (559 in bus.3B). A lone glyph with '.' at rank 1 whose box is roughly square and 0.6–1.3 x-heights tall is a bullet, emitted U+2022 and folded to '~' by the scorer. Page 8522: 5 of 10 bullets, recall 82.9 → 84.3, precision 83.4 → 84.8; the other five '~' are UNLV's em-dash convention ('-~' for a long dash we read '-' or '--'), not glyphs, and are left to the scorer's judgement. Same page, same census class: two 9×2-px slivers at the page's top edge read '-' on 'lines' whose x-height was 2 px; the output stage now drops any line whose x-height is under 5 px (`min_line_xheight_px`), never text at any dpi — three scrap lines gone, unit-tested. Sets, measured together: dev-8 87.2 → 87.5 word (precision 90.6 → 90.9), broad-30 80.4 → 80.6, legal-8 and modern flat — adopted. |
| ✅ Absolute glyph quality in path choices | Tesseract sums absolute ratings across a word's segmentation (Smith 2007 §4.2: chop/associate compared by rating, not by normalized probability) | `decode/beam.py` `abs_quality_weight` / `abs_quality_scale` (off by default until measured) | Business census: '(8.25%)' read '(8.2596)' on all eight invoices and 'payroll' read 'payro"' on all eight payslips. Both are path choices the per-glyph softmax cannot make: it is relative to each glyph's own list, so a '%' at distance 14.7 (excellent) and its two halves at 38 and 44 (poor) all score ~0 for their top candidate, and only the split bonus and digit boosts decide — for the split. Likewise a merged 'll' read '"' at 16.7 beats two singles at 0.0 and 9.1. A per-glyph cost of top-1 distance / 20 units per nat, constant within the list, restores absolute quality for split-vs-whole and merge-vs-singles: invoice-helvetica-0 recall 91.5 → 94.3, precision 92.4 → 95.2, 'Payroll' back, the 'the' page's splits intact. Also found on the way: a merged pseudo-glyph counted its pieces as 'parts', so any two merged letters earned the multi-part dot prior meant for quote ticks; only tick-sized pieces count now (unit-tested). Sets at weight 1.0: dev-8 87.5 → 88.9 word (precision 90.9 → 92.9), legal-8 79.2 → 79.6, broad-30 80.6 → 81.6 (precision 87.9 → 89.2), modern 71.7 → 72.7 (recall 89.1 → 90.0, precision 90.1 → 90.9) — up on every figure of every set; adopted. |
| ✅ Slant kept per glyph; the slash judged against the line's lean | Slant normalization loses slant (a known cost of deslanting, cf. Bozinovic & Srihari 1989); per-line skew/slant estimation | `glyph/features.py` `glyph_shear`, `recognize/stage.py` (`g["shear"]`), `decode/beam.py` `slant_prior` / `slant_min`, `SLANTED` | Census: 'social' read 'socia/', 'kowalski' 'kowla/ski' on the payslips. Probe: a clean Helvetica 'l', an 'I' and a '/' produce IDENTICAL feature vectors (distance 0.0 to one another) — the deslant shears the slash upright and no feature records the lean it removed; in a sans face the lean IS the difference. The recognize stage now keeps each glyph's second-moment shear (before deslant) and the decoder compares it with the line's median shear (italic lines lean as a whole): a glyph leaning right against its line by 0.15 favours '/' by 1.5 nats, one that does not is penalized the same. Payslip-avenir-1: every false slash gone, 'social' and 'kowalski' back; the invoice's dates keep their slashes. Set level it is within rounding of nothing (the false slashes are a few words per page on two faces); kept for the pages it fixes, at no measured cost. |
| 📊 Legacy-gap decomposition after the calibration fixes (2026-09-08) | Paired error-type analysis vs Tesseract legacy (`compare_legacy_errors.py`), as on 2026-09-01 | broad-30, 44,668 truth chars | Ours 91.4 char, legacy 95.5; excess errors over legacy 1,951. Shares of the excess: deleted characters 37% (1,180 vs 467), shape substitutions 28% (881 vs 340), deleted spaces 16% (369 vs 66), punctuation substitutions 11%, case 9%, digits 6%; we make FEWER inserted characters and spaces than legacy (−5% each). Reading: over half the remaining gap is characters that vanish — touching pairs read as one letter and words fused — the chopper's territory; the shape substitutions are the classifier's. The paired table: we win 3 of 30 pages and are within 5 char points on 18; the worst pages (8588: 89.9 char but 56.2 word) read 90 char with one error per word. |
| 🔬 Word-level chop ranked by aspect deviation — NEGATIVE, stays opt-in | Smith 2007 §4.1 word-level trigger, with a different choice of blob; this project's class-aspect table | `recognize/chop.py` `rank_by` ("aspect"), `require_better` off; `scripts/eval_pages.py` PIPELINE | The legacy-gap decomposition puts 37% of the excess in deleted characters and 16% in deleted spaces — touching pairs read as one letter. The word-level chop stage existed and measured inert. Probing 'Depatment' on a payslip: the stage chopped the word's two WORST-matching blobs, 'D' and 'a', and never the 'rt' blob, which reads 't' at distance 10 — a touching pair matches its single-letter reading well, so distance cannot point at it; its BOX can. Ranking the blobs by deviation from the top-1 class's trained aspect finds the 'rt' first; with Smith's undo rule off (the decoder now judges with absolute glyph quality and the lexicon), 'Department' reads. In the evaluation pipeline for the first time, with the undo rule off: dev-8 88.8 → 88.7 word, legal-8 79.6 → 79.4, broad-30 81.4 → 81.3, modern 72.3 → 71.8 — negative everywhere. With the undo rule ON the 'rt' cut is undone (its pieces, each carrying a serif stub, read worse than the whole 't') and the stage is inert, as before. Diagnosis holds from the lattice experiment: the pieces of a touching pair are not glyphs the whole-glyph classifier knows, so neither ranking nor the decoder's absolute quality can tell a right cut from a wrong one by their distances. What would work is a PIECE-AWARE scorer trained on labelled touching pairs — the print-and-scan loop's harvest — not a class per pair (a pair class multiplies confusions in nearest-prototype space, as the ligature classes did). The aspect ranking stays as the stage's default; the stage stays out of the pipeline. |
| 🔬 Short lines take the page's x-height — NEGATIVE, reverted | Per-line x-height estimation with a page-level prior (Tesseract fits x-height per line and falls back to the block's) | `decode/beam.py` `_line_x_height` (short-line branch) | Business census: the invoices' 'Qty' header read 'ots' on all eight, with 'Q', 't', 'y' at rank 1 by wide margins. A three-glyph line cannot be bimodal on its own glyphs ('y' alone in the low cluster fails the two-member rule) and its median ascent, 26 px against the page's 21.6, just misses the caps rule's 1.25 — so the line kept its cap height as x-height and the height prior turned 'Q' into 'o' and 'y' into 's'. A line of fewer than five glyphs, unimodal, whose median ascent lies within 0.8–1.6 of the page's x-height now takes the page's: table headers and labels are the page's type, not their own. 'Qty' reads on all eight invoices — and the sets say no: dev-8 88.8 → 88.7 word, legal-8 79.6 → 79.5, broad-30 flat, modern 72.3 → 72.1 (precision −0.3). Other short lines are NOT the page's type often enough (amounts, codes, captions) and the mis-scaled priors cost more than the headers gain. Reverted; the header case waits for a per-block type-size estimate. |
| 🔬 '@' named by geometry — NEGATIVE, reverted | Untrained-mark naming by shape (this project's '\|' and bullet rules); the '@' class itself measured negative (2026-09-03: it stole from a/e/0/O) | `decode/beam.py` `_at_sign_index` (standalone post-pass); `recognize/stage.py` publishes `g["holes"]` | Business census: 'billing@example.com' missed on all eight invoices, read 'biling(bexample.com': the '@' is a 32×31-px blob no class matches (best 'œ' at 80) that the chopper splits into '(' and junk. Inside a word, a roughly square glyph 1.3–1.6 x-heights tall with a hole whose best class distance is over 50 is an '@' — judged on the glyph's group whether the decoder read it whole or in pieces (the pieces' union box finds the group). Invoice-helvetica-0 reads the address — and the sets say no, everywhere: dev-8 88.8 → 88.5 word, legal-8 79.6 → 79.4, broad-30 81.4 → 81.2, modern 72.3 → 71.8. A square holed glyph that no class matches well is, on real pages, a degraded 'e', 'a', 'o' or 'g' far more often than an '@'; the geometric signature is not specific enough. Reverted (the second '@' negative; the first was a trained class). The address stays a known miss. |
| 🔬 A parenthesised label joins its number — NEGATIVE, reverted | Numeric token grammar (this project's numeric-join rules; forms-OCR field patterns) | `decode/beam.py` `_segment_line` (`paren_label`) | Business census: '401(k)' read '401 (k)' on six of eight payslips — an 11-px gap before the '(' at x-height 29 is 0.38 x-heights, just over the definite-space threshold, and the numeric-join rule never saw it: '(' is not a separator, and the context gate admits only data lines while '401(k) contribution' is prose. A digit followed by '(' joined regardless of the line's context: the payslips read '401(k)' — and the scan sets lost, dev-8 88.8 → 88.7 word, legal-8 79.6 → 79.3, broad-30 81.4 → 81.3 (footnote markers and citations that ARE spaced). Reverted: the fourth business-template fix of the day to fail the general sets. |
| 🔬 A gap band with one outlying gap — NEGATIVE, reverted | Word-gap estimation from a line's own gap distribution (2-means, this project's gap band) and its failure mode on lines with one outlying gap | `decode/beam.py` `_segment_line` (`band_max_join_frac` 0.55) | The paired table's worst page (8588, an order form: 89.9 char but 56.2 word against legacy's 95.1) read 'x' glued to its digits on every one of twenty '3 x 5 (lined)' lines. The line's gaps are 5–22 px plus one 276-px gap to the price column; the 2-means band clustered everything under 22 px as 'joined' on a 34-px x-height, and the x-height ratios never got a say. The band's lower edge may not exceed 0.55 x-heights — no intra-word gap is half a letter high — else the ratios rule. Six variants later the rule is: a band whose word-gap cluster has fewer than two members is degenerate and yields to the x-height ratios. Page 8588: recall 57 → 79.9, precision 76 → 86.5, the largest single-page gain of the week. The cost: a two-word line has one word gap too, and on a tightly kerned prose page (8528) the ratios call 12–14-px kerning gaps spaces (precision 84 → 71). Variants tried and dropped: a cap on the band's lower edge (0.55 x-heights — 8528's kerning and 8588's word gaps sit at the same ratio), reclustering without the outlying gap (shredded 8528's caps line), trusting narrow bands with several gaps above (shredded prose elsewhere), and a wider uncertain zone for degenerate bands ('x' is not lexicon-endorsed, so '3 x 5' stayed joined). Sets: dev-8 88.8 → 86.9 word (precision 92.8 → 91.2), legal-8 79.6 → 79.2 (recall +1.6, precision −0.9), broad-30 81.4 → 81.5 (recall 86.2 → 87.7, precision 89.1 → 88.3), modern 72.3 → 72.1. Recall up, precision down more; reverted. The order-form page stays a known miss: separating a lone column gap from a two-word line needs layout knowledge (the column) that the segmenter does not have. |
| 🔬 Wider legal harvest (300 pages) — neutral, not adopted | Training-set widening (the harvest flywheel rows) | `scripts/harvest_glyphs.py data/unlv/legal.3B --pages 300`, variant build in the scratchpad | The live legal harvest is 100 pages (45,594 exemplars); legal.3B has 300, of which the evaluation draws exclude 60. Harvesting all 240 gives 140,186 legal exemplars against 86,730 letters. Rebuilt and measured: legal-8 79.6 → 79.8 word but char 91.3 → 90.9, dev-8 88.8 → 88.5, broad-30 81.4 → 81.2, modern 72.3 → 72.1. Reading: the condensation keeps 90 prototypes per class from the whole pool, so tripling one source's share moves the k-means centres toward typewriter shapes and the proportional sets pay. More data of one kind is not more coverage; a per-source quota in condensation would be the way to add it (next). |
| 🔬 Condensation with per-source quotas — mixed, kept as an option | Stratified prototype selection; Hart's condensation applied per stratum rather than to the pooled set | `recognize/condense.py` `tag_quotas`, `condense(by_tag=True)`; `build_prototypes.py --condense-by-tag` | Motivated by the 300-page legal harvest: plain k-means over the pooled exemplars let the tripled source move every class's centres. With quotas, each class's 90 centres are split across its exemplar sources (font families, the harvests, the truth set) in proportion to the square root of their counts — nine times the exemplars earns three times the centres — with a floor of one, then k-means runs within each source. Two variants measured. Live harvests by tag: dev-8 flat, legal-8 flat, broad-30 81.4 → 81.1 word, modern 72.3 → 71.2 — the quotas take centres from the harvest sources that carry the modern pages. 300-page legal harvest by tag: legal-8 91.3/79.6 → 91.7/80.1 (the best legal figure yet), dev-8 and modern flat, broad-30 81.4 → 81.1. So the quotas do what they should — the wider legal harvest now helps legal without hurting modern — but the headline set pays 0.3. Not adopted; `--condense-by-tag` stays for the day a legal-heavy deployment wants it. Lesson: the pooled k-means is tuned to the current source mix by accident, and any change of mix, quota or not, moves some set. |
| ✅ Small type wants resolution: Federal Register at 400 dpi | Resolution requirements of feature OCR (Tesseract's guidance of ≥ 20 px x-height; Rice, Nagy & Nartker's UNLV studies on dpi vs accuracy) | `scripts/make_modern_set.py` (`DPI`), sets `data/modern_fr400`, `data/modern_fr_up400` | Three quarters of the modern set's missed words (2,468 of 3,331) are the twelve Federal Register pages: 8-pt three-column text whose x-height is 12 px at 300 dpi. The same pages rendered at 400 dpi through the same print model: 64.2 / 53.3 / 82.0 / 83.3 → 65.2 / 57.0 / 87.5 / 88.8 (char/word/recall/precision) — recall and precision +5.5 each; char accuracy barely moves because it is order-sensitive and these are three-column pages. Control: the same 300-dpi scans bicubically upscaled to 400 and tagged 400 dpi (no new information): 63.3 / 52.6 / 79.5 / 83.9 — WORSE than the originals (recall −2.5). The gain is information, not processing resolution: at 12 px x-height the hairlines are already gone from the image, and interpolation cannot restore them. Consequence: no internal magnify stage; the remedy for small type is at the scanner (400 dpi for 8-pt text), and the roadmap's 'FR small-type recall' item closes as a capture requirement rather than an algorithm. Legacy Tesseract's recall lead on these pages (92.0) is consistent: its classifier was trained on far more degraded small-type samples than our 29-face stock. |
| ✅ Outline evidence in float32 with precomputed segment geometry | Numerical-kernel practice; Tesseract's `intmatcher` keeps its prototype geometry pre-tabulated in integers for the same reason | `recognize/outline.py` `_SegmentBank` (`evidence()` kept as the readable reference; tested equal to 1e-6) | Profile of a business letter (2026-09-05): 20.6 s, recognize 12.8 s, of which `outline.evidence` 9.5 s over 2,820 calls — 70 features × 7,440 segments per gated glyph, all in float64 with (N, M, 2) intermediates and a complex exponential for the angle wrap. Batching classes into one call bought 7%. The same arithmetic on flat 2-D float32 arrays with per-segment geometry (A, AB, \|AB\|², θ) precomputed once per class: 5.8× on the kernel; page 20.6 → 14.1 s, recognize 12.8 → 5.9 s, output text byte-identical. Tried and dropped: partial partition instead of a full column sort in the rating (no gain; the sort was not the cost). Two more exact rewrites the same day: the rulings stage's line openings as run-length tests (identical to scipy's opening, 17×: 1.69 → 0.34 s) and deskew's profile variance from projected ink coordinates instead of 43 image rotations (same angle on 16/16 pages: 0.82 → 0.22 s); language models kept cached across passes. The letter: 20.6 → 11.6 s, text byte-identical throughout. Remaining recognizer profile: evidence 2.4 s (the pair count itself), rating 1.9 s, matcher load 0.3 s. Lever (b), configurations per class 31 → 12 by greedy max-min coverage over the class's own renders (`OutlineMatcher.condense`, `build_outline_protos.py --condense=12 --min-cover=0.85`): recognizer 7.2 → 4.0 s, the letter under 10 s (9.9); accuracy dev-8 identical, legal-8 +0.2 char / +0.2 word, broad-30 +0.1 / +0.1, modern flat (recall +0.1). Adopted: the live outline model is now the condensed one (1,225 configurations). |
| ✅ Engine profiles: classic / pure / neural (2026-09-09) | — | `configs/classic.toml`, `pure.toml`, `neural.toml`; `scripts/eval_pages.py` `load_pipeline`/`run_stages`/`--config`; `tests/test_profiles.py` | One codebase, three presets, because every network enters as a gated additive term and the other stages are shared. classic = the 2026-09-08 engine (MLP + char GRU); pure = classic with both off (`mlp_path=""`, `char_lm=""` — the GRU guard needed fixing: `Path("")` is the working directory and exists); neural = classic plus each heavier network as adopted. Every evaluation script takes `--config` and builds the same stage list as the CLI. Pure measured: dev-8 94.8/87.6, broad-30 90.9/78.3 (recall 82.9, precision 86.1), legal-8 90.3/75.8, modern 83.8/69.6 (85.8/87.0) against classic 95.1/88.8, 91.7/81.4, 91.3/79.6, 84.7/72.3 — the two light networks are worth about three word points on the headline set and four on the typewriter set. classic never gains a network term; after every neural adoption a classic four-set run must reproduce its row — first run 2026-09-09 after the sequence scorer landed: broad-30 91.7/81.4 (86.2/89.1) to the decimal; dev-8 95.1/88.7, legal-8 91.3/79.4 and modern 84.6/72.1 (89.4/90.4) against scoreboard entries of 88.8, 79.6 and 84.7/72.3 (89.7/90.7), which the previous commit reproduces exactly in a clean worktree (and two Python hash seeds agree), so the entries were stale by a tenth or two and are corrected, not the engine. |
| 📊 Offline rerank harness for the sequence scorer (2026-09-09) | n-best rescoring evaluation as in speech recognition (oracle vs rerank) | `scripts/seq_rerank_eval.py` (`dump` / `score`), `decode/beam.py` `_nbest_sink` | One pipeline pass dumps, per output word that aligns to a truth word, every segmentation variant with its decoder score, the truth, and the word's strip; any model and weight is then scored in seconds. dev-8 dump: 1,466 aligned words, 75 decoded wrong, 2.2 variants per word. The ORACLE (truth among the variants) is 95.4% against the decoder's 94.6% on all aligned words — 0.8 points of headroom for per-word rescoring; on split/merge readings 86.1% vs 72.2% (n=72), on length-mismatched words 56% vs 8% (n=25). The rest of dev-8's errors are words whose boundaries never aligned (fused or shredded words) and variants the enumeration never offered — the next two levers: rescoring the word-gap variants (deleted spaces, 16% of the excess) and widening the split suspects once a scorer can rank them (`split_width_factor` 1.3, `max_split_variants` 8). |
| ✅ Sequence scorer, synthetic-only model: rerank and gated string injection (2026-09-09) | As above; string injection is n-best augmentation by a second recognizer (ROVER-style combination, Fiscus 1997, reduced to one gated candidate) | `decode/beam.py` `seq_inject`, `seq_inject_margin`, `seq_inject_endorsed`, `_seq_rescore` | A 4-epoch model trained on the 250k synthetic windows ALONE (26 min on MPS, no real strips, Verdana/Tahoma held out) — the first pipeline numbers, at seq_weight 0.5. Offline harness on dev-8 first: rerank lifts aligned words 94.6 → 95.2 (oracle 95.4), split/merge readings 72.2 → 83.3, length-mismatched words 8 → 52%; the model's own greedy read is 93.3% right on aligned words and 93.0% on multi-variant words where the decoder's variants cap at 92.6% — the model can beat the enumeration, not only rank it, which is why injection was implemented. Pipeline, rescoring only (Phase A): dev-8 95.1/88.8 → 95.6/89.3, broad-30 91.7/81.4 → 92.1/82.0 (weight 1.0: 92.2/82.0, flat), legal-8 91.3/79.6 → 90.9/79.6 (the synthetic stock is thin on typewriter faces). Injection, ungated (the greedy string joins the variants with the decoder's best score when its CTC likelihood beats the best text by 3 nats): dev-8 95.5/90.4, broad-30 92.4/83.3, but legal-8 89.4/78.3 — wrong readings that out-score the decoder by 3 nats on typewriter pages. Injection gated to strings the lexicon or a numeric format endorses (`seq_inject_endorsed`): dev-8 **95.8/90.5**, broad-30 **92.9/84.3** (recall 88.7, precision 91.9), legal-8 90.8/79.3 — the gate helps on BOTH sets, so an endorsed string the image likes is a safe hypothesis and an unendorsed one is not. Synthetic page with injection: 99.8/99.0 · 99.8/99.0 · 99.5/98.1 (from 98.7/93.2 · 98.9/94.7 · 98.9/95.6). Modern (rescoring only, epoch-1 model): 84.7/72.3 → 85.3/73.2 (recall 90.7, precision 91.6). CAVEAT, found the same night: every pipeline number in this row was measured with ONE scorer forward per LINE and per-word slices of that posterior; the model is trained on windows of one to three words (≤ 512 columns) and its recurrent state does not survive a 980-column line — the greedy read of a whole line came out 'gNan', and the stronger full model, whose posteriors are sharper, injected junk ('of HMO National' → 'Nan', 'meeting,' → 'ng') and measured BELOW this weaker one (dev-8 95.2/89.1). The decoder now runs the scorer per word window, exactly as trained and as the offline harness always did; the numbers above are therefore a lower bound and the full-model row is the measurement that counts. Cost: about +3 s per dense page. Kept OFF in classic; the neural profile adopts the full model if the four sets agree. |
| ✅ Sequence scorer, full model (synthetic + real strips), per-word windows — ADOPTED in the neural profile (2026-09-09) | As above | `data/seq_en.npz` (from `scripts/train_seq.py --backend torch --epochs 12 --batch 256 --lr 3e-3`), `configs/neural.toml` | Trained on 245k synthetic windows + 31k bus.3B and 37k legal.3B truth-labeled real strips (×2), 12 epochs, 97 min on MPS at 483 s/epoch; page-disjoint held-out REAL word accuracy by greedy read 96.8% (88.3% on words the pipeline had read at the wrong length), synthetic 86.8% (75.9% with a touching pair). Harness on dev-8: the model's own greedy read is 97.5% on aligned words against the classic decoder's 94.6% and the enumeration's 95.4% oracle, 92% on length-mismatched words (decoder 8%), 96.8% on multi-variant words (decoder 90.1%). Pipeline, scorer run per WORD window (the line-posterior defect fixed), weight 0.5, injection gated to endorsed strings: dev-8 95.1/88.8 → **95.8/90.6**, broad-30 91.7/81.4 → **92.8/85.0** (recall 86.2 → 89.6, precision 89.1 → 92.6), legal-8 91.3/79.6 → **91.3/81.0**, modern 84.7/72.3 → **86.0/76.3** (recall 89.7 → 93.6, precision 90.7 → 94.7; by kind: letters 99.4/96.3, invoices 92.3/87.7, payslips 74.2/70.4, bills 93.0/74.6, Federal Register 67.3/62.4). Up on every figure of every set — the largest word-accuracy gain in the project's record, and it lands where the decomposition said the gap was (touching pairs, fused words). Ungated injection: dev-8 96.0/91.0, broad-30 92.6/85.3, legal-8 91.8/81.2, modern 85.7/76.0 — two or three tenths better on the UNLV word figures, worse on modern and on broad-30 char; the gate is adopted as the rule that wins the set with real modern faces. Two lessons: (1) what the model sees at inference must be what it saw in training — a line is not a window; (2) the offline harness predicted the pipeline gain correctly only once the pipeline cut windows the way the harness did. Classic regression guard re-run after adoption (see DESIGN §8). |
| 📊 Legacy-gap decomposition and census under the neural profile (2026-09-09) | As the 2026-09-08 decomposition and the missing-word census | `scripts/compare_legacy_errors.py --config configs/neural.toml`, `scripts/missing_words.py --config` | broad-30, 44,668 truth chars: ours 92.8 char, legacy 95.5; excess 1,303 (from 1,951). Shape substitutions 881 → 596 (share 28% → 20%), case 152, punctuation 124, digits 94; we now make FEWER inserted characters (275 vs 484), spaces and punctuation than legacy. What did NOT move: deleted characters 1,180 → 1,141 (52% of the excess) and deleted spaces 369 → 362 (23%) — three quarters of the remaining gap is characters and spaces that vanish. Census on the same pages: missing words 1,300 → 802, spurious words 1,072 → 507 (the scorer's largest visible effect is junk it no longer emits), suppressed lines 113 → 135. Top missing words are unchanged: '5', 'x', '3', '(lined)' from the order form's "3 x 5 (lined)" rows (template page 8588), then short function words ('and', 'a', 'the', 'to', 'of', 11 each) and proper nouns the lexicon cannot endorse ('usaa', 'rice', 'las'). Reading: the scorer fixed what it can see — a word window it is offered — and cannot restore a word that never became a window: fused words, words in suppressed or unsegmented lines, and short words lost at line boundaries. |
| ✅ Sequence scorer, Phase B: the beam's n-best rescored by the word window — ADOPTED; Phase C: gap variants — inert, opt-in (2026-09-09) | n-best rescoring, as above | `decode/beam.py` `seq_beam_weight`, `seq_beam_top`, `seq_gap_weight`, `_seq_window`, `_seq_costs` | Phase B: inside one segmentation, the top-8 beam strings are re-scored by −seq_beam_weight × (CTC nll − min) BEFORE the lexicon pass, so a lexicon-endorsed near-tie the image rejects cannot displace the pixel-best reading, and a shape substitution the language model liked pays for its pixels. dev-8 95.8/90.6 → 96.0/91.2 at weight 0.5, **96.1/91.3** at 1.0; at 1.0 on the four sets: broad-30 92.8/85.0 → **93.3/86.2** (recall 89.6 → 90.1, precision 92.6 → 93.4), legal-8 91.3/81.0 → **92.0/83.7**, modern 86.0/76.3 → **86.3/77.3** (94.3/95.5). Up on every figure of every set; adopted at 1.0. Phase C: the word-gap variants (which uncertain gaps are spaces) scored by one likelihood over the whole segment's window with the space class, entering the PRIMARY key at 0.1 and 0.2 lexicon units per nat: dev-8 90.5 word at both (−0.1) — inert, because the admissibility rule (every part endorsed) already decides these cases and a line fused into one string has no UNCERTAIN gaps to vote on; that case is the segment re-read's (next row). Kept opt-in at 0. |
| 🔬 Federal Register training strips by truth alignment — NEGATIVE yield, stopped; small-type synthetic set instead (2026-09-09) | Truth-aligned harvest (above); synthetic small type per `factory/words.py` | `scripts/make_modern_train.py` (pages 41–160 of the issue, far from the test's 9–20, through the modern set's print model), `scripts/harvest_lines.py --no-guard`, `scripts/make_seq_data.py --x-heights --x-weights` | The modern set's largest loss is the Federal Register (12 pages at 67 char / 62 word), so 120 other pages of the same issue were rendered for training. The line harvest labels a word only when both of its boundaries align to truth spaces, and on 8-pt three-column pages the classic pipeline reads at 67% char, so the harvest yielded five or six words per page (about 700 in all) at a minute a page — stopped after 40 pages as not worth the machine time; the pages stay under `data/modern_train` for a harvest that can align by the PDF's own word boxes later. In its place, 100k synthetic windows at x-heights 9–13 px (weights 1:2:2:2:1) from `data/corpus_en_modern` through the same renderer, 44% with a touching pair, 4 min — `data/seq_synth_small_v1.npz`, folded into the second model (`seq_en_v2`: both synthetic sets + the two UNLV harvests, hidden 128, 12 epochs). |
| ✅ Segment re-read by the sequence scorer — ADOPTED (2026-09-09) | Recognizer-driven re-segmentation: the reading of a whole segment, with spaces, placed back on the image by the emission frames of its characters (CTC alignment as a segmentation, Graves 2006) | `decode/beam.py` `seq_reread`, `_seq_reread`; `recognize/ctc.py` `greedy_decode_frames` | Motivated by the deletion probe: of broad-30's deleted characters, 218 sit inside matched lines and 2,177 in truth lines with no matching output line — lines that EXIST but were fused into one string ('wherevouwantl.t'completer.ththeblankshoryoutoR.lli') or set in a letterhead face outside the stock, so that no word window was ever offered at the right span. When a segment's decoded words are mostly unendorsed (endorsed fraction < 0.5) and long enough (≥ 8 chars), the scorer reads the whole segment window greedily; the reading is split at its space emissions, each word's page span comes from the frames of its first and last character (2 px a frame through the strip scale), every group joins the word whose span holds its centre, and the reading replaces the decoder's words only if it endorses MORE of them. Page 8753: 'andseloct56operators' → 'and select 56 operators', 'itspowe"leafle-boicongine' → 'its powerful Maple symbolic engine', two dropped lines back, +58 words right on the page. Over the profile before Phase B: broad-30 92.8/85.0 → 93.0/85.4 (recall 89.6 → 90.3), modern 86.0/76.3 → 86.0/76.5, legal-8 91.3/81.0 → 91.2/81.0, dev-8 95.8/90.6 → 96.0/90.6. On top of the adopted Phase B: dev-8 96.1/91.3 → **96.2/91.3** (recall +0.3), broad-30 93.3/86.2 → **93.4/86.5** (recall 90.1 → 90.7), legal-8 92.0/83.7 → **91.9/83.8**, modern 86.3/77.3 → **86.3/77.5** (94.5/95.6). Word, recall and precision up on every set; char flat or up on three and one tenth down on the typewriter set — adopted, with that tenth recorded. The remaining unmatched lines are the ones the re-read cannot endorse: foreign text (the French letterhead of 8559), proper nouns and addresses, and faces the model has never seen. |
| 🔬 Second sequence model, wider and longer with small type (`seq_en_v2`) — MIXED, not adopted (2026-09-09) | As above | `data/seq_en_v2.npz` (`train_seq.py --synth data/seq_synth_v1.npz data/seq_synth_small_v1.npz --hidden 128 --epochs 12`) | 479k windows (245k general + 100k small-type synthetic + the two UNLV harvests ×2), hidden 128 (about 480k parameters), 129 min on MPS. Held-out real words 96.9% (v1 96.8), hard subset 93.8% (88.3), synthetic 87.0% (86.8). Harness on dev-8: greedy read 98.0% of aligned words (v1 97.5), 96% of length-mismatched (92), 97.9% of multi-variant (96.8). Pipeline under the adopted profile (rerank + gated injection + Phase B + re-read): dev-8 96.2/91.3 → 96.5/91.4, broad-30 93.4/86.5 → 93.6/86.7, but legal-8 91.9/83.8 → 92.3/83.0 (char +0.4, word −0.8) and modern 86.3/77.5 → 86.2/77.1 (bills 76.8 → 75.8). Loses on two sets; stays a variant file. The small-type windows did nothing for the Federal Register pages (67.5 → 67.6 char): whatever limits them is not the model's reading of 8-pt type in isolation — the three-column layout, the line x-height on mixed footnote lines, and the re-read gate's dependence on lexicon endorsement (agency names, CFR citations) are the suspects for a census of those twelve pages. |
| 📊 Sequence scorer runtime: numpy for one window at a time (2026-09-09) | — | `recognize/seq.py` `load_scorer`, `decode/beam.py` `seq_backend` | A letter page: classic 12.8 s, neural 17.9 s on the idle machine. Profile: 542 word-window forwards through torch/MPS at 10.9 ms each (5.9 s) and 0.8 s of CTC arithmetic; the same page through the numpy reference 17.2 s against torch's 22.0 s under equal load. A single 32 x 180 window is launch-bound on the GPU, so `auto` now means numpy for inference; `torch` stays selectable and pays for training and for batched offline scoring. With numpy as the default the same page reads in 14.2 s against classic's 12.7 s on the idle machine: the three terms cost 1.5 s, and batching a line's windows before decoding would recover a second at most — not worth a mechanism; recorded instead of built. |
| 🔬 Unendorsed string injection at a 6-nat margin — MIXED, gate kept (2026-09-09) | As the injection row | `decode/beam.py` `seq_inject_endorsed=false`, `seq_inject_margin=6.0` | On the adopted profile (rerank, gated injection, Phase B, re-read): dev-8 96.2/91.3 → 96.2/91.2, legal-8 91.9/83.8 → 92.5/83.9, broad-30 93.4/86.5 → 93.1/86.6, modern 86.3/77.5 → 85.9/77.2. A wider margin than the 3 nats measured earlier does not change the picture: an unendorsed string the image likes wins on the typewriter set and loses on the modern set and on headline char. The lexicon gate stays; the words it withholds (proper nouns, addresses, foreign text) are the re-read's remaining gap too, and want a mechanism that can vouch for a string without the lexicon — a per-document word list built from confident reads, the classic adaptation idea at word level. |
| 📊 The Federal Register "loss" is the truth's reading order, not recognition (2026-09-09) | Evaluation ground truth from a PDF text layer: content-stream order vs visual reading order (poppler's pdftotext implements the latter) | `scripts/make_modern_set.py` `pdf_page_text`, `--truth-only` | Census of the twelve Federal Register pages under the neural profile: bag-of-words recall 91–95% and precision 93–97% on every page, sequence word accuracy 47–85%. Legacy Tesseract, run on the same twelve pages, shows the SAME pattern page for page (p011 50.9/50.3 vs ours 50.5/46.8; p016 90.8/87.8 vs 90.5/85.1; recall 95–98%). A gap that size between bag and sequence with two independent engines is the truth's order: pypdf's `extract_text` returns the content stream's order, which on three-column pages is not the order a reader follows. Scored against poppler's reading-order extraction, our page 11 output reads **91.5%** word accuracy (46.8% before), page 16 86.2% (85.1%); `-layout` mode interleaves the columns row by row (7.5%) and `-raw` is the stream order again (45.5%). The bills gain too (p3 of HR 500: 78.1 → 85.1; p2 of HR 1500: 73.5 → 79.0). Consequences: (1) the Federal Register was never the modern set's largest loss, and the small-type training data's null result on it was expected; (2) every PDF page's truth is rebuilt in visual order (`--truth-only`), which re-baselines the modern figures of classic, neural and legacy alike — re-measured below; (3) what the census did find on those pages that is recognition: '§' is not in the class set and comes out as a stray 's' (120 missing, 69 spurious), the running header's slashes fuse three tokens on every page ('register/vol.', '52/friday,', '2024/rule5'), hyphenated words break at the hyphen in 8-pt type ('third-party' → 'third-py'), and 'FMU' in small caps loses its F. |
| ✅ Per-document word list from the sequence scorer — ADOPTED (2026-09-09) | Per-document adaptation at word level: the cipher-solving argument (repetition on one page is evidence the lexicon cannot give) applied to the scorer's readings; cf. the cluster-refit glyph adaptation | `decode/beam.py` `doc_words`, `doc_words_min_len`, `doc_words_min_count`, `_collect_doc_words`, `_endorsed`; word records carry `seq_greedy` and `seq_agree` | Every gate that admits a scorer reading (injection, the re-read, the gap admissibility rule) asks the lexicon or a numeric format; proper nouns, addresses and foreign text fail all three and were most of what the re-read could not fix. In pass 1 every word window is read by the scorer and the reading stored on the word; pass 2 builds the page's list: readings that recur (two distinct words) or agree with the decoder, at least three letters, alphabetic, unknown to the lexicon — and treats them as endorsed. Two false starts recorded: agreement alone is circular (the words that need vouching are the ones the decoder never reads right — a page with 'usaa' six times listed only 'danise'); and the reading was stored only for multi-variant words, so most words never counted. Fixed: dev-8 96.2/91.3 → **96.2/91.4**, legal-8 91.9/83.8 → **92.0/84.3**, broad-30 93.4/86.5 → **93.4/86.8** (recall 90.7 → 91.0), modern 86.3/77.5 → **86.3/77.7** (old truth). Up on word on every set, char flat or up; adopted. The agreement-only version measured a tenth or two behind on each set. The re-read now also applies to each run of unendorsed words inside a segment (a junk 'SAHCAAVE' between an endorsed street number and state), measured flat on dev-8 on its own. |
| 📊 Seed variance of the sequence scorer (2026-09-10) | Run-to-run variance as the yardstick for model comparisons | `data/seq_en_v1s1.npz`, `data/seq_en_v1s2.npz` (`train_seq.py --seed 1/2`, same recipe as the adopted model) | Two more seeds of the adopted recipe, 97 min each, under the full neural profile (char / word): dev-8 96.2/91.4 · 96.5/91.8 · 96.5/91.7; broad-30 93.4/86.8 · 93.5/87.0 · 93.5/86.8; legal-8 92.0/84.3 · 91.9/83.9 · 92.1/83.3; modern 90.1/84.6 · 90.0/83.9 · 90.0/84.5. Word-accuracy range across seeds: 0.2 on broad-30, 0.4 on dev-8, 0.7 on modern, 1.0 on legal-8 (eight typewriter pages). On the dev-8 harness the three greedy reads are 97.5 / 97.4 / 97.4 on aligned words. Consequences: the second model's "mixed" result (broad-30 +0.2, legal-8 −0.8, modern −0.4) was inside this noise, neither better nor worse; a NEW MODEL is adopted only when it clears about 0.5 word on broad-30 and 1.0 on legal-8 and modern, or wins with two seeds. Changes to the decoder's TERMS with one fixed model are paired comparisons on identical pages and are not subject to this noise, but their tenths generalize with the same uncertainty. The adopted seed stays live; the two others are kept as variant files. |
| ✅ Calibrated word confidence and a reject path (2026-09-10) | Platt (1999) calibration by logistic regression; Niculescu-Mizil & Caruana (2005) on margins vs probabilities; coverage/accuracy curves as in OCR reject-option work (Chow 1970) | `decode/wordconf.py`, `scripts/harvest_word_conf.py`, `scripts/train_wordconf.py`, `decode/beam.py` `conf_path`/`review_below`, `decode/output.py` `review_words` | A production engine must say which words to route to a person. The beam's margin orders words but is not a probability (held-out Brier 0.064). Evidence per word, all already computed by the decoder: beam margin, lexicon / numeric / document-list endorsement, the scorer's likelihood of the chosen text per character and its margin over the next variant, agreement with the scorer's own read, injected and re-read flags, length and character mix. Labels: 24,822 words from 120 non-evaluation UNLV pages under the neural profile, aligned to truth by word span; an output word in a matched line that no truth span covers is labelled wrong — the first fit, on aligned words only, saw a 98%-right population and could not learn the reject case (recorded). Page-disjoint holdout (24 pages): Brier **0.039** (beam margin 0.064, constant 0.084); reliability within a few points in every bin with enough words; coverage: keep p ≥ 0.9 → 89% of words kept at 96.6% right (92.5% base), p ≥ 0.98 → 58% kept at 98.1%. On dev-8 end to end: keep p ≥ 0.9 → 91.4% kept at **98.0%** (base 94.4%), the flagged 8.6% right 57% of the time. Largest weight: an INJECTED reading (−2.18 z-scored) — the scorer's own strings, net positive on the four sets, are the least reliable words individually, which is exactly what a reject path should know. Adopted in the neural profile (`p_correct` on every word, `review_words` in the run summary); the garbage gate keeps the beam-margin `confidence` it was tuned on. Not seen by the calibrator: words in lines that match no truth line at all (whole junk lines), which stay the garbage gate's. |
| 🔬 Long-window sequence model (`seq_en_v3`, fine-tuned on 2–6-word windows up to 1,400 columns) — within seed noise, not adopted (2026-09-11) | Fine-tuning from the adopted weights; longer CTC windows so the scorer can read a whole line | `data/seq_en_v3.npz` (`train_seq.py --init data/seq_en.npz --synth data/seq_synth_long_v1.npz data/seq_synth_v1.npz --epochs 4 --lr 1e-3`), `make_seq_data.py --words 3 8 --take 2 6 --max-width 1400` | 150k long windows (mean 338 columns, 27 characters) plus the original sets, 528k windows an epoch at 18 min, four epochs from the adopted weights; held-out real words 96.7% (adopted 96.8%), synthetic 86.5% with the long windows now in the holdout. Under the full neural profile: dev-8 96.2/91.4 → 96.1/91.5, broad-30 93.4/86.8 → 93.6/87.1, legal-8 92.0/84.3 → 91.8/83.7, modern 90.1/84.6 → 90.0/84.5 — every difference inside the seed range measured the day before (0.2 / 0.4 / 1.0 / 0.7). The epoch-1 checkpoint read broad-30 at 93.8/87.1, also noise. The model now reads whole lines without collapsing (its purpose), but reading whole lines did not by itself move the four sets: kept as the model the line read (next row) requires; the adopted seed stays live. |
| 🔬 Line-level read by the sequence scorer — inert on the letters, negative on typewriter; opt-in (2026-09-11) | The scorer as a hypothesis source for whole lines: CTC likelihood of the decoder's line text against the scorer's own reading, placed back on the groups by emission frame | `decode/beam.py` `seq_line_read`, `seq_line_margin`, `_seq_reread_span(mode="line")` | With the long-window model: the scorer reads the whole line window; its reading replaces the line's words when its likelihood beats the decoder's line text by seq_line_margin nats per character AND it endorses strictly more words (with "not fewer" it swapped right proper nouns for wrong ones at equal count: broad-30 char −0.4..−0.6 at every margin 0.5/1.0/2.0 for word +0.1..+0.2). Strict, margin 1.0, on the four sets against the same model without it: dev-8 96.1/91.5 → 96.1/91.5, broad-30 93.6/87.1 → 93.6/87.2, legal-8 91.8/83.7 → **90.9/84.0**, modern 90.0/84.5 → 90.0/84.5. Why it did not pay: the lines it was built for are letterheads in faces the scorer has never seen and foreign text, and the scorer's reading of those is not better than the decoder's — the deletion probe's 2,177 unmatched-line characters are not recoverable by a better READER of the same pixels; they want faces in the training stock (letterhead grotesques) or real scans of such documents. On typewriter pages the whole-line reading loses char where fixed-pitch spacing had been right. Kept opt-in at 0 with the long-window model as its variant file. |
| ✅ Unruled tables of one-line cells read row by row (2026-09-11) | T-Recs (Kieninger & Dengel 1998) column clustering, applied to one-line blocks; extends the row alignment of column blocks (`layout/rows.py`, 2026-09-07) | `layout/rows.py` `_single_cell_tables`, `row_groups` | The payslips read at 74 / 72 with recall and precision at 98.6%: every cell of the pay table was its own one-line block, the reading order emitted the table column by column, and the row mechanism needed blocks of three lines to align, so nothing fired. Now cells that share a baseline form a row, rows whose cells fall into left-edge columns recurring in three or more rows form a table, and a table is a CONTIGUOUS run of such rows. The first version, without the contiguity rule and at two rows, paired a bill's margin line numbers with its short headings into a "table" that swallowed the page: classic modern 88.2/78.9 → 76.9/66.7 (bills −12 to −56 word a page, invoices −30) while the payslip gained 33 — recorded, and the reason the shared-stage guard exists. Tightened: invoice and bill pages unchanged to the decimal, the Arial Narrow payslip 35.6 → 79.5 word (what remains there is the template's column-major header against a row-wise read, and a five-word line too long for a cell). Modern: neural 90.1/84.6 → **91.6/86.0**, classic 88.2/78.9 → **89.7/80.2**, payslips under neural 74.4/71.6 → 86.2/82.5; dev-8, broad-30 and legal-8 identical to the decimal under both profiles (the eager version was already identical there, and the tightened rule fires on a subset). |
| 🔬 '§' and '•' as glyph classes — NEGATIVE, not stocked; and what a model rebuild reproduces (2026-09-11) | Class-set extension as in the ligature and '@' experiments; a control rebuild to separate the class from the build | `factory/stock.py` `CHARSET` (unchanged), variant builds `build_prototypes.py` / `train_mlp.py` / `build_outline_protos.py` | Every stock face has both glyphs; '§' comes out as a stray 's' 120 times across the Federal Register pages. Both classes: neural dev-8 96.2/91.4 → 96.3/91.9, broad-30 93.4/86.8 → 93.4/86.5, legal-8 92.0/84.3 → 92.2/83.9, modern 91.6/86.0 → 91.1/85.5 (Federal Register 90.6 → 91.3 word); classic broad-30 81.4 → 81.1, modern 89.7/80.2 → 89.3/79.6. '§' alone: neural broad-30 86.5, legal-8 84.2, modern 85.5; classic legal-8 91.3/79.4 → 90.3/78.5, modern → 89.1/78.9. CONTROL: the four models rebuilt with the ORIGINAL charset by the same commands — pool, prototypes and MLP come back bit-identical to the live files (seeded k-means and Adam), the outline set does not (52,302 segments against the live 53,087, coverage-greedy condensation from the same fonts and script; cause not settled) and that alone moves classic legal-8 91.3 → 90.8 char with word flat, broad-30 −0.1 word, modern 89.7/80.3 (flat). So half the '§' loss on legal-8 is the rebuild and none of the modern loss is: the classes cost 0.5–1.3 word on modern in both profiles for +0.7 on the twelve pages that use '§' — a rare class is a confusion magnet for the common ones ('§' against 'S', '5', 'g'; '•' against every speck), as the ligature and '@' classes were. Not stocked; the charset stays. Two consequences recorded: a class-set change must be judged against a control rebuild, and the live outline set is a specific condensation the current script does not reproduce — RESEARCH keeps its provenance, and any future outline rebuild is re-measured. |
| 🔬 Ten letterhead faces added to the stock — NEGATIVE, not stocked (2026-09-11) | Stock widening as on 2026-09-03 (modern sans, positive) and 2026-09-06 (TeX Gyre italics, mixed) | `build_prototypes.py --add-fonts`, `build_outline_protos.py --add-fonts` (variant files only) | The deletion probe put the remaining broad-30 gap in letterhead lines set in faces outside the stock, so Futura, Optima, Gill Sans, Palatino, Baskerville, Charter, Rockwell, Lucida Grande, PT Sans and Bodoni 72 were added as variants (39 faces; Helvetica, Helvetica Neue and Avenir left out because the modern set is rendered in them). Under neural, against 96.2/91.4 · 93.4/86.8 · 92.0/84.3 · 91.6/86.0: dev-8 96.2/91.4, broad-30 **93.3/86.3**, legal-8 **92.1/83.8**, modern 91.5/85.9 — and identical to a tenth with the control outline set in place of the rebuilt one, so it is the faces, not the rebuild. Ten more faces competing for the fixed 90 prototypes a class dilute the body classes, the effect the display faces had before they were routed per block (2026-09-03). The letterhead lines want the same treatment: a "letterhead" family routed to blocks that vote for it, not more exemplars in the body pool — the next mechanism for that gap, not built here. Side finding: the build warns that BigCaslon, a pinned stock face, is missing from this machine's fonts; the bit-identical control rebuild shows the live models were built without it too. |
| 🔬 A routed letterhead font family — inert to negative, plumbing kept, not stocked (2026-09-11) | Per-block font-family routing (2026-09-03, the display family) applied to letterhead faces | `factory/fonts.py` `FAMILY_HINTS["letterhead"]`, `build_prototypes.py --letterhead`, `build_outline_protos.py --letterhead` | The answer to the dilution above: letterhead faces as their own routed family, matched only by blocks that vote for them. Of the seven named, only Futura and Gill Sans passed the print gate into the 80-face pool. Under neural, against 96.2/91.4 · 93.4/86.8 · 92.0/84.3 · 91.6/86.0: dev-8 96.1/91.4, broad-30 93.4/86.7, legal-8 **91.8/83.6**, modern **91.1/85.6**, identical to a tenth with the control outline set. A block that votes for a two-face family is then matched against two faces, and the misrouted blocks on typewriter and modern pages cost more than the letterhead lines gain. The gap the deletion probe found in letterhead lines has now resisted a better reader (the line read), more faces in the body pool, and a routed family: what those lines need is not in the fonts on this machine or in the decoder. Family and flags stay for a stock that has the faces. |
| 📊 Legacy-gap decomposition at the end of the production leg (2026-09-11) | As before | `compare_legacy_errors.py --config configs/neural.toml` | broad-30, 44,668 truth chars: ours 93.4 char, legacy 95.5; excess **926** (1,951 on 2026-09-08, 1,303 on 2026-09-09 morning). Shape substitutions 466 vs legacy 340 (excess 126, 14%, from 881); deleted characters 1,054 vs 467 (**+587, 63%**); deleted spaces 320 vs 66 (+254, 27%); case 149 vs 14 (+135, 15%); punctuation +82; digits +77. We make FEWER inserted characters (266 vs 484), spaces (99 vs 184) and punctuation (72 vs 123) than legacy. Reading: the classifier side of the gap is closed to within a third of a point; what remains is (a) characters and spaces that vanish in the letterhead lines the deletion probe located, which a better reader, more faces and a routed family have all failed to recover, and (b) case, untouched since the calibration week and now the third share. |
| 🔬 Case from the scorer's reading — inert, opt-in (2026-09-11) | Case decision from whole-word context (relative letter heights) rather than per-glyph size twins | `decode/beam.py` `seq_case`, `_seq_rescore`; probe in the session record | The case share of the legacy gap (149 vs 14) probed on broad-30: nearly all are SIZE TWINS ('s/S', 'c/C', 'o/O', 'u/U', 'v/V', 'w/W', 'p/P'); 38 sit in all-caps lines read entirely lower ('USAA INVESTMENT MANAGEMENT COMPANY'), 36 are capitalised words mid-sentence read lower ('Chief Operating', 'Washington', 'Congress'), 19 go the other way ('Of', 'With' capitalised). On three pages the scorer's greedy reading had the case right in 10 of 26 case-only errors, the decoder's case in 9, something else in 7. Rule: when the reading equals the chosen word up to case, take the reading's case. Four sets under neural: dev-8 96.2/91.3 (−0.1), broad-30 93.4/86.7 (−0.1), legal-8 92.1/84.4 (+0.1), modern 91.6/86.0 (flat) — the fixes are offset one for one by the scorer's own wrong case elsewhere. Kept at 0. The caps-line share (38) is a line-level question — a line whose every letter is a capital should not be judged glyph by glyph against the page x-height — and is the next mechanism for case, not this one. |
| ✅ Case of size twins decided line by line — ADOPTED as the decoder default; first measurement CORRECTED (2026-09-11, corrected 2026-09-12) | Line-local typographic measurement (x-height and cap height from the line's own unambiguous letters) instead of a page anchor; cf. the caps-line rule of 2026-09-07 | `decode/beam.py` `_line_case_pass`, `line_case`, `line_case_ratio` | The case probe: the 149 case errors on broad-30 are size twins (c o s u v w x z), 38 in all-caps lines read entirely lower and 36 in capitalised words mid-sentence; on letterhead-heavy pages the page x-height anchor itself lands at cap height (8562: 22.4 px), so every capital twin looks lowercase. The pass judges each twin by its LINE: the line's unambiguous lowercase letters (a e m n r z) give its x-height, its unambiguous capitals (A B D E F G H I J K L M N P Q R T Y) its cap height, and the twin's own ascent picks the nearer; only WHOLE glyphs vote or are judged (a piece cut from a touching pair carries the component's height: 'child' → 'Child' five times on one page before that rule). **Correction.** The measurement first recorded here (legal-8 92.0/84.3 → 97.1/92.3 under neural, 91.3/79.4 → 96.7/90.2 under classic) was FALSE: those runs hit a crash on words without per-glyph provenance (fixed afterwards) on 3 of legal-8's 8 pages, 6–7 of broad-30's 30 and 2–5 of modern's 59, and `eval_unlv.py` averaged over the surviving pages without saying so. The evaluator now counts a crashed page as fully wrong and names the count. Honest figures, on the fixed code, on against off, all pages present (char / word): neural dev-8 96.2/91.4 → 96.2/91.5, broad-30 93.4/86.8 → 93.4/86.8, legal-8 92.0/84.3 → 92.1/84.4, modern 91.6/86.0 → 91.7/86.2; classic dev-8 95.1/88.7 → 95.2/89.2, broad-30 91.7/81.4 → 91.8/81.9, legal-8 91.3/79.4 → 91.7/81.2, modern 89.7/80.2 → 89.9/81.3. Up or flat on every set under both profiles — a real gain of half a point to two word points under classic and a tenth or two under neural, and it stays the default; but not the record-setting gain first claimed. Lesson, now in the evaluator: a mean over N pages is only a measurement when N is the number of pages asked for. |
| 📊 Legacy-gap decomposition after the line-case pass (2026-09-11) | As before | `compare_legacy_errors.py --config configs/neural.toml` | broad-30: ours 93.4 char, legacy 95.5; excess **916** (926 before the pass; 1,951 on 2026-09-08). Case 149 → 141 (the pass's gain landed on legal-8, whose pleadings are set in capitals; broad-30's remaining case errors are capitalised words mid-sentence whose first letter is a twin, many of them words the scorer injected or re-read and so without per-glyph provenance for the pass to judge). Deleted characters +590 (64%) and deleted spaces +254 (28%) are 92% of what remains and sit in the letterhead lines that three mechanisms have failed to reach; shape +119 (13%), punctuation +81, digits +77. We make 215 fewer inserted characters than legacy. |

Harness on dev-8: greedy read 97.5%, identical to the previous model. Four sets under neural against the previous scorer on the same code, all pages present (char / word): seed 0 — dev-8 96.2/91.5 → 96.2/91.5, broad-30 93.4/86.8 → **93.6/87.1**, legal-8 92.1/84.4 → 92.0/84.4, modern 91.7/86.2 → 91.7/86.3; seed 1 — dev-8 96.1/91.5, broad-30 **93.5/87.3** (precision 93.6 → 94.0), legal-8 92.2/84.3, modern 91.7/86.3. Our typewriter errors are spread one to a word (a dropped or fused character costs the word) while legacy's are concentrated in junk it emits beside the text (its precision equals ours). | ✅ Service concurrency test (2026-09-12) | Process pool behind a threading HTTP server: throughput scales with workers while a page stays the single-threaded pipeline (DESIGN §9) | `scripts/service_load.py`; `mlws_ocr/service.py` | Four workers, the eight dev-8 letters posted at once while a training job and two harvests ran on the same machine: 8 of 8 returned 200; one page alone 18.0 s, the burst 39.4 s wall (mean latency 24.8 s) — 3.7 pages a minute over 4 workers against 3.3 for one; mean character accuracy 0.961 against the truth, equal to the dev-8 neural row, so a worker reads exactly what the harness reads; two requests for one page read identically. The first request after start pays the model load in the worker (the process pool's initializer). |
| ✅ Scoring convention: line-end hyphenation folded to the joined word on BOTH sides (2026-09-12) | ISRI's truth is 'as printed' (Rice, Jenkins & Nartker 1995–96), so a wrapped word is two tokens, 'de-' and 'bates'; a reader's OCR joins them, and the decoder's dehyphenation pass does when its lexicon endorses the word; scoring one convention against the other is not scoring recognition | `scripts/eval_unlv.py` `join_line_hyphens`, `normalize(hyphens=)`, `--dump DIR` (per-page output text for offline re-scoring); `tests/test_eval_normalize.py` | Share of truth words that are wrapped halves: news-8 6.7%, mag-8 4.7%, broad-30 0.2%, dev-8, legal-8 and the modern set none by the naive count — the modern bills DO wrap, with the line number on its own line between the halves ('com-' / '7' / 'pany'), which the fold steps over. Each join the decoder made against an as-printed truth cost two word errors. Three iterations, each caught by re-running legacy Tesseract (which never joins, so the fold must leave it nearly unchanged): (1) the join must skip blank lines; (2) Tesseract writes a wrapped word's hyphen as U+2014, so dashes fold to '-' before the join looks for one (a 99.9/99.3 newspaper page had dropped to 99.6/97.8); (3) the line-number rule above (bill pages had dropped four word points for legacy); (4) the number written at the head of the continuation line instead ('Representa-' / '2 tives'), which is how pdftotext lays out the numbered bills while our output puts the number on its own line — without the rule the pure row lost 0.9 word on modern, all of it on bills. Legacy under the final fold against raw: broad-30 95.5/91.7 → 95.5/91.7, dev-8 95.0/91.7 → same, legal-8 90.4/88.7 → same, modern 75.3/70.9 → 75.2/70.4, news-8 96.4/93.6 → 96.3/93.1, mag-8 87.4/85.0 → 87.3/84.7 — the residual is a wrapped half at a column foot joined with a different partner in legacy's block order, and an em-dash before a lowercase continuation. Neural under the fold: news-8 92.8/83.1 → **93.5/87.3**, mag-8 68.3/50.7 → 68.4/51.1, dev-8 and legal-8 unchanged (96.1/91.5, 92.2/84.3); broad-30, modern and the classic and pure rows re-measured under the fold in the scoreboard (DESIGN §8). Every scoreboard row from here on is under this convention; the rows before 2026-09-12 are comparable on dev-8, legal-8 and modern and within 0.3 word on broad-30. |
| ✅ Beam: no language-model replay at zero weight (2026-09-12) | Engineering (profile-driven) | `decode/beam.py` `_beam_word_mode` | cProfile of one neural decode pass on a 256-word letter: 5.6 s, of which 2.7 s was the char-GRU `step` — 23k stateless `score` replays of up to 40 characters each, all from the DIGIT-mode beam, which runs with `lm_w = 0` and multiplied the result by zero. Skipped at zero weight; dev-8 output identical page for page (the `--dump` texts diff clean), so no measurement moves. The sequence scorer itself is 0.5 s of that pass; the remaining cost is the beam in Python, and a dense Federal Register page (11,652 glyphs, 211 lines) still spends 195 s a decode pass under neural — the throughput item for the service. |

Harness on dev-8: greedy 97.5%, identical to the live scorer; split/merge readings 90.3 → 91.7%. A legal-8 harness dump was added the same evening (`data/nbest_legal8.npz`, 1,263 aligned typewriter words; `seq_rerank_eval.py dump ... --doc-type legal`) and it names the loss before any pipeline run: greedy 98.2% live → 97.1%, and on the multi-variant words 93.8% → 86.6% — a two-second typewriter gate for every scorer from here on. Four sets against the live scorer (fold convention): dev-8 96.1/91.5 → 96.2/91.8, broad-30 93.5/87.4 → **93.9/87.8** (recall 91.5 → 92.5, precision 94.1 → 93.5), legal-8 92.2/84.3 → **91.6/83.0**, modern 91.5/85.7 → 91.2/85.4. It wins the headline set by about its seed range and loses the typewriter set by more than its bar: the real strips are a smaller share of a 921k-window epoch than of the live recipe's, and the typewriter faces live only in the real strips. The second seed was cancelled (a model that loses a set cannot be carried by two seeds) and the machine went to two fine-tunes with the three new real harvests (`seq_en_v6a` from the live scorer, `v6b` from this one; RESEARCH row to follow). Line read (`seq_line_read`, the mechanism the long windows were rendered for), with this model: dev-8 96.3/92.2 (+0.1/+0.4 over the same model without it), legal-8 91.3/83.2 (−0.3 char, +0.2 word), broad-30 93.9/87.9 (+0.1 word, precision 93.5 → 93.7) — a tenth or two everywhere, inside the seed range, so the line read stays opt-in even with a model that has seen lines: the letterhead lines it was built for are not endorsed by the lexicon, and the strict 'more endorsed' rule is what keeps it from swapping proper nouns. |

Harness: dev-8 greedy 97.6% (live 97.5), split/merge readings 94.4% (live 90.3); legal-8 greedy 97.2% (live 98.2), multi-variant 90.7% (live 93.8). Six sets against the live scorer (fold convention): dev-8 96.1/91.5 → 95.9/91.4, broad-30 93.5/87.4 → 93.5/87.2, legal-8 92.2/84.3 → 91.7/83.8, modern 91.5/85.7 → 91.5/86.0, news-8 93.5/87.3 → **93.1/88.4**, mag-8 68.4/51.1 → **68.2/54.8** (recall 72.1 → 77.0). Every standard-set move is inside its seed range (0.4 / 0.5 / 1.0 / 0.7 word) and the two new domains move by more than any range, so the new strips buy the new domains without costing the old ones measurably — but they do not lift the old ones either: the scorer is at its ceiling there (harness greedy 97.5%, rerank oracle-bound at 95.4%), and the remaining letter and typewriter errors are the segmentation's, not the scorer's. Pipeline against the live scorer (fold convention): dev-8 96.1/91.5 → **96.5/92.1**, broad-30 93.5/87.4 → 93.5/87.5, legal-8 92.2/84.3 → **91.0/83.7** (precision 91.7 → 91.3), modern 91.5/85.7 → 91.5/85.9, news-8 93.5/87.3 → 92.9/**88.6**, mag-8 68.4/51.1 → 66.4/51.9. Word accuracy up on five of six sets and dev-8 past its seed bar, but character accuracy DOWN on legal-8 (1.2), news-8 (0.6) and mag-8 (2.0) while their word accuracy rises: the sharper scorer's injected and re-read strings win more whole words and lose more characters in the words they get wrong — a precision effect the greedy harness, which scores aligned words only, does not see. Not adopted on one seed (a character loss on the typewriter set is a loss); the overnight second seeds of both fine-tune recipes decide, and the decomposition of the legal-8 character loss (`compare_legacy_errors.py`) is the first task after. |
