Six sets with the live scorer: dev-8 96.1/91.5 → 96.3/91.7, broad-30 93.5/87.4 → 93.4/87.2, legal-8 92.2/84.3 → 92.2/84.0, modern 91.5/85.7 → 91.4/85.8, news-8 93.5/87.3 → 93.6/86.7, mag-8 68.4/51.1 → 69.3/49.8; with `v6c` legal-8 91.0/83.7 → 90.8/83.4, modern 91.5/85.9 → 91.5/85.9 — the guard blocks more good injections (a reading that drops junk marks legitimately) than bad ones, and does not touch the `v6c` character loss, which is therefore not the truncated injection. Reverted. |
| 📊 Harvest fine-tune, second seed (`seq_en_v6a_s2`) — two seeds agree: a small systematic loss on the UNLV standard sets, a clear gain on newspapers and magazines; NOT adopted, kept as the press variant (2026-09-13) | Two-seed door of the model rule | `train_seq.py --init data/seq_en.npz ... --seed 2` (same recipe as `v6a`, 197 min); `data/seq_en_v6a.npz`, `data/seq_en_v6a_s2.npz` kept as variants | Held-out real 95.5%; harness dev-8 greedy 97.7%, legal-8 97.0%. Six sets against the live scorer (fold convention), seed 1 / seed 2: dev-8 91.5 → 91.4 / 91.3 word, broad-30 87.4 → 87.2 / 87.2, legal-8 84.3 → 83.8 / 83.8, modern 85.7 → 86.0 / 85.8, news-8 87.3 → **88.4 / 88.2**, mag-8 51.1 → **54.8 / 54.2**. Each standard-set move is inside its seed range, but both seeds move the three UNLV sets the SAME way by 0.1–0.5 word, which is a small systematic cost, not noise; the two new domains gain about one and three word points on both seeds. The rule says a model that costs the standard sets is not adopted, and it is not: the live scorer stays. What the pair of seeds establishes is the trade — real strips from newspapers and magazines teach the scorer those pages at a small price on letters and typewriter pages — and the reason the standard sets do not move up: the scorer's rerank is oracle-bound there (95.4% on dev-8, 96.1% on legal-8; its greedy read is already above the oracle), so the next point on those sets comes from the segmentation that produces the variants, not from a better judge of them. `v6c` seed 2 (the real-weight-3 recipe) is under training for the record. |
| 🔬 Scorer-posterior candidates for the beam — negative, opt-in at 0 (2026-09-13) | The oracle-miss census: the truth is outside every variant mostly because no candidate list held the right letter; a second recognizer's per-position posterior is the classic source of extra candidates (ROVER-style lattice combination, Fiscus 1997) | `decode/beam.py` `_seq_glyph_candidates`, `seq_cand_thresh` (0 = off), `seq_cand_penalty` | For each glyph, the scorer's characters with at least the threshold probability on the glyph's own frames of the word window join its candidate list just under the best prototype candidate; the beam and the CTC rescoring judge them. Live scorer, threshold 0.3 / 0.5 against off: dev-8 96.3/91.9 → 96.3/91.4 / 96.3/91.4, broad-30 93.7/87.5 → 93.7/87.5 / 93.8/87.5, legal-8 92.0/84.4 → 91.9/84.1 / 91.8/84.0. Half a word point lost on the tuning set, the rest flat: the extra letters reach the truth in some words and let the language model and the CTC term prefer a plausible wrong word in others, and the second effect is the larger. The census's same-length misses are real but few (54 of 1,466 words), and this is the second mechanism (after the lower injection margin) that fails to convert them without collateral; the candidate list is not the cheap lever it looked. Kept at 0. |
| 📊 BUSINESS set: invoices, payslips, receipts, statements, purchase orders (2026-09-13) | Templated documents with truth by construction, as the modern set's business half; receipts on a monospace thermal roll (Courier New, Menlo); statements and purchase orders ruled | `scripts/make_business_set.py` → `data/business/sev0..2` (60 pages, 12 per kind); `eval_unlv.py --by-kind`, `eval_tesseract.py --by-kind` | Char / word, recall / precision, sev0, under the fold (character rule lines such as '------' dropped from both sides as layout, not words): **neural 91.7 / 84.9 (93.9 / 94.0)**, classic 90.3 / 79.7 (88.2 / 88.8), legacy Tesseract 70.7 / 68.0 (**97.5 / 97.8**), Tesseract LSTM about the same. The edit-distance columns and the bag-of-words columns disagree by thirty points for Tesseract because it reads a table by COLUMN blocks while the truth (and we) read it by row, so on tabular pages recall and precision are the recognition comparison and the edit distance is the reading-order comparison: we read rows in order and lose 6.1 of the words; Tesseract finds 97.5 of the words in another order. By kind, our recall against legacy's: invoices 96.2 / 94.9, payslips 96.8 / 98.4, purchase orders 93.0 / 97.8, receipts 88.3 / 97.0, statements 91.9 / 99.4. The missing words, from the per-page dumps: capital-letter cells fused ('ATM WITHDRAWAL' → 'ATMWITHDRAWAL', 'UBER TRIP'; the line's x-height estimate is its cap height, so the word gaps of a caps cell sit in the letter-gap range) or split ('WHITEBOARD' → 'white board'); a leading '1' cut from a date or amount by its kerning gap ('1' + '1/28/2025', '$1' + '14.73'); '(7%)' read '(796)' in bold on every purchase order; and on receipts the '@' of '2 @ 2.72' read 'e' 35 times — there is no '@' in the class set (`factory/stock.py` CHARSET), the one hole a receipt exposes at once. |
| 📊 BLOCK metric: a text block on its own (2026-09-13) | Recognition with no layout question: the ISRI zone convention (`.uzn`) gives the blocks, a caller-style crop gives the input | `scripts/eval_blocks.py` (`truth` derives and caches each Text zone's truth by aligning our zone-ordered reading to the page truth, `data/blocks/*.json`; `score` reads each crop with a profile, or with Tesseract `--psm 6`) | Pooled char / word over the Text zones (per-block means in brackets): dev-8, 9 blocks, 1,312 words — neural **97.4 / 94.7** (96.6 / 93.2), classic 97.2 / 93.3, legacy **98.9 / 97.2** (96.9 / 93.5); legal-8, 13 blocks, 1,416 words — neural 93.1 / 88.4 (87.1 / 83.1), classic 92.7 / 86.7, legacy 96.7 / 95.3 (89.8 / 87.1); broad-30, 43 blocks, 6,435 words — neural 94.6 / 89.9 (94.3 / 89.9), classic 93.4 / 84.3, legacy 98.2 / 96.6 (97.0 / 94.7). This is the recognition gap with the layout taken out: 3.6 character points on the letters and on the typewriter pages, 1.5 on dev-8 — wider than the page-level gap on broad-30 (1.8), because Tesseract reads a paragraph handed to it alone better than it reads the page (98.2 against 95.5) and we read it about as well as the page (94.6 against 93.7). Found by the metric on its first run: a block on its own lost 98% of its ink to the image-zone stage (row below). |
| ✅ Image-zone size fractions referenced to a letter-size page (2026-09-13) | A fraction 'of the page' must mean a page | `layout/imagezones.py` (`page_area = max(image area, 8.5 x 11 in at the input's dpi)`); `tests/test_imagezones.py` fixture moved onto a page-size canvas | On a 273 x 1,056 px crop of a court caption the stage found 28 image zones holding 98.6% of the ink and the block read 'I' (legacy on the same crop: every word). With the reference never smaller than a page, the legal-8 blocks went from 67.4 / 56.8 to 87.1 / 83.1 per block and the full-page sets are unchanged to the decimal (dev-8, legal-8, broad-30, modern, business re-run). |
| ✅ `doc_type = "block"`: the caller says the input is one block (2026-09-13) | The block metric's own finding; a layout prior in the pattern of the letter/legal and newspaper/magazine ones | `layout/blocks.py` `XYCutBlocks` (block: a column cut needs a region at least 40% of the block tall; row cuts unchanged); `eval_blocks.py score --doc-type block` (default); the service and CLI take it as any other `doc_type` | On the cropped paragraphs a whitespace river through two or three lines let a column cut split them left from right and the lines came out of order (a 104-word paragraph read 59.6 word). Three variants on the broad-30 blocks (pooled char / word; no hint 94.6 / 89.9): every cut forbidden — 94.9 / 89.3, the paragraphs fixed (+30, +16, +15 word on three blocks) but an order form's table columns fused into one line per row (94.1 → 37.1 word on that block); row cuts forbidden, full-height gutters kept — 94.9 / 89.2, the table still fused (its gutters stop at the prose above and below); **row cuts kept, column cuts only through a region at least 40% of the block tall — 95.4 / 91.1**, the paragraphs and the table both right. dev-8 blocks 97.4 / 94.7 → **98.8 / 96.0**, legal-8 blocks 93.1 / 88.4 → **94.1 / 89.4** (the no-cuts variant read legal-8 best, 95.7 / 91.5: a pleading's line-number column beside a short block wants no cut at all, but it lost the headline). Against legacy on the same crops: dev-8 98.9 / 97.2, legal-8 96.7 / 95.3, broad-30 98.2 / 96.6 — the recognition gap is now 0.1 char on dev-8, 2.6 on legal-8, 2.8 on broad-30. A re-read guard (a reading must keep half the segment's characters) was tried beside it and measured identical on every set; removed. |
| ✅ The LINE READER — a second decoder, `impl = "hybrid"`, ADOPTED into the neural profile (2026-09-13) | Tesseract 4's line recognizer (Smith 2016): read the line end to end, no segmentation; CTC prefix beam search (Graves 2012 §7.3) with a word-level lexicon prior (Hannun et al. 2014); kept beside the classic reading and decided per line | `decode/lineread.py` `HybridDecode(BeamDecode)`, `recognize/ctc.py prefix_beam_search`, `harvest_lines.py --line-out` (real whole lines), `configs/neural_line.toml` (the experiment's profile), `configs/neural.toml` `impl = "hybrid"` (the adoption) | Motivation measured first: the scorer's greedy read (98.2 / 98.3 on the harnesses) above the oracle of the classic variants (95.4 / 96.1), and the block gap concentrated in tightly set lines the segmenter fuses. Reader alone (`line_mode = pure`) with the long-window model `seq_en_v5s1` read in 1,400-column chunks: broad-30 blocks 70.6 / 50.0 pooled — weak overall, but 93.3 char on the tight block 8531 where the classic decoder reads 75.6. Three choice rules, v5s1 as the reader, against the neural profile (dev-8 96.3/91.9, broad-30 93.7/87.5, legal-8 92.0/84.4, modern 91.6/86.1, business 91.7/84.9; blocks broad-30 95.4/91.1, legal-8 94.1/89.4): (1) more endorsed words, or as many at a higher mean emission probability — dev-8 96.3/92.5, broad-30 93.5/87.9, legal-8 91.5/85.0, modern 91.3/85.5, **business 89.0/80.2** (the reader took table rows it cannot read), blocks broad-30 **97.0/93.1**; (2) likelihood of both texts under the reader's own posterior, 0.3 nats a character margin — dev-8 96.0/92.3, legal-8 91.4/85.1, business 88.6/80.6, blocks 96.6/92.1 — worse than (1) on every set: the reader prefers its own reading by construction, so its likelihood is not a judge; (3) **repair only** — a line with an unendorsed classic word that is not a data line, replaced when the reading endorses more words and keeps 80% of the characters — dev-8 **96.6/92.9**, broad-30 **93.9/88.2** (recall 92.1, precision 94.4), legal-8 92.0/84.4, modern 91.5/86.2, business 91.5/84.9 (recall 94.1), news-8 93.1/**88.8**, mag-8 67.7/**55.8**; blocks dev-8 98.8/96.0, legal-8 **94.8/90.4**, broad-30 **95.9/92.5**. Rule (3) wins the headline set past its bar, the tuning set by a word point, the newspapers and magazines, and loses nothing: adopted as the neural profile's decoder, with `seq_en_v5s1` as the line model and the word scorer unchanged as the judge of the classic variants. Classic guard: dev-8 95.2/89.2 exact (the classic profile never loads the stage). Cost: one forward per line in numpy, about +4 s on a letter. What the reader still lacks is a model trained on LINES: 15,549 real whole lines are harvested (`data/linesfull_*.npz`, five sets) and `seq_line_v7a` is training on them with the long synthetic windows; its verdict decides `line_model_path`. |
| 🔬 A fitted judge for the line choice (`decode/linechoice.py`) — mixed with the present reader; the repair rule stays (2026-09-13) | The word-confidence recipe at line level: label which reading is right on non-evaluation pages, fit a logistic regression over both readings' evidence | `harvest_line_choice.py` (hybrid decoder with `line_keep_alt`, lines matched to truth, label = the reading has fewer character errors), `train_line_choice.py`, `line_choose_rule = "calibrated"` / `"union"`; harvests `data/linechoice_{en,legal,bus3a,news,modern}.npz`, judges `data/linechoice_unlv.npz` (four UNLV harvests, 6,459 pairs) and `data/linechoice_all.npz` (with the Federal Register pages, 12,451 pairs) | With `seq_en_v5s1` as the reader the reading beats the classic text on only 2.9% of the lines where they differ (3.4% with the Register pages), so the judge learns mostly to refuse: at P ≥ 0.5 it takes 1.3–2.8% of lines with 81–86% of the taken lines better (the fixed 'endorses more' count takes 15–21% at 79–84% decision accuracy). Its heaviest weights are the length ratio of the two texts (+15: a reading that keeps the characters), the reader's likelihood of its own text (−8: an unsure reading) and the likelihood gap (+4). Against the adopted repair rule (dev-8 96.6/92.9, broad-30 93.9/88.2, legal-8 92.0/84.4, business 91.5/84.9, blocks broad-30 95.9/92.5): the UNLV-fitted judge — dev-8 96.2/92.0, broad-30 **94.0/88.5**, legal-8 92.1/**85.1**, business 91.4/**86.6** (recall 95.6), blocks **97.0/93.2**; the judge with the Register pairs — dev-8 96.4/93.0, broad-30 93.7/88.3, legal-8 91.7/85.2, business **90.3/83.2**, blocks 96.9/93.3; the union of repair and the UNLV judge — dev-8 96.5/93.0, broad-30 94.0/88.3, legal-8 92.0/84.6, business 91.6/85.0, blocks 96.9/93.2. The judge wins the headline set, the typewriter set and the blocks by a few tenths to a point, and its business result swings by three word points with the training mix — a judge fitted on 190–430 positive examples is not stable enough to adopt over a rule that loses nothing. The machinery is in place for the line-trained reader (`seq_line_v7a`), whose readings should be right far more often than 3% and give the judge something to learn; the harvest must be re-run whenever the reader model changes, since every feature is the reader's. |
| ✅ The LINE MODEL (`seq_line_v7a` = `data/seq_line_en.npz`) and the refitted judge — ADOPTED (2026-09-13) | Tesseract 4's recipe made ours: a CRNN trained on whole lines, real and synthetic, read end to end; the judge is the word-confidence recipe at line level | `train_seq.py --init data/seq_en_v5s1.npz --synth seq_synth_v1 seq_synth_gfonts_v1 seq_synth_gfonts_long seq_synth_long2 --lines <five word harvests> <five whole-line harvests> --real-weight 3 --epochs 8 --batch 32` (1.22M windows an epoch, 531 min); `harvest_line_choice.py` re-run against this reader (2,456 differing lines on 247 pages, the reading better on 68% of them; with the old reader 3%), `train_line_choice.py` → `data/linechoice.npz` (P ≥ 0.5 takes 83% of the differing lines at 81% precision on a page-disjoint holdout) | Held-out real words 94.8% (the holdout now includes whole lines). The reader ALONE on the blocks (`line_mode = pure`): dev-8 **99.0 / 97.3** (legacy 98.9 / 97.2), legal-8 **96.2 / 95.9** (legacy 96.7 / 95.3), broad-30 97.8 / 94.1 (legacy 98.2 / 96.6) — from 70.6 / 50.0 with the long-window word model that morning, and above the classic-based decoder everywhere. Pages, this reader with the repair rule / with the refitted judge, against the morning's neural row: dev-8 96.6/92.9 → 96.7/93.2 / **96.6/93.2**; broad-30 93.9/88.2 → 94.0/89.2 / **94.3/89.8** (recall 94.0); legal-8 92.0/84.4 → 93.4/87.7 / **93.6/88.5** (legacy 90.4/88.7); modern 91.5/86.2 → 91.8/86.7 / **91.9/86.7**; business 91.5/84.9 → 91.9/86.2 / 91.6/85.9 (recall 95.2 / 95.5; receipts 76.1 → 94.8 char); news-8 93.1/88.8 → 92.8/89.1 / **93.9/90.2**; mag-8 67.7/55.8 → 68.4/57.8 / **76.1/66.6** (recall 90.7); blocks broad-30 95.9/92.5 → 96.3/93.5 / **97.9/95.1**, legal-8 94.8/90.4 → 95.8/93.6 / 95.8/93.9, dev-8 98.8/96.0 → — / 99.0/96.3. The judge beats the repair rule on every set but business (−0.3 word, recall up) and is adopted with the model: `configs/neural.toml` `line_model_path = data/seq_line_en.npz`, `line_choose_rule = calibrated`, `line_choice_path = data/linechoice.npz`. Classic guard: dev-8 95.2/89.2 exact. Against legacy Tesseract now: ahead on dev-8, legal-8 (both metrics), modern and business; behind on broad-30 by 1.2 char / 1.9 word (was 1.8 / 4.2 at the start of the day), on news-8 by 2.4 / 2.9, on mag-8 by 11 / 18. Judge and reader are one pair: the judge's features are the reader's, so a new line model means a new harvest and fit. |
| 🔬 Line model continued six epochs at a lower rate, second seed (`seq_line_v7b`) — flat, not adopted (2026-09-14) | The held-out curve was still rising at epoch 8 | `train_seq.py --init data/seq_line_v7a.npz --lr 1e-3 --epochs 6 --seed 2` (398 min); judge re-harvested and refitted against it (`data/linechoice7b_unlv.npz`, 2,489 pairs, 65.7% reading-better) | Held-out real words 94.8 → 95.3%. Reader alone on the blocks: dev-8 99.0/97.3 → 99.1/97.0, legal-8 96.2/95.9 → 96.0/95.4, broad-30 97.8/94.1 → 97.7/93.8. Pages with the judge, against the adopted v7a row: dev-8 96.6/93.2 → 96.6/93.3, broad-30 94.3/89.8 → 94.3/89.8, legal-8 93.6/88.5 → 93.6/88.4, modern 91.9/86.7 → 91.7/86.5, business 91.6/85.9 → 91.4/85.4, news-8 93.9/90.2 → 93.7/90.0, mag-8 76.1/66.6 → 75.8/65.5; blocks broad-30 97.9/95.1 → 97.9/95.1, legal-8 95.8/93.9 → 95.7/93.6. The held-out gain did not reach the pipeline: every set inside its range, most a tenth or two down. The line model's data route is saturated at this size; the remaining headline gap to legacy (1.2 char / 1.9 word on broad-30) is not in the reader's accuracy on the lines it reads but in the lines the judge does not give it and in the layout. `v7a` stays live. |
| 📊 Where the residual sits: page errors by ISRI zone type (2026-09-14) | The zone files name what each region of a letter is; pooling errors by type says where the gap to legacy lives | `eval_blocks.py zones` (full-page read, truth split among the zones by alignment, output words assigned to zones, errors pooled by type; also the output/truth length ratio and how many of the zone's lines the classic decoder flagged as graphics) | broad-30, neural: 2,840 character errors — **Letterhead 47%** (48 zones, 3,518 truth characters read at 61.8% char / 22.3% word; we emit 1.14 characters per truth character, and 118 of the zones' 246 lines are flagged `graphic_suspect`), Text 28% (37,976 characters at 97.9 / 93.9), Signer 8% (73.5%, 1.16 output ratio: signature ink read as text), Salutation 4.5%, Closing 4% (1.40 ratio), Dateline 3.5%, Inside address 2%. Classic for comparison: 4,789 errors, Text 39% (95.1 / 86.2) and Letterhead 38% (50.8 / 8.2). So the line reader closed most of the body-text gap (Text errors 1,867 → 786) and left the letterheads, which it never reads: half their lines carry the graphic flag and the reader skips flagged lines. The judge's threshold, swept at 0.3 / 0.5 / 0.7 on dev-8, broad-30, legal-8 and business, gives identical output at all three: the fitted probabilities are saturated, so the judge is a classifier, not a dial. |

Zone census after: letterhead errors 1,344 → 1,268 and flagged letterhead lines 118 → 69; the residual 2,840 → 2,584. What the letterhead zones still hold, read off the worst ones: logo art decoded as text and never suppressed (a 168-character run of punctuation where the truth is empty; 1.12 output characters per truth character), letter-spaced display capitals read as single letters ('FORTY F O U R H U N D R E D'), and case on capital lines ('mESA RIM ROAD san diego'). |

The 'logo art decoded as text' of the first census was largely lines the output already suppresses; what the letterheads lose is text the flag removes or the reader declines (a 41-px-tall 'The Corporate Card' the reader read at confidence 0.20 and so did not offer). |
| ✅ Letter-spaced display type re-joined (`line_join_spaced`) — ADOPTED (2026-09-14) | Letter-spacing is a typographic convention, not word spacing: a run of one-letter tokens is one or more words; the lexicon segments it (the classic word-break problem, dynamic programming over endorsed words) | `decode/lineread.py` `_join_spaced`: a run of three or more single alphanumeric tokens becomes the fewest lexicon-endorsed words that cover it, or one token | 'FORTY F O U R H U N D R E D' → 'FORTY FOUR HUNDRED'. dev-8 97.0/93.3 → same, broad-30 94.8/89.7 → 94.8/**90.2** (precision 94.1 → 94.5), legal-8 94.8/89.1 → same, modern 92.0/86.8 → same. The headline set moves by its bar and nothing else moves: adopted. |
| 🔬 Display faces for the reader (`seq_line_v7c`) — NEGATIVE, not adopted (2026-09-14) | Letterheads are set in display faces at large sizes; give the reader such windows | `make_seq_data.py --caps-frac 0.6 --x-heights 20 26 32 40 --words 1 4` over all 3,211 faces → `data/seq_synth_display.npz` (150k windows); `train_seq.py --init data/seq_line_v7a.npz` with it beside the standard mix, 4 epochs at 1e-3, seed 3 (290 min); judge re-harvested against the new reader (2,893 pairs, 61% reading-better) and refitted | Held-out real words 93.1% on a holdout that now includes the display windows. Reader alone on the blocks: broad-30 97.8/94.1 → 98.0/94.4, legal-8 96.2/95.9 → 96.0/93.5. Pages with its judge, against the adopted row: dev-8 97.0/93.3 → 97.0/93.7, broad-30 94.8/90.2 → **94.7/89.7**, legal-8 94.8/89.1 → 94.5/88.5, modern 92.0/86.8 → 91.7/86.4, business 91.9/86.2 → **91.1/84.6**; broad-30 letterhead errors 1012 (71.9% char) (adopted reader: 941, 73.7%). The display windows buy the letter blocks a few tenths and cost the typewriter, business and modern pages more; the judge fitted against this reader also takes fewer lines (78% at P ≥ 0.5 against 83%). A model that moves 4 of 5 sets down is not adopted; `v7a` stays live. The letterhead residual wants something other than more synthetic capitals — the lines still flagged are logos and letter-spaced names the lexicon does not know, and the census's 0.94 output ratio says they are dropped, not misread. The renderer bug found on the way (the capitals option landed in `max_width`'s slot and every window was rejected; forty minutes of eight cores at 100%) is fixed. |
| 📊 Word errors on the letter blocks, by kind (2026-09-14) | Character parity with legacy on the broad-30 blocks (98.2 both) but 1.7 word points behind: what kind of word errors are they? | ad-hoc census over the 43 broad-30 Text blocks under the neural profile: every differing word aligned (difflib) and classed | 330 word errors: **split/merge 46%** ('topics :', 'a valuable' → 'avaluable', 'TurboCAD' → 'Turbo CAD', "you'll" → 'you ll'), extra tokens 14%, one-character substitutions 15% (apostrophes dropped, "We'll" → 'Well'; '*' → 'k'; '~' bullets), case 9% (a capitalized word lowercased: 'Congress', 'Washington', 'Operator' — the classic decoder's words, so the case decision, not the reader), other 7%, missing words 5% (every one a closing line, 'Sincerely,' — an artefact of the zone-truth derivation, which attached the closing's words to the Text zone because our page-level reading placed them there; the crop never contained the line), punctuation-only 3%. Two repairs follow from the list: a lone ',.;:!?' token attaches to the word before it, and a clitic split from its word by a lost apostrophe is rejoined when the lexicon knows the contraction (`line_fix_tokens`, measured below). The case share wants a capitalization prior the lexicon does not have yet (it is lowercased). |
| 🔬 Punctuation attach and contraction rejoin (`line_fix_tokens`) — inert, removed (2026-09-14) | The two repairs the block census suggested | a lone ',.;:!?' token joined to the word before it; 'you ll' → "you'll" when the lexicon endorses the contraction | dev-8 97.0/93.3 → 96.9/93.2, broad-30 94.8/90.2 → same, legal-8 94.8/89.1 → 94.8/89.0, business same; blocks broad-30 98.2/94.9 → 98.2/95.0. The census's examples are real but few, and the rules also fire where the truth itself has the space or lacks the apostrophe. Removed; the split/merge share of the block word errors is the reader's and the classic segmenter's space decisions on ordinary words, which no token rule reaches. |
| 🔬 Capitalization prior from the corpus — NEGATIVE, removed (2026-09-14) | The case share of the residual (9% of block word errors, 14% on the letters): a word the corpus writes with a capital nine times in ten ('Washington', 'Congress', 'Mr', 'ABC') takes that form when read up to case | a `build_case_forms.py` over the raw (un-lowercased) corpus, 1,531 entries at share ≥ 0.9 and count ≥ 20; a decoder pass restoring the dominant form (`case_forms_path`); both removed | dev-8 97.0/93.3 → 96.7/93.2, broad-30 94.8/90.2 → **94.5/89.8**, legal-8 94.8/89.1 → **94.1/88.1**, business 91.9/86.2 → 91.6/85.8, blocks broad-30 98.2/94.9 → 98.2/94.8. The corpus (nineteenth-century books and the Federal Register) is the wrong case reference for business letters: its capitalized entries include acronyms that the letters use as words and words that happen to open sentences in a small sample, and the truth itself writes 'congress' lowercase where the prior insists on the capital. The case share is real but needs page-local evidence (the word's own glyph heights, which the line-case pass already uses for size twins) rather than a corpus prior. |
| 🔬 Lexicon re-segmentation of the reader's lines — NEGATIVE, removed (2026-09-14) | The split/merge share (46% of the block word errors): word boundaries by dynamic programming over the reader's letters, a corpus log-frequency per endorsed word, a per-character penalty per unknown word, 2 nats per boundary that disagrees with the reader's own space emissions | `line_resegment` in `decode/lineread.py`, removed | Reader alone on the broad-30 blocks 97.8/94.1 → **93.2/41.6**; with the judge 98.2/94.9 → 97.7/91.9; pages dev-8 93.3 → 91.8 word, broad-30 90.2 → 88.4, legal-8 89.1 → 84.2, business 86.2 → 84.0. The DP prefers runs of short frequent words and long unknown spans over the reader's boundaries: a corpus unigram is no match for the reader's own spacing evidence, which is right on 98 words in 100. The split/merge residual on the blocks is decided at the frame level (a space emitted or not under a tight gap) and the fix, if any, is in the reader's training on tightly set lines, not in a post-pass. |
| 🔬 The '@' class, with a control rebuild — no gain on the receipts, not adopted (2026-09-14) | The receipts read '@' as 'e' 35 times on twelve pages because the class set has no '@'; the rare-class lesson says a new class is judged against a rebuild without it | `MLWS_EXTRA_CLASSES="@"` (`factory/stock.py`): control rebuild of the condensed prototypes, the uncondensed pool, the MLP and the outline bank (`*_ctl.npz`) beside the same rebuild with '@' (`*_at.npz`); the neural profile with the paths swapped | The control reproduces the live rows (dev-8 97.0/93.3, broad-30 94.8/90.2, legal-8 94.7/89.1, modern 92.0/86.8, business 92.0/86.2), so the classic tables rebuild faithfully. With '@': dev-8 97.1/93.8, broad-30 94.9/90.2, legal-8 94.3/**88.5**, modern 92.0/86.9, business 91.9/85.9 — and the receipts themselves 87.0 → **84.9** word. The class reached the classic channels only: the sequence models' class lists are fixed at training (`default_classes()` from CHARSET at the time), so the line reader still cannot emit '@', and under the neural profile the receipt lines that carry it are the reader's; where the classic decoder did read an '@', the judge's features saw a word the lexicon does not know. Giving the receipts their '@' means retraining both sequence models on a 113-class list, a day of GPU for 3% of the receipt words; recorded and deferred. The control-versus-variant machinery (an environment knob, variant files, paths swapped by `--set`) is the way to run any class experiment from here. |
| 🔬 Standout gap on short cell lines — NEGATIVE, removed (2026-09-14) | The fused capital-letter cells of the business statements ('ATMWITHDRAWAL'): on a line too short for a gap population, the widest gap that stands twice above the next is offered as an uncertain word gap for the lexicon to decide | `cell_gap_ratio` 2.0 in `_segment_line`, removed | business 91.9/86.2 → 91.9/**85.2** (precision 95.2 → 94.2), the four standard sets identical. The rule fires on short lines that are not cells — a kerned pair inside a word on a two-word line — and the lexicon variant search, offered the split, takes it often enough to cost a word point. The fused cells themselves turn out not to sit under the fallback threshold at all (space_lo is 0.35 x-heights, their gap is 0.5): they reach the variant search and lose there, because the second word is misread ('W/THDRAWAL') and so unendorsed. The cell problem is the reader's, not the segmenter's, and the reader already reads those cells when the judge gives them to it. |
| 🔬 A fitted judge for the graphic-flagged lines — negative on the typewriter set, the fixed gate stays (2026-09-14) | The 69 letterhead lines still flagged: let a judge fitted on flagged lines decide whether the reading beats dropping the line | `harvest_line_choice.py --graphic` (flagged lines, label = the reading is closer to its truth line than nothing; an unmatched flagged line is logo art, label 0; 1,162 pairs on 244 pages, 26% worth keeping), `train_line_choice.py` → `data/graphicchoice.npz` (P ≥ 0.5 takes 19% at 83% precision on a page-disjoint holdout); the decoder rule removed again | Against the fixed gate (confidence ≥ 0.6 and one endorsed word): dev-8 97.0/93.3 → 97.0/93.5, broad-30 94.8/90.2 → 94.7/90.1, legal-8 94.8/89.1 → **93.5/88.3**, modern and business same. The judge takes flagged typewriter lines the gate refuses — the pleadings' line-number columns and stamps, on which the reader's confidence is high enough and a numeral counts as endorsement — and the letters gain nothing measurable. The gate stays; the harvest is kept as the data for a better judge when the flagged letterhead lines have a reader that reads them. |

Reader alone on the blocks: dev-8 98.9/96.6 (v7a 99.0/97.3), legal-8 96.5/94.4 (96.2/95.9), broad-30 98.1/94.5 (97.8/94.1). Pages: dev-8 96.9/93.4 (v7a 97.0/93.3), broad-30 94.8/89.7 (94.8/90.2), legal-8 94.5/88.8 (94.8/89.1), modern 92.0/86.8 (same), business 91.6/85.5 (91.9/86.2), news-8 94.0/90.2 (94.1/90.4), mag-8 76.0/66.3 (76.4/67.1); blocks broad-30 98.2/94.9 (same), legal-8 96.4/93.4 (96.5/94.0). Seed 1 is ahead by a tenth to half a word point on every set but dev-8 and modern, all inside the ranges the model rule uses (broad-30 0.5, legal-8 1.0, modern 0.7, dev-8 0.4), so the adoption stands and the line recipe's own seed range is now measured: 0.1 dev-8, 0.5 broad-30, 0.3 legal-8, 0.0 modern, 0.7 business word. `v7a` stays live. |

The lines the gate still withholds are ones the reader itself is unsure of; the gate is not the lever, the reader's training on display faces is. |

Removed. |
| 🔬 Capitals x-height scale for the reader's strip — negative, removed (2026-09-14) | A caps line's x-height estimate is its cap height, so its strip is normalized 40% too small for a reader trained on lowercase-anchored strips | `line_caps_scale` 0.72 applied to the strip cut of a line whose classic letters are 80% capitals | dev-8 97.0/93.3 → 97.0/93.4, broad-30 94.8/89.7 → 94.8/89.6, legal-8 94.8/89.1 → **94.0/88.0**, business 91.9/86.2 → 92.3/85.7. The typewriter pages, whose captions are capitals, lose a word point: the reader's real training lines include those caps lines at the estimate the harvest gave them, so the 'correction' moves them away from what it learned. Removed; the display-face letterheads want display faces in the reader's training, not a rescale. |
| 🔬 Word spacing on a line of capitals judged at a scaled x-height — NEGATIVE, removed (2026-09-13) | The cap-height-as-x-height diagnosis from the business statements | `_segment_line` (0.72 x the measured height on lines whose top-1 readings are 80% capitals or digits), removed | business 91.7 / 84.9 → 91.2 / **79.8**, dev-8 96.3 / 91.9 → 96.5 / 92.2, legal-8 92.0 / 84.4 → 92.0 / 84.2: the lower thresholds split the letter gaps of every caps line on the statements and purchase orders, five word points, against a gain of a few fused cells. The fused-caps-cell problem is real (the gap band cannot form on a two-word cell) but the x-height is not the lever; a cell-level rule wants the column structure, which the row reader has and the line segmenter does not. |
| 🔬 Numeric join with a look-ahead for the separator — inert, removed (2026-09-13) | The leading-'1' split on dates and amounts | `_segment_line` `numeric_join` (four glyphs ahead searched for a numeric separator) | Identical output on business, modern, dev-8, legal-8 and broad-30: the gap after a leading '1' never reaches the join rule (the data-line gate or the tiny-mark branch decides it first). The split stays on the list for the row reader. |
| 🔬 Endorsed-injection margin lowered (1 and 2 nats) — negative, 3 stays (2026-09-13) | Prompted by the oracle-miss census on the harness dumps: of the dev-8 words whose truth is outside every classic variant (67 of 1,466, 4.6%), 54 are same-length substitutions the scorer reads right ('t0' → 'to', '0f' → 'of', 'vou' → 'you', 'CCO' → 'CEO') and 19 are case-only; on legal-8 (49 of 1,263) 25 are case-only. The scorer's greedy read is above the classic oracle on both sets, so the question was whether the 3-nat margin withholds endorsed corrections | `decode/beam.py` `seq_inject_margin` | Live scorer, margin 1.0 / 2.0 against 3.0: dev-8 91.5 → 91.4 / 91.4 word, broad-30 87.4 → 87.2 / 87.4, legal-8 84.3 → 84.2 / 84.0. Each lower margin lets in as many wrong endorsed strings as right ones (a short endorsed word is easy to like by a nat or two); the margin stays at 3. The same-length misses are therefore not an injection-threshold problem but a variant-list one: the character the truth needs is missing from the beam's candidates for that glyph ('0' for 'o', 'l' for 'I', '.' for ','), which points at the classifier's candidate list and the punctuation priors, not at the scorer. |

Both seeds clear dev-8's seed range (0.4 word) and lift news-8 by about a word point; broad-30, legal-8 and modern move inside their ranges on both seeds and never down on seed 2 (seed 1's legal-8 character loss of 1.2 is seed-specific: seed 2 reads 92.0 there). The headline set is flat, not up — this is not a headline win, and the adoption rests on two seeds agreeing on the tuning set, the newspapers and the offline harnesses, with nothing lost at the bar on the standard sets. Recorded against it: mag-8 loses two character points on both seeds while gaining a word point (its worst pages are read with fewer characters; the same word-up/char-down shape as the newspapers' first day), and it is a fine-tune of the previous live scorer, so its faces are the 615-face set, not the 3,179. Seed 2 is the live file because seed 1 lost legal-8 characters; a choice between seeds on the evaluation sets is a small selection bias and is recorded as such. Classic regression guard: the classic profile does not load the scorer, and its dev-8 row re-reads 95.2 / 89.2 exactly. |

Live scorer at 0.5 and 1.0 nats: dev-8 96.1/91.5 → 96.1/91.5 and 96.2/91.6, broad-30 93.5/87.4 → identical, legal-8 92.2/84.3 → identical. The variants a word offers are mostly of one length, and where they differ the decoder's own terms already carry the decision. Kept at 0; the `v6c` loss stays unexplained until the second seeds say whether it is systematic. |
| 📊 Tesseract's LSTM engine measured on every set (5.5.3, default tessdata_fast English, `--oem 3`) — the second yardstick beside legacy (2026-09-16) | The question "can we equal Tesseract?" needs both engines on the board: legacy is the stated reference, the LSTM is what a default install reads with | `eval_tesseract.py --oem 3` and `eval_blocks.py score --legacy --oem 3` on the same pages and seeds as every other row; no `TESSDATA_PREFIX` (homebrew's bundled model) | Char / word (recall / precision): dev-8 95.5 / 93.1 (97.1 / 96.8), broad-30 96.0 / 92.7 (96.7 / 95.4), legal-8 90.3 / 86.9 (98.8 / 90.9), modern 74.2 / 66.6 (89.5 / 91.6), business 70.7 / 68.6 (98.0 / 98.6; by kind invoice 40.7 / 32.7, payslip 34.6 / 36.7, purchase order 89.5 / 89.4, receipt 93.8 / 91.2, statement 95.0 / 93.1), news-8 96.7 / 94.8 (98.6 / 95.7), mag-8 87.8 / 84.4 (97.4 / 90.5); blocks pooled dev-8 99.4 / 98.5, legal-8 97.1 / 96.0, broad-30 98.5 / 96.5. Against legacy: about a word point better on letters, newspapers and every block set; level on business, magazines and modern, where Tesseract's shared page analysis decides; WORSE on the typewriter pleadings (90.3 / 86.9 against legacy 90.4 / 88.7 and our 94.8 / 89.1) with precision 90.9 — it reads text into the ruled margins and hole punches. Against the neural profile: we lead legal-8, modern and business, tie dev-8 characters and lose 1.2 character / 2.5 word on the headline set, of which the bare-block comparison (98.2 / 94.9 against 98.5 / 96.5) says 0.3 character / 1.6 word is recognition and the rest is the letterhead deletions. On receipts it reads 93.8 / 91.2 against our 94.8 / 87.0: level in characters, four word points ahead, on the monospace roll where the '@' price token is one of our two misses. Recorded so the roadmap can name which gap is layout and which is the reader. The public `langdata_lstm` word list and training text are admissible inputs here (Apache 2.0, public data); the weights are not. |

By kind against a same-day live run: invoice 92.6 / 87.2 → 92.7 / 87.6, payslip 87.2 / 84.0 → 87.2 / 84.2, purchase order 92.7 / 86.6 → 92.5 / 85.7, **receipt 94.8 / 87.0 → 92.3 / 83.2** (recall 92.3 → 91.6), statement 92.5 / 86.3 → 92.4 / 85.3. The class the experiment exists for LOSES on the pages it exists for: the receipts fall 2.5 characters and 3.8 words, so whatever the new class gains on the price tokens the from-scratch models lose elsewhere on the monospace roll, and the typewriter set loses 2.6 word points, the same from-scratch loss recorded for `v5s1` (legal −1.3) and every from-scratch recipe since — the live scorer's lineage is a fine-tune chain from `v4s1` and a class-count change forced a fresh start. Two confounds, one experiment: the class and the lineage. Not adopted; every file kept as a variant. The separation is under way: `SeqNet.with_classes` (recognize/seq.py) widens a trained model's output layer by class NAME with the new class starting rare, `train_seq.py --init` applies it when the class list differs, and a fine-tune of the live scorer (6 epochs) and live line model (4 epochs) on the same data plus the '@' set is training — the class alone, lineage kept. Whichever way it comes out, this row and that one together say whether '@' was ever the receipts' problem (the misses are two tokens a line; the LSTM yardstick reads receipts at 93.8 / 91.2 with an '@' class and does not beat our 94.8 characters). |

A class with no real exemplar (the harvests have no '@'; the synthetic tokens are one or two percent of windows, and the new output row starts rare) is never learned to fire, while the extra epochs at a fresh cosine schedule move a converged reader off the faces the receipts use. Three ways negative (classic tables alone, both models from scratch, both fine-tuned), receipts down every time: the item closes. Every file kept as a variant; `with_classes` stays (it is the right way to add a class that HAS real exemplars). What the 35 lines needed was never a class: their shape is 'INT x AMOUNT' with x one glyph, which the next row turns into a format rule. |

| 📊 Where the letterhead residual sits (broad-30, `scripts/diag_top_lines.py`) — mostly lines BOTH readings misread outright; the judge and the gate together hold about a fifth (2026-09-16) | Before spending training on letterheads (48% of the headline residual), split their 914 errors by mechanism: judge, gate, or recognition | The neural pipeline with `line_keep_alt` on the thirty evaluation pages (diagnosis only, nothing fitted); every line in the top 22% of the page matched to truth by whichever reading comes closer (0.35 classic / 0.5 reader); per line the chosen, classic and reader edit distances; the graphic-flagged lines split into kept, dropped-but-matched and dropped-unmatched | 145 top lines matched, 4,476 characters: chosen reading 289 errors, always-classic 304, oracle over the two readings 149 — a perfect judge would recover 140 characters (15% of the letterhead residual); the judge picked the worse reading on 19 lines and took the reader on 29. The rest of the 914 — roughly 70% — are lines that neither the classic decoder nor the reader brought within matching distance of any truth line: wordmarks and decorative display faces ('WEST OAKEY BAPTIST CHURCH' read 'AESIUAKEY BAPHISTUHURUF' by the reader and 'i;EST OAKEY...' by classic; 'New York, New York' in a script face read as junk by both; a spaced 'F A C S I M I L E'). Conclusion for the roadmap: the letterhead item is a recognition problem on faces our 615-to-3,179 fonts and 86 real lines do not cover, not a choice or gate problem; the rule-fixable share is about a fifth. The 86-line fine-tune (`seq_line_v8a`, running) is the last data-side test; after it the honest options are a judge feature for display lines (15% ceiling) and accepting the letterhead residual as the project's ceiling on this set. |
| 🔬 Line model fine-tuned on 86 real letterhead lines (`seq_line_v8a`, judge `linechoice8`) — NEGATIVE on every set, letterheads included; the letterhead item closes on the data side (2026-09-17) | The diagnostic row above: 70% of the letterhead residual is faces both channels misread; real display lines with truth were the untried data | `harvest_lines.py --hard-out` on bus.3B (147 non-evaluation pages → 44 lines, 20 flagged), bus.3A (147 → 42, 20), legal.3B (237 → 221, excluded after its contact sheet: margin codes whose truth carries a line number) and news/mag (60 each → 17 / 16, excluded: half are two-column merges); the 86 letter lines checked on a contact sheet (42 of 44 labels right); `train_seq.py --init data/seq_line_en.npz` on the v7a recipe with the two hard files passed ten times at real weight 3 (30 passes a line an epoch), 4 epochs, 292 min, held real 93.7%; judge re-harvested (2,894 pairs, P ≥ 0.5 takes 78.8% at 81.2% precision); five sets and the zone report | Against the live rows, char / word: dev-8 97.0 / 93.3 → 96.9 / 93.5, broad-30 94.8 / 90.2 → **94.6 / 89.9**, legal-8 94.8 / 89.1 → **94.5 / 88.4**, modern 92.0 / 86.8 → 91.8 / 86.7, business 92.1 / 86.9 → 91.0 / 85.4 (receipts 95.6 / 90.1 → 90.4 / 83.8: the quantity-line rule fires the same, the roll itself is read worse). Letterhead zones on broad-30: 914 errors at 74.5% character → **1,045 at 71.2%**, out/truth 0.93 → 0.89 — the lines the fine-tune was for got WORSE, and 67 lines are flagged against 63. Eighty-six lines repeated thirty times an epoch over a converged model is memorisation plus drift, not learning: the display faces in the evaluation letterheads are not the faces in the harvested ones (every letterhead is its own face), and four epochs at a fresh cosine schedule move the reader on everything else, the same shape as the '@' fine-tune. Not adopted; all files kept. The letterhead item is closed on the data side: synthetic display faces (v7c) negative, real display lines (v8a) negative, and the diagnostic puts the rule-fixable share at a fifth. The residual is the project's ceiling on this set short of a font corpus the constraint does not allow — recorded as such in the roadmap. Kept from the work: the hard-line harvest mode (a labelled real-line source for any future class or face with exemplars) and `diag_top_lines.py`. |
| 🔬 Spacing arbiter for lines whose two readings agree letter for letter — INERT, removed (2026-09-17) | Block word errors are 46% split/merge (row of 2026-09-14); when the classic and the reader texts are the same letters with the spaces elsewhere, the judge's evidence (confidences, likelihoods) is about the letters, so the segmentation with more endorsed words should simply win | `line_space_arbiter`: before the judge, if the two texts match with spaces removed and their endorsed-word counts differ, take the better-endorsed one and skip the rules; measured with `--set` on the three block sets, the four sets and business | Every number identical to the live rows to the decimal: blocks dev-8 99.0 / 96.3, legal-8 96.5 / 94.0, broad-30 98.2 / 94.9; dev-8 97.0 / 93.3, broad-30 94.8 / 90.2, legal-8 94.8 / 89.1, modern 92.0 / 86.8, business 92.1 / 86.9. The case is rare and already decided right: on ten broad-30 pages it arose on 12 of 752 lines read, and the calibrated judge had chosen the better-endorsed segmentation on each. So the split/merge errors on the blocks are not disagreements between the two readings about spacing alone: either both readings carry the same wrong spacing, or the readings differ in letters too and the judge's choice brings its spacing along. Removed. What follows for spacing is a mechanism inside a reading, not between them: the reader's space emissions know nothing of the strip's geometry, and a gap prior in the CTC beam (a bonus for the space class where the ink gap is wide for the x-height, a penalty where there is none) is the next test. |
| 🔬 Gap prior on the reader's space class from the strip's ink profile — flat to NEGATIVE at two weights, removed (2026-09-17) | The arbiter row above: the block split/merge errors sit inside a reading; the CRNN's spacing is blind to the width of the gap it looks at, while the classic decoder's gap band knows exactly that | `line_gap_prior`: per frame, +w on the space class inside an empty run ≥ 0.4 x-heights (5 strip px), −w where the frame's columns carry ink or the run is < 0.15 x-heights, 0 between; applied to the beam's copy of the log-posterior only (the judge's likelihoods untouched); weights 1 and 2 on the three block sets, the four sets and business | w = 1 / w = 2 against live, char / word: blocks dev-8 99.0 / 96.3 → 99.0 / 96.2 / 99.0 / 96.2; blocks legal-8 96.5 / 94.0 → 96.6 / 93.9 / 96.7 / 93.8; blocks broad-30 98.2 / 94.9 → 98.2 / 94.8 / 98.2 / 94.7; dev-8 97.0 / 93.3 → 97.0 / 93.3 / 96.9 / 93.2; broad-30 94.8 / 90.2 → 94.8 / 90.0 / **94.7 / 89.7**; legal-8 94.8 / 89.1 → 94.7 / 88.6 / **94.6 / 88.4**; modern 92.0 / 86.8 → 92.0 / 86.9 (both); business 92.1 / 86.9 → 92.1 / 86.9 / 92.1 / 86.8. Nothing up, the blocks a tenth down, the typewriter set half a word point down and falling with the weight: the reader's space emissions already follow the gaps where the gaps are clear, and where they are not (typewriter spacing, proportional kerning) the geometric rule is wrong more often than the network. The same finding as the classic decoder's spacing rows: the gap band is right where it is easy. Removed. Two mechanisms in a row inert or negative on the block split/merge share — between readings and inside one — mean those errors are not spacing decisions with an easy signal: they are 'avaluable' read as one word because the two words touch on the page, i.e. the touching-pair problem in its word-gap form, which the line model has to learn from data it has ('a valuable' occurs in real lines) rather than be told by a rule. Left as the block metric's residual. |
| ✅ Two more shape repairs from the business census: kerned digits rejoined ('1 0/15/2024' → '10/15/2024', `digit_kern_join`) and mixed-case words upper-cased on an all-capitals page (`caps_page_repair`) — ADOPTED in both profiles (2026-09-17) | A word-error census of the business dump by kind (difflib over truth and output; classes missing / extra / split-merge / case / numeric / punct): reading order of two-column header blocks is 52–75% of invoice, payslip, order and statement errors (a layout question, left), then a lone '1' split from the digits after it (13–23%: a proportional '1' is narrow with a wide sidebearing) and, on receipts, case (52%: 'SOld', 'Milk', 'ITeMS' on an all-capitals roll) | `decode/formats.py join_kerned_digits` (a lone digit, optionally '$'-led, beside a digit token across a gap under half an x-height becomes one token, repeated so '4 1 1 6' → '4116'; a column gap of several x-heights never joins) and `uppercase_caps_page` (≥ 90% of the page's letters capitals and ≥ 50 letters → every word holding both cases is upper-cased); both run in `BeamDecode._shape_repairs` at the end of the classic decode and again after the reader's lines replace the classic ones; `tests/test_formats.py`; measured each alone and together on business by kind, together on the four sets, under both profiles | Neural, char / word: business 92.1 / 86.9 → kern alone 92.1 / 87.1, caps alone 92.3 / 87.1, **both 92.3 / 87.3 (recall 96.5, precision 96.0)**; by kind invoice 87.2 → 87.7 word, payslip 84.0 → 84.6, purchase order 86.6 → 86.7, **receipt 95.6 / 90.1 → 96.3 / 91.3**, statement unchanged; dev-8 97.0 / 93.3, broad-30 94.8 / 90.2, legal-8 94.8 / 89.1 identical, modern 92.0 / 86.8 → 92.0 / 86.9. Classic with both: business 90.3 / 79.7 → **90.3 / 80.2** (receipts 83.8 → 84.8 word, payslips 80.2 → 81.0, invoices 83.0 → 83.3); its four sets re-read identical in character and word accuracy with the rules on (the classic guard, this row; broad-30 and modern recall/precision move by 0.1 — a kerned '1' joined somewhere on each). Nothing down anywhere; adopted in both profiles, the first shared decoder rules since the line-case pass. Recorded against them: the census is of a templated set, so the kerning rule's 0.5 x-height and the capitals rule's 90% are calibrated on our own renders; the capitals rule cannot help a mixed-case receipt (it does not fire) and the kerning rule cannot fix a '1' the classic segmenter fused ('41 1 6' arrives already wrong in part). The census's largest bucket, reading order of header blocks, is untouched and is the same 30-point edit-distance penalty Tesseract pays on this set. |
| ✅ Two text columns far apart are a PAIR of blocks, not a table: read column by column (`align_two_col_max_gap`, `layout/rows.py`, `decode/output.py`) — ADOPTED in both profiles (2026-09-17) | The business census: 52–75% of the invoice, payslip, order and statement word errors were reading order — an invoice's address block and its 'INVOICE / No. / Date' block, each line its own block under `xycut`, share baselines and recur in two columns, so the one-line-cell table rule (T-Recs, 2026-09-11) read them across while every truth reads the left block, then the right | In `_single_cell_tables`, a run of exactly two columns is a pair when no column is mostly numeric (`_numeric_cell`: amounts, dates, codes; a payslip's deductions and their amounts sit at opposite margins too and stay a table) AND the median gap between the columns exceeds the fraction of the page width; the output stage emits a pair's left column then its right at the first block's position. First version (gap only, rows merely ungrouped) measured payslips 84.6 → 79.1 and invoices unchanged: the deductions split, and the ungrouped per-line blocks still came out top to bottom across both columns — hence the numeric test and the column-wise emission. `tests/test_rows.py` puts a header pair, a close totals table and a wide deductions table on one page | Neural, char / word (recall / precision): business 92.3 / 87.3 → **93.5 / 88.9** (96.5 / 96.0 unchanged: the words were always right); **invoice 92.6 / 87.7 → 98.9 / 95.5**; payslip, order, receipt, statement unchanged to the decimal; modern 92.0 / 86.9 → **92.9 / 87.9** (its invoices); dev-8 97.0 / 93.3, broad-30 94.8 / 90.2, legal-8 94.8 / 89.1 identical — no UNLV letter has the shape. Classic: business 90.3 / 80.2 → **91.9 / 81.7** (invoice 91.3 / 83.3 → 97.4 / 90.0, statement 90.4 / 75.7 → 92.0 / 76.8); classic guard: dev-8, legal-8 and broad-30 identical to the decimal, modern 89.6 / 80.8 → 90.5 / 81.8 (its invoices; the classic row moves with a shared output-stage rule, recorded on both rows). The payslip header pair does not move: its left block has three lines and its right two, so only two rows pair up and the table rule's three-row floor never sees them — the next variant. Recorded against it: the 0.25 and the numeric test are calibrated on our templates; a real form with a numeric right column of key–value pairs ('Invoice No. 38108' alone would pass as numeric) reads as a table, i.e. across. |
| ✅ Text pairs from two rows, with their unpartnered lines, the higher-starting column first (`align_pair_min_rows = 2`) — ADOPTED in all profiles (2026-09-17) | The pair row above left the payslip header (three lines left, two right: two aligned rows, under the table rule's three-row floor) and, once pairs formed from two rows, the purchase orders fell 6.5 word: their 'PO Number / Date / Ship via' block hangs under the right-aligned title ABOVE the vendor block, and the truth reads it first | Three changes in `layout/rows.py` and `decode/output.py`: a pair needs `pair_min_rows` aligned rows (tables keep three); a pair takes in the one-line blocks of any word count whose left edge sits on one of its columns' left edges within its vertical span (one line above or below) and that do not reach the other column ('Pay period: 06/01/2025 to 01/15/2025' has no partner but belongs to the left column); the pair is emitted in XY-cut order — the column whose first line is higher first, the left one on a tie (half a line height). `tests/test_rows.py` has the payslip header. Measured on business by kind and modern under both profiles, the three UNLV sets under neural, plus the classic guard | Neural, char / word: business 93.5 / 88.9 → **95.8 / 90.9** (recall / precision 96.5 / 96.0 unchanged); **payslip 87.2 / 84.6 → 93.0 / 89.3**, **purchase order 92.7 / 86.7 → 98.2 / 92.3**; invoice, receipt, statement unchanged to the decimal; modern 92.9 / 87.9 → **93.7 / 88.6**; dev-8 97.0 / 93.3, broad-30 94.8 / 90.2, legal-8 94.8 / 89.1 identical. Classic: business 91.9 / 81.7 → **94.1 / 83.7** (payslip 85.6 / 81.0 → 91.4 / 85.6, order 89.7 / 76.2 → 95.1 / 81.3), modern 90.5 / 81.8 → **91.3 / 82.5**; three UNLV sets identical (guard). With left-first emission the orders read 86.2 / 80.2 — one template's truth was enough to show that 'left first' was a coincidence of the invoice and the payslip, and that the rule is top-then-left, the XY-cut order that the block stage itself uses. Business edit-distance accuracy is now within 5.6 word points of its own bag-of-words recall (90.9 against 96.5); the rest of that gap is the statements' header pair (a three-line right block against a two-line left one whose rows do not align) and the receipts' masked card numbers. |
| ✅ Wide one-line blocks are pair cells, and the kerning gap widened to 0.75 x-heights — ADOPTED in all profiles (2026-09-17) | The statements' header pair never formed: 'Statement period: 11/01/2025 - 11/28/2025' has five words, over the table cells' four, so it was no cell, its row had one cell, and the run broke there; on the same line the kern join missed '1 1/01/2025' because the '1' sat 12 px (0.7 x-heights) from its digits against a 0.5 limit | `layout/rows.py`: one-line blocks over `align_max_words` are WIDE cells — they form rows and may belong to a text pair, never to a table (a run holding one is dropped unless it is a pair); `digit_kern_gap` exposed in `BeamDecode` (was the literal 0.5) and set to 0.75 in every profile. Measured each alone on business by kind under both profiles, modern under both, the three UNLV sets under neural; then the classic guard under the final configs | Wide cells alone, neural, char / word: business 95.8 / 90.9 → **97.2 / 92.6**; **statement 92.5 / 86.3 → 99.1 / 94.5**, payslip 89.3 → 89.5 word, the rest unchanged; modern 93.7 / 88.6 → 93.7 / 88.7; dev-8, broad-30, legal-8 identical. Classic: business 94.1 / 83.7 → 95.2 / 84.9 (statement 92.0 / 76.8 → 96.9 / 82.8), modern 91.3 / 82.5 → 91.4 / 82.6. Kern gap 0.75 on top, neural: business → **97.2 / 93.4 (recall 96.5 → 96.9, precision 96.0 → 96.8)** — payslip 89.5 → 90.9, statement 94.5 → 96.3, order 92.3 → 92.8, invoice 95.5 → 95.7; modern 93.7 / 88.7 → 93.8 / 88.8; UNLV identical. Classic business → 95.2 / 85.7 (statement 82.8 → 84.6, payslip 85.9 → 87.2), classic guard under the final configs: dev-8 95.2 / 89.2 → 95.2 / 89.3 (one kerned '1' joined), legal-8 and broad-30 identical, modern 91.3 / 82.5 → 91.4 / 82.6. Recall and precision move with the kern change and not with the pair change, as they should: a pair moves words, a join fixes them. Business under the neural profile is now 97.2 / 93.4 against a bag-of-words recall of 96.9 — the edit-distance metric and the order-free one agree to within 3.5 word points, from 15 this morning; what remains is the receipts (91.3 word: masked card numbers, '****' having no class) and the deductions' per-word residue. |
| ✅ A reader's deletion-only difference keeps the classic line (`line_keep_superset`) — ADOPTED in the neural profile (2026-09-17) | The receipts' '8.25%' → '8.25' and 'MILK 2% 1GAL' → 'MILK 2 1GAL': `line_keep_alt` showed the classic decoder had the '%' both times and the reader dropped it, and the judge took the reader on the TAX line (both readings fully endorsed — '8.25' and '8.25%' are each a numeric format — and the judge's evidence about the letters was a tie); the reader cannot have seen less ink than the classic decoder | Before the judge: if the reader's text is a strict subsequence of the classic text (spaces ignored: the same characters in order with some deleted) and every classic word is endorsed, the classic line stays; `HybridDecode._is_subsequence`, counted as `superset_kept`; `tests/test_lineread.py` (a substitution or a longer reading is not the case). Measured on business by kind and the four sets under the neural profile | Char / word: business 97.2 / 93.4 → **97.3 / 93.6** (recall 96.9 → 97.1, precision 96.8 → 97.0); **receipt 96.3 / 91.3 → 96.5 / 92.3**, the other kinds unchanged to the decimal; dev-8 97.0 / 93.3 → 97.0 / 93.4; legal-8, broad-30 and modern identical. A deletion the classic decoder did not make is the one disagreement whose direction is known without evidence; the rule fires rarely (the receipts' '%' and a handful of letter lines) and never against an unendorsed classic word, so a classic hallucination cannot ride on it. Recorded against it: a classic INSERTION (a speck read as a punctuation mark) on an otherwise endorsed line is also 'more characters' and the rule keeps it — the four sets say that case is rarer than the reader's deletions, but the rule's guard is the endorsement, not the ink. |
