Classic: business 95.2 / 85.7 → 95.2 / 85.7 with the kinds trading places — receipt 94.8 / 84.8 → **96.4 / 87.2** (the class does read the roll's asterisks under classic), payslip 91.8 / 87.2 → **89.2 / 84.3**, invoice 97.4 / 90.2 → 97.9 / 90.8, statement 97.1 / 84.6 → 97.3 / 84.8. The same shape as the '@' class in the classic channels (2026-09-14: dev-8 93.3 → 93.8, legal-8 89.1 → 88.5): an extra rare class lifts the letters' tuning set and costs the typewriter set six tenths, twice now with two different glyphs — the effect is the rebuild with one more class competing on the typewriter glyphs, not the glyph. Not adopted (legal-8 and broad-30 down; the target tokens unfixed under the profile that matters). The masked-number item closes: the reader would need the class with real exemplars it does not have (29 footnote asterisks), which is the '@' lesson again. Tables kept as variants; the numeric format stays (harmless, and right). |
| 🔬 Line model fine-tuned with a tight-gap synthetic set (`seq_line_v9a`, judge `linechoice9`) — the block split/merge residual FLAT, receipts down; not adopted; the block-spacing item closes (2026-09-17) | The block metric's 46% split/merge share moved with neither a rule between readings nor a gap prior inside one (rows above); the remaining hypothesis was data: the reader has seen few words that touch, so teach it with lines rendered at 0.05–0.18 em between words | `make_seq_data.py --word-gap 0.05 0.18 --words 3 8 --take 2 6 --max-width 1400` → `seq_synth_tightgap.npz` (150k windows, mean width 321, 45.6% touching letters; the stock faces, the Google Fonts checkout being gone since the reboot); `train_seq.py --init data/seq_line_en.npz` on the v7a recipe plus the set at natural weight (1.37M windows an epoch, 4 epochs, 344 min, held real 94.0% — no drift this time: a 150k-window set at natural weight is the v7a regime, not the 86-line one); judge re-harvested (2,735 pairs, P ≥ 0.5 takes 83.7% at 78.4% precision); three block sets, four sets, business | Against the live rows, char / word: blocks dev-8 99.0 / 96.3 → 99.0 / 96.2, blocks legal-8 96.5 / 94.0 → 96.5 / 93.6, blocks broad-30 98.2 / 94.9 → 98.2 / 95.0 — the target flat; dev-8 97.0 / 93.4 → 97.1 / 93.5, legal-8 94.8 / 89.1 → 94.9 / 89.5 (a tenth past its 0.3 range), broad-30 94.8 / 90.2 → 94.8 / 90.1, modern identical, business 97.3 / 93.6 → 97.1 / 92.9 with **receipts 96.5 / 92.3 → 95.7 / 89.9** and the other kinds within a tenth or two. The blocks' touching words did not move with 150k synthetic touching-word lines: either the block residual is not what the synthetic gaps render (real touching words are kerning pairs and ink bleed, not a uniform narrow gap), or the reader already reads the synthetic case and the residual is the judge's, which the inert arbiter row argues against. Either way the third mechanism aimed at it is flat, and the item closes as the block metric's residual. Recorded against the run: the receipts' monospace roll fell 2.4 word points, the THIRD line-model change to cost it ('@' fine-tune, letterhead fine-tune, this) — the Courier/Menlo roll is the domain most sensitive to any movement of the reader, having no real strips of its own in the harvests; a future line model should carry a monospace-roll harvest, which would need a real receipt corpus with truth. Not adopted; `seq_synth_tightgap.npz` kept as data, `--word-gap` kept in the renderer. |
| 📊 Two public corpora of REAL business documents on first contact: SROIE receipts and FUNSD forms — every engine reads them badly, ours worst; resolution is not the cause on the receipts (2026-09-17) | Every business number so far was our own templates'; the receipts' monospace roll fell on every line-model change for want of a real strip; the plan (2026-09-17) named real public corpora as the next lever | `make_external_sets.py`: SROIE (ICDAR 2019 task 1; the corrected mirror github.com/zzzDavid/ICDAR-2019-SROIE, 626 receipts with line boxes and transcripts, MIT for the corrections, competition data for research) → a seeded 60-receipt evaluation set and 566 for harvest, truth in reading order; FUNSD (Jaume et al. 2019, 199 scanned forms, non-commercial research) → the official 50 test forms at 2x and 149 training forms, truth = entities in reading order. Both in the UNLV layout under `data/ext/`, evaluation and harvest in separate directories (the guard by construction). `eval_unlv.py` and `eval_tesseract.py` unchanged; legacy tessdata re-fetched | Char / word (recall / precision): SROIE — neural 55.8 / 23.0 (52.0 / 53.2), classic 47.2 / 10.0, legacy Tesseract 56.2 / 29.4 (56.1 / 55.4), **Tesseract LSTM 64.0 / 40.3 (72.7 / 73.3)**; FUNSD at 2x — neural 53.0 / 26.7 (39.9 / 47.3), classic 35.9 / 12.2, legacy 54.8 / 32.0, **LSTM 66.4 / 47.2 (66.3 / 71.5)**. Diagnosis on one receipt (461 px wide, x-height 7.5 px against the reader's 10-px training floor): the bold header and the printed boilerplate read; the faded dot-matrix lines come out as junk from BOTH channels ('(JM0517726)' → classic 'I !MUSI u (lt)', reader "I17'6)") while the LSTM reads them. Upscaling tested: SROIE at 2x — neural **41.9 / −4.0** (recall 58.6, precision 50.0: more junk than words), LSTM 61.9 / 32.7; FUNSD at 3x — neural 58.3 / 31.0 (recall 49.4), LSTM 65.4 / 45.6. Resolution helps the forms a little and hurts the receipts; the receipts' problem is the thermal print itself — dotted, faded strokes no synthetic degradation renders and no harvest holds — and a page whose speckle our cleanup, tuned for 300-dpi paper, turns into words (precision 50%). The honest reading: on the documents the business set was standing in for, we are level with legacy Tesseract in characters and eight to thirteen points behind the LSTM, whose 4,500-font training run covered dot-matrix faces. The lever is the data: `harvest_boxes.py` cuts real line strips straight from the truth boxes (our pipeline cannot align these pages), with baseline and x-height from the box's own row profile (smoothed; held to a line box's proportions, the first probe having blown dot-matrix lines up threefold), checked on a contact sheet; the two harvests feed a line-model fine-tune (`seq_line_v10a`, next row). |
| 🔬 Line model fine-tuned with the REAL receipt and form strips at real weight 3 (`seq_line_v10a`) — SROIE +7.7 characters, template receipts +3.7 word, legal-8 −2.8 word; MIXED, not adopted; the judge exonerated (2026-09-18) | First contact (row above): the receipts are a domain no harvest held; `harvest_boxes.py` now gives 30,325 real receipt lines and 6,598 real form lines with truth | `train_seq.py --init data/seq_line_en.npz` on the v7a recipe plus `linesfull_sroie` and `linesfull_funsd` at real weight 3 (1.32M windows an epoch, 4 epochs, 310 min); judge re-harvested on the four UNLV harvests plus 56 receipts (`linechoice10`: 3,315 pairs, P ≥ 0.5 at 70.9% precision — the receipt pairs cost it seven points); then the model re-measured on legal-8, dev-8 and SROIE under the LIVE judge and under a judge without the receipt pairs (`linechoice10b`, 78.7%) to split model from judge | Against the live rows, char / word: **SROIE 55.8 / 23.0 → 63.5 / 31.5** (recall 52.0 → 58.4; legacy 56.2 / 29.4 passed, the LSTM's 64.0 characters matched, its 40.3 words not); FUNSD 53.0 / 26.7 → 54.9 / 28.5; business 97.3 / 93.6 → **97.6 / 94.2** with the templated **receipts 96.5 / 92.3 → 98.5 / 96.0** — real thermal-roll strips are what the synthetic roll was missing, as the three earlier receipt losses said; modern 93.8 / 88.8 → 93.7 / 88.7; blocks broad-30 98.2 / 94.9 → 98.2 / 94.8; dev-8 97.0 / 93.4 → 96.9 / 93.0; broad-30 94.8 / 90.2 → 94.8 / 89.9; **legal-8 94.8 / 89.1 → 93.3 / 86.3**. The three judges (live, with receipts, without) give legal-8, dev-8 and SROIE identical to the decimal — on a legal page all three take the same 2 of 32 lines — so the typewriter loss is the model's: 100k windows an epoch of dot-matrix print (33k real lines × 3) pulled the reader toward it and off the typewriter, whose own harvest is 38k lines × 3. Not adopted at this weight. `train_seq.py --lines-once` added (a file taken once an epoch, not `--real-weight` times) and `seq_line_v10b` under training: the same recipe with the two real sets at weight 1 — the domain at a third of the pull. Lesson for the record: the first real data from a new domain moves the reader by more than its share, and the weight is the dial, not the data. |
| ✅ The real-strip line model at weight 1 (`seq_line_v10b`) still costs the typewriter set; the weight-3 model becomes the RECEIPT PROFILE (`configs/neural_receipt.toml`) — ADOPTED as a profile, the standard profile unchanged (2026-09-18) | The weight was the dial (row above): a third of the pull should keep legal-8 while keeping some of the receipts' gain | `train_seq.py --lines-once` on the two real sets, otherwise the v10a run (1.26M windows an epoch, 4 epochs, 300 min); judge refitted with the receipt pairs (`linechoice10c`, P ≥ 0.5 at 73.0% precision); the same eight evaluations | Weight 1 against live, char / word: SROIE 55.8 / 23.0 → 60.0 / 26.7 (weight 3: 63.5 / 31.5); FUNSD 54.3 / 27.5; legal-8 94.8 / 89.1 → **94.3 / 87.5** (weight 3: 93.3 / 86.3); dev-8 96.9 / 93.0; broad-30 94.8 / 90.0; business 97.2 / 93.4 with the templated receipts 96.5 / 92.3 → 96.3 / 91.7 — the roll's gain gone with the weight. Half the receipts' gain, still a 1.6-word typewriter loss: the dial trades one domain for the other, and no setting holds both — dot-matrix receipt lines and typewriter lines are near neighbours (monospace, broken strokes) that the reader now separates worse. Decision: the standard neural profile keeps `seq_line_v7a` and its judge (nothing in it moves), and the weight-3 model ships as a second neural profile for receipts, `configs/neural_receipt.toml` — identical to `neural.toml` except `line_model_path = data/seq_line_receipt.npz` (= v10a) and `line_choice_path = data/linechoice_receipt.npz` (= linechoice10). Its rows: SROIE 63.5 / 31.5 (legacy 56.2 / 29.4, LSTM 64.0 / 40.3), business 97.6 / 94.2 (receipts 98.5 / 96.0), FUNSD 54.9 / 28.5. A profile is the project's own mechanism for a domain choice the caller can make (`doc_type` does it for layout); Tesseract ships per-domain models for the same reason. What it does not do is read receipts and pleadings with one model — `seq_line_v11a`, the v7a recipe from its v5s1 init with the real sets among the harvests (not a fine-tune of a converged model), is training to test whether a full run holds both. |
| ✅ BUG: the fitted judge never decided a line — the choice chain fell through to the endorsed-count rule; fixed, re-measured, the judge ADOPTED in fact (2026-09-18) | Three different judges and three thresholds gave the receipt profile identical numbers to the decimal, and on a receipt the reader's line was refused at P(reader) = 0.86 and taken at 0.08 | In `HybridDecode.run` the calibrated branch computed `take`, then an `if repair … elif likelihood … else` chain with no case for "calibrated" recomputed `take` by the endorsed count. Since the judge's adoption (2026-09-13) every neural-profile row was the ENDORSED-COUNT rule's; the rows that compared 'judge' with 'repair' compared endorsed-count with repair; the judge's fitted weights were never applied. The choice is now one method, `_choose_line`, each rule returning its own decision, with a test that a judge saying yes beats counts saying no and the reverse; `line_choose_rule = "calibrated"` unchanged in the profile. Every set re-measured under the neural profile, and the receipt profile on its sets | With the judge deciding, against the live rows (endorsed count), char / word: dev-8 97.0 / 93.4 → **97.4 / 94.5**; broad-30 94.8 / 90.2 → **95.1 / 91.0** (recall / precision 94.5 / 94.5 → 94.3 / 95.4); legal-8 94.8 / 89.1 → 94.4 / **90.2**; modern 93.8 / 88.8 → **94.1 / 90.5**; business 97.3 / 93.6 → **97.5 / 95.1** (precision 97.0 → 98.2; receipts 96.5 / 92.3 → 96.6 / 91.3); news-8 94.1 / 90.4 → **94.8 / 91.9**; mag-8 76.4 / 67.1 → **73.7 / 64.1**; blocks broad-30 98.2 / 94.9 → **97.2 / 94.6**, blocks dev-8 99.0 / 96.3 → 99.1 / **97.6**, blocks legal-8 96.5 / 94.0 → 95.9 / **95.1** (the same shape: words up, characters mixed). dev-8 re-read 97.4 / 94.5 after the choice was extracted into `_choose_line`, identical. Receipt profile: SROIE 63.5 / 31.5 → **67.2 / 35.8**, business 97.6 / 94.2 → 97.5 / 94.7. Word accuracy up 0.8 to 1.7 on every standard set, on business and on the newspapers; the judge takes more reader lines and the reader wins them by words while losing a few characters (legal-8 −0.4 character with +1.1 word, the blocks −1.0 character: the word-up / character-down shape recorded for every sharper reader), and the magazines lose three points, where the reader is worst. Adopted: the code now does what the config has said for five days, and the four sets are up in words with characters within half a point on three and down 0.4 on the typewriter set — recorded, not hidden. The headline set stands 0.4 character / 0.7 word from legacy Tesseract. Erratum for the rows of 2026-09-13/14 that name the judge: their numbers are real and reproducible, but the mechanism behind them was the endorsed-count rule; the judge's own effect is this row. Lesson for the house: a configuration value must be shown to change an output before its adoption row is written — the profile test now checks the decision method directly. |
| 📊 The receipt profile's choice on real receipts: reader-only, reader with numeric lines kept, judge (fixed), and the judge's threshold — reader-only reads real receipts best; the judge stays as the profile's default (2026-09-18) | On a real receipt the judge (then still the endorsed count) kept classic junk the reader had right; a receipts-only judge and thresholds of 0.3 and 0.15 changed nothing — the observation that led to the bug row above | `configs/neural_receipt.toml` with `--set decode.line_mode=pure`; with `line_pure_keep_numeric=true` (new option: in pure mode a data line — half its classic words numeric formats — keeps the classic reading); with a judge fitted on 545 receipt pairs only; with `line_choice_thresh` 0.3 / 0.15; on SROIE and business | SROIE, char / word: judge as shipped (endorsed count in fact) 63.5 / 31.5; receipts-only judge 63.5 / 31.5 and thresholds 0.3 / 0.15 identical (the tell); **reader-only 69.7 / 38.4** (recall 59.8, precision 60.3: 5.7 characters past the LSTM's 64.0, 1.9 words short of its 40.3); reader-only keeping numeric lines 66.8 / 35.1; the judge deciding (after the fix) 67.2 / 35.8. Business under the receipt profile: judge 97.6 / 94.2, reader-only 96.7 / 91.8 (templated receipts 98.5 / 96.0 → 95.9 / 86.0), keep-numeric 97.1 / 93.4, judge fixed 97.5 / 94.7. Reader-only is the best real-receipt setting by 2.5 characters over the fixed judge and costs the templated business pages 2.9 words: the classic decoder still reads clean Courier and the amount columns better than the reader, and the judge knows it. The profile keeps the judge, so that it reads a mixed business stream well; a caller with nothing but thermal-roll receipts sets `line_mode = "pure"` and gets the 69.7 — the profile's comments say so. `line_pure_keep_numeric` stays as an option (the compromise setting). |
| 🔬 One line model for every domain: the v7a recipe from its v5s1 init with the real receipt and form lines among the harvests (`seq_line_v11a`) — real receipts +10.9 word under the STANDARD profile, blocks +0.8, legal-8 −0.6, modern −0.3; pending its second seed (2026-09-19) | The fine-tunes traded the roll against the typewriter at every weight; a full run from the pre-line init, with the two domains present from the first epoch, is the fair test of whether one model holds both | `train_seq.py --init data/seq_en_v5s1.npz` on the v7a recipe (synth v1 + gfonts_v1 + gfonts_long + long2; five word harvests; seven whole-line harvests incl. `linesfull_sroie` and `linesfull_funsd`, all at real weight 3; 1.32M windows an epoch, 8 epochs, 616 min, held real 86.1% on the holdout that now includes the receipts and forms); judge re-harvested on the four UNLV sets (`linechoice11`, 79.6% decision accuracy; with receipt pairs `linechoice11s`, 77.1%, not used); the eight evaluations with the judge deciding (post-fix rows as the baseline) | Against the fixed-judge rows, char / word: **SROIE 55.7 / 22.9 → 65.0 / 33.8** (the receipt profile reads 67.2 / 35.8; legacy 56.2 / 29.4); FUNSD 53.0 / 26.7 → 54.8 / 29.2; dev-8 97.4 / 94.5 → 97.3 / 94.8; broad-30 95.1 / 91.0 → 95.2 / 90.7; **legal-8 94.4 / 90.2 → 93.9 / 89.6**; modern 94.1 / 90.5 → 94.0 / 90.2; business 97.5 / 95.1 → **97.7 / 95.3** (templated receipts 96.6 / 91.3 → 97.9 / 93.6); **blocks broad-30 97.2 / 94.6 → 98.3 / 95.4** (the character point the judge cost on the blocks comes back). Closer to holding than any fine-tune: the typewriter loss is 0.6 word (the fine-tunes: 2.8 and 1.6) against a recipe seed range of 0.3, and modern moves 0.3 against a range of 0.0; broad-30's 0.3 is inside its 0.5. Not adopted on one seed — the two-seed door: `seq_line_v11s2` (seed 2, same chain) is training. If both seeds hold legal-8 within its range, the model takes the standard profile and the receipt profile goes; if both lose it, the two-profile arrangement stands and the receipt profile's model becomes v11a (better on the forms and the blocks than v10a). Either way the finding stands that a full run carries the new domain at a fraction of the fine-tune's cost to its neighbour. |
| 📊 One line model for every domain, second seed (`seq_line_v11s2`) — the two seeds agree: real receipts +11–12 word and blocks +1 under the standard profile, modern −0.3/−0.5 word and legal-8 −0.4/−0.5 character on both; NOT adopted under the four-set rule, the trade put to the owner (2026-09-19) | Two-seed door of the model rule | The v11 chain with `--seed 2` (614 min, held real 86.3%); judge `linechoice11s2` (77.4%); the eight evaluations | Seed 1 / seed 2 against the fixed-judge live rows, char / word: SROIE 55.7 / 22.9 → 65.0 / 33.8 / **65.0 / 35.0**; FUNSD 53.0 / 26.7 → 54.8 / 29.2 / 52.3 / 29.0; dev-8 97.4 / 94.5 → 97.3 / 94.8 / 97.4 / 94.6; broad-30 95.1 / 91.0 → 95.2 / 90.7 / 95.1 / 91.1; **legal-8 94.4 / 90.2 → 93.9 / 89.6 / 94.0 / 90.1**; **modern 94.1 / 90.5 → 94.0 / 90.2 / 93.9 / 90.0**; business 97.5 / 95.1 → 97.7 / 95.3 / **97.7 / 95.6**; blocks broad-30 97.2 / 94.6 → 98.3 / 95.4 / **98.4 / 95.8**. Both seeds move the same way everywhere that matters: the real receipts gain eleven to twelve word points with no profile switch, the blocks a point, business half a point, dev-8 and broad-30 hold; modern loses 0.3 and 0.5 word (its recipe range is 0.0) and the typewriter set 0.4–0.5 character on both seeds, its word within range on seed 2. A systematic loss on two standard sets is what the rule refuses, and the rule stands: the standard profile keeps `seq_line_v7a`; the receipt profile keeps `v10a` (67.2 / 35.8 on the receipts against v11's 65.0). Recorded for the owner's decision, because the trade is unlike the ones the rule was written for: the earlier refusals (v6a, v6c seed 1) traded standard sets for press sets that are measured only; this one trades half a word point on templated modern pages and half a character on the typewriter set for twelve word points on real receipts under the profile a caller gets by default, with Tesseract still four and nineteen points behind on the two sets that pay. If the owner takes it, `seq_line_v11s2` and `linechoice11s2` become the standard profile's reader and judge and the receipt profile is retired; if not, this row is the record and the receipt profile is the route. |
| ✅ One line model for every domain ADOPTED — `seq_line_v11s2` = `data/seq_line_en.npz`, judge `linechoice11s2` = `data/linechoice.npz`; the receipt profile retired (2026-09-19) | The owner's decision on the two-seed row above: twelve word points on real receipts by default against half a word point on modern and half a character on the typewriter set | Live files swapped (previous kept as `seq_line_v7a.npz`, `linechoice7_unlv.npz`); `configs/neural_receipt.toml` removed — its model `seq_line_receipt.npz` (= v10a) stays as a variant for anyone reading nothing but thermal rolls; the rows not in the chain (news-8, mag-8, blocks dev-8 and legal-8, reader-only on SROIE) measured under the adopted files; dev-8 re-read as the identity check | The standard profile's rows are now seed 2's (char / word): dev-8 97.4 / 94.6, broad-30 95.1 / 91.1 (94.6 / 95.3), legal-8 94.0 / 90.1, modern 93.9 / 90.0 (92.5 / 96.5), business 97.7 / 95.6 (97.4 / 98.3; receipts 97.9 / 93.7), SROIE 65.0 / 35.0 (56.7 / 58.6), FUNSD 52.3 / 29.0 (40.0 / 51.1), blocks broad-30 98.4 / 95.8, news-8 95.4 / 92.5 (96.1 / 95.2), mag-8 77.0 / 68.5 (91.8 / 86.3) — both above their pre-judge rows — blocks dev-8 99.2 / 97.3 and legal-8 96.6 / 95.7; dev-8 re-read identical. Reader-only decoding on the receipts under the new model: **72.1 / 42.6** (60.0 / 62.0) — past the LSTM's 64.0 / 40.3 in both columns, the first real set where a plain decoding beats it outright; the judge's default keeps a mixed stream's business rows and is the profile's setting. An exception to the four-set rule, taken knowingly and written down: the rule's refusals had traded standard sets for sets we only measure; this trade is against two standard sets by amounts within a point, for a real domain the default profile now reads twelve word points better and Tesseract's legacy engine reads six characters worse. The receipt profile goes because one model now carries the roll within two characters of the fine-tuned one; the reader-only setting (`line_mode = "pure"`) remains the receipts-only option and its number under the new model is in the follow-up cell. Release v0.4.0 carries the new reader and judge in the bundle. |
| 📊 The forms' residual is recognition of blurred low-resolution type, not layout — the degradation model gains a low-resolution scan term; `seq_line_v12a` under training (2026-09-19) | FUNSD is the one real set where the LSTM leads by a wide margin (66.4 / 47.2 against our 52.3 / 29.0); a census before any training, as for the business pages | `--dump` of the 50 forms under the adopted model, difflib census by error class, then recall of every truth word by its FUNSD field label (question / answer / header / other) from the annotation JSON; one form's line overlay and per-line classic / reader readings; the forms re-measured at 3x under the new model. Then `Degradation.downsample` (area-average down by 1.6–3x and bilinear back, before the optics blur; `sample_theta` applies it to a share of degraded windows, held so the scanned x-height stays ≥ 4 px; `make_seq_data.py --lowres-frac`; `tests/test_words.py`) and `seq_synth_lowres.npz` (150k lines of 3–8 words at 60% low-resolution, 361 s) | Census: 81% of the word errors are multi-word spans where the truth's ENTITY order (key, then value, in annotation order) and our line order differ — a convention penalty on the edit-distance columns, so recall and precision are the honest columns here; recall of truth words is flat across field types (question 49.6%, answer 46.7%, other 44.8%, header 60.2%) — not a handwriting or a field-type effect. On a sample form the line finder places every field correctly and both channels misread clean-to-the-eye type: 'COMPETITIVE' → 'COMPETTNVE' (reader; the classic had it and the judge took the reader), 'Kool Waterfall' → 'Kool ta' (classic; the reader had it). The forms are 754 px wide, a letter page at 72 dpi read at 2x: an x-height of 10–12 px whose strokes are a bilinear blur of 5–6 px originals, a blur of roughly twice the 6%-of-x-height cap the degradation model allows. At 3x under the adopted model: 52.3 / 29.0 → **58.0 / 33.5** (recall 40.0 → 49.4) — resolution is a lever on this set and the reader has never seen the regime. So the model gets the regime as data, the way the receipts got theirs: the v11 recipe (seed 2) plus the low-resolution set among its synthetic sets, a full run, `seq_line_v12a`, with the judge and the eight evaluations to follow. If it lifts the forms without moving the four sets, the FUNSD row moves and the standard set at 2x stays the row's convention (3x recorded beside it); the forms' entity-order penalty stays as the metric's residual. |
| 🔬 Line model with the low-resolution synthetic set among its synthetic data (`seq_line_v12a`) — the forms FLAT, receipts down; not adopted (2026-09-20) | The forms census (row above): blurred 72-dpi type the reader never saw; 3x upscaling lifted the forms six characters, so give the reader the regime as rendered data | The v11 recipe (seed 2) plus `seq_synth_lowres.npz` (150k lines of 3–8 words, 60% scanned at 1.6–3x below rendering resolution and resampled back) among the synthetic sets: 1.47M windows an epoch, 8 epochs, 720 min, held real 85.6%; judge `linechoice12` (77.9%); the eight evaluations | Against the live rows, char / word: **FUNSD 52.3 / 29.0 → 52.7 / 29.2** (recall 40.0 → 40.2) — the target unmoved; SROIE 65.0 / 35.0 → 63.7 / 32.8; dev-8 97.4 / 94.6 → 97.2 / 95.1; broad-30 95.1 / 91.1 → 95.2 / 91.4; legal-8 94.0 / 90.1 → 94.2 / 89.9; modern 93.9 / 90.0 → 94.0 / 90.0; business 97.7 / 95.6 → 97.5 / 95.4; blocks broad-30 98.4 / 95.8 → 98.2 / 95.5. Everything within its seed range except the receipts (−2.2 word) and the forms flat: a downsample-and-resample of clean rendered type is not what a fax machine does to type — the fax's blur is anisotropic, thresholded at the sender, dithered by the receiver, and then JPEG-compressed by the archive — and the reader learned a regime that does not occur. The same lesson as the tight-gap set (2026-09-17): a synthetic imitation of a real degradation transfers only when it is the degradation, and the two real regimes we have moved (typewriter, thermal roll) each moved on REAL strips. Not adopted; `Degradation.downsample` stays in the model (it is a true term of a scanner, and cheap), `seq_synth_lowres` kept as data. The forms' next mechanism is therefore preprocessing, not data: the 3x result says the reader can read these pages when they arrive at a working x-height, so a page-level magnify keyed on the MEASURED x-height (median component height before layout) is the honest test — the internal magnify measured negative once (2026-09-0x) on small type inside a normal page, and hurt the receipts at 2x page-wide; keyed on x-height it would fire on the forms and not on either. |
| 🔬 A page-level magnify keyed on the measured type size (`cleanup/magnify.py`, slot `magnify`, impl `xheight`) — forms +2 characters, receipts −11 words, the four sets untouched; kept as an OPTION, off in every profile (2026-09-20) | The forms at 3x read six characters better (row above), so bring a small-type page up to a working size from its own measurement rather than from a caller's flag | A new first slot in every profile: median height of glyph-like connected components on an Otsu binarization (area 6–4,000 px, aspect within 1:8); below `target_px` the grayscale page is resampled (bicubic) by the ratio, capped at 3x, and the dpi scaled with it; `target_px = 0` off (the profiles' setting). `tests/test_magnify.py`: a 14-px rendering is scaled, a 40-px one is not. Measured with `--set magnify.target_px=20` on the forms, the receipts, the four sets and business | Char / word against live: **FUNSD 52.3 / 29.0 → 54.3 / 29.7** (recall 40.0 → 42.6); **SROIE 65.0 / 35.0 → 60.7 / 23.5** (recall 56.7 → 57.6, precision 58.6 → 58.1 — the words are found, the edit distance collapses: doubled junk and split lines); dev-8, legal-8, broad-30, modern, business identical to the decimal — the measure fires on no 300-dpi page. The forms gain two characters, not the six of a flat 3x, because their measured scale comes out near 1.3 (a 2x FUNSD page's glyph-height median is about 15 px against the 20-px target); the receipts, whose median is 10–12 px, are scaled 1.7–2x and read as badly as at the fixed 2x of 2026-09-17 under the old reader — magnification is not what the roll needs (its strips are normalised to 13 px anyway by the reader; what changes is the classic segmentation and the layout, which produce more lines and more junk words). No default holds both; as an option a caller who knows the input is a low-resolution FORM sets `target_px`. The forms item closes at its ceiling with the data we have: recognition of faxed type reachable by resolution (3x: 58.0 / 33.5) but not by a default rule or by rendered data (`seq_line_v12a`, flat). |
| 📊 Where the time goes: a per-stage profile of a letter and a business page (`scripts/profile_page.py`), and three identity-preserving changes in the recognizer — a letter 18.5 → 17.4 s (2026-09-20) | The neural profile reads a letter in ten to fifteen seconds against Tesseract's two; aim the work at the stage that costs | `profile_page.py` times every stage on one page (two runs, the first warming the caches) and prints the decoder's counters; `cProfile` inside the recognize stage for the functions. Changes, each tested equal to what it replaced and checked by a dev-8 dump identical to the previous text: the outline feature walk vectorised (`np.interp` along the polyline in place of a per-point search), the outline ratings of a glyph's candidate classes computed from ONE evidence matrix over their joint segment bank (`OutlineMatcher.ratings`, banks cached per class tuple), and the per-segment top-L sums from a partition of the top rows in place of a full column sort | A UNLV letter (8739_001, 411 words) under the neural profile: **18.54 s** = recognize.prototypes 8.81 (47%), decode.hybrid 3.23 + 3.18 (the two decode passes, before and after adaptation: 35%), adapt 1.57, everything else under 0.4 s each; the same page under classic 13.53 s (recognize 8.34, two decode passes 2.2). A templated invoice 7.28 s. The torch backend as configured is SLOWER — 28.84 s, the decoder 8.4 s a pass — because it runs one window at a time and pays the launch cost 419 times; batching the windows is the fix, not yet done. Inside recognize (cProfile, 11.1 s): the outline opinion 4.8 s (`evidence` 1.9, ratings and their sorts 1.5, outline features 1.2), glyph features 3.3 s (hole counts 1.1), confidence chopping 3.0 s (features re-extracted for every piece), GED 1.25 s over 50k Python calls. After the three changes recognize reads 7.6 s and the letter **17.4 s**; the text of every dev-8 page identical at each step. What is left, in order of size: the two decode passes (a second full decode after adaptation, 3.2 s each: the candidates could be re-scored rather than re-decoded), the outline evidence kernel (an N×M exp over every segment of every candidate class), the chop stage's feature re-extraction, GED in Python, and the torch backend's batching. None is a rule change; each is an engineering item with the same identity guard. |
| 📊 The receipts' word errors by kind under the adopted model (2026-09-20) | We lead the LSTM on real receipts in characters and trail it by five words under the judge; where are the words? | `--dump` of the 60 SROIE evaluation receipts under the neural profile, difflib census by error class against the line-level truth | 4,922 word errors of 6,663 words: **multi-word spans 73%** — whole lines the judge kept from the classic decoder as junk ('(JM0517726) NO 122.124 JALAN' → "I !MUSI u (lt) JJC) 12:' | ,4 JALAI("), the reader-only setting's gain in one class; **case 7%** — the bold header 'tan woon yann' for 'TAN WOON YANN' and 'Qty' for 'QTY', all-lower-case words on an all-capitals page that `caps_page_repair` leaves alone because it upper-cases mixed-case words only; capitals misread 5% ('TEL' → 'ILL', 'GST' → 'SSI'); extra 5%, missing 4%, numeric 3% ('07-3523888' → '67.V43?88'), split/merge 1.6%, punctuation 1.5%. Two shapes follow: `caps_page_all` (an all-lower-case word of two or more letters on a capitals page is upper-cased too; measured in the next row) and, for the 73%, the judge's receipt-line problem already recorded — reader-only decoding reads the receipts at 72.1 / 42.6. The digit misreads on the faded roll are the reader's and stay. |
| ✅ A modularity pass on the beam decoder: post-passes, sequence-scorer terms and line geometry moved into their own modules, each move identical on dev-8 (2026-09-20) | `decode/beam.py` had grown to 2,389 lines through the week's rules; legibility is a deliverable | Three mechanical moves, each by the AST (method blocks copied verbatim, `self.`/`cls.` references to moved names rewritten, thin aliases left on `BeamDecode` so every caller and test keeps working), each followed by the full suite and a dev-8 dump compared file by file with the dump before it: `decode/postpass.py` (line and sentence case, word-case coherence, stray digits and letters, dehyphenation, the bullet and bar tests, the digit-twin table), `decode/seqterm.py` (the `SeqTerms` mixin: line posterior, window rescoring, segment re-read, page word list, the scorer cache), `decode/segment.py` (gap band, line x-height, word segmentation, k-best merge, numeric punctuation). The choice between the two readings had already become `HybridDecode._choose_line` (2026-09-18), the shape repairs `decode/formats.py` (2026-09-17) | dev-8 97.4 / 94.6 and every page's text identical after each move; the suite 156 tests; `beam.py` 2,389 → 1,532 lines. Two slips on the way, both caught by the suite before any dump: a regex meant to lift one constant ran on to the next closing brace (the file was restored from the commit and the move redone from the AST), and a staticmethod's delegate lost an argument. Recorded so the next mover uses the AST from the start. The two instance methods of the segmentation that use the decoder's state (`_best_gap_variant`, `_join_decoded`) stay in the class. |
| 🔬 All-lower-case words upper-cased on a capitals page too (`caps_page_all`) — INERT on the receipts, kept as an option, off (2026-09-20) | The receipts census: 7% of the word errors were all-lower-case words on all-capitals receipts ('tan woon yann', 'Qty'), which `caps_page_repair` leaves alone | `uppercase_caps_page(..., all_lower=True)`: an alphabetic word of two or more letters that is entirely lower case is upper-cased when the page is ≥ 90% capitals; `tests/test_formats.py`; measured on SROIE, business by kind and dev-8 | SROIE 65.0 / 35.0 → 65.1 / 35.0; business 97.7 / 95.6 identical (templated receipts 97.9 / 93.7 → 98.0 / 93.8); dev-8 identical. The rule barely fires: on a real receipt the junk lines the judge keeps carry enough lower-case letters that the page falls under the 90% floor, so the same lines that hold 73% of the errors also switch this rule off. Inert; the option stays for a clean capitals page, off in every profile. |
| 📊 The Library of Congress "By the People" transcriptions as a printed-text source: a 434k-page human-transcribed corpus surveyed and three printed campaigns probed — the Legal Reports read within two characters of the LSTM untrained; historical letterpress and playbills far behind (2026-09-20) | The one corpus on the jina-ocr training list that fits every rule (public domain, HUMAN labels, bulk download); is there printed material with truth in it, and how do we read it? | The comprehensive package (loc.gov item 2026395156, CSV, 2026-08-28): 434,006 completed pages, 24 campaigns; `Medium` is empty for most rows, so the campaign is the typed/printed proxy — manuscripts 309k pages, printed campaigns: Early Copyright Title Pages 97k (1790–1870 letterpress), African American Perspectives in Print 11k, Historical Legal Reports 10k (20th-century typescript and print), Federal Theatre playbills 8k. `make_btp_set.py` draws pages from a campaign into our layout (IIIF JPEG → grayscale TIFF, the volunteers' transcription as truth); 40 pages each from three campaigns; neural profile and the LSTM on the same pages | Char / word (recall / precision): **Legal Reports — neural 74.7 / 55.5 (62.4 / 62.8), LSTM 76.8 / 64.5 (68.9 / 75.3)**; Copyright title pages — neural 53.9 / 11.2 (53.4 / 43.6), LSTM 75.0 / 59.2 (71.5 / 71.6); Playbills — neural 50.1 / 17.1 (62.3 / 62.5), LSTM 67.7 / 49.4 (77.3 / 74.7). The transcriptions are per page in the volunteers' order, with their line breaks, so the edit-distance columns carry an order convention (as on the forms); recall and precision are the honest columns. The Legal Reports are the domain this engine is for — typewritten and printed office pages, the pleadings' cousins — and it reads them within two characters of the LSTM having never seen one; 9,807 such pages with human truth are a harvest the line matcher (`harvest_lines.py`, which aligns the pipeline's lines to truth lines) can use as it stands, since the transcripts keep the lines. The title pages and playbills are display letterpress of another century: a domain the project does not target, recorded like the press sets. Next: a Legal Reports harvest split (evaluation stems excluded) downloaded and harvested, and the line model's recipe extended with it — the receipts' path. |
| 🔬 Line model with the Legal Reports harvest among its real strips (`seq_line_v13a`) — Legal Reports +3.0 characters / +4.7 words, past the LSTM's characters; legal-8 −0.6 word and the blocks −0.9; MIXED, not adopted on one seed (2026-09-20) | The By the People probe: typescript and printed office pages with human truth, read within two characters of the LSTM untrained; 9,807 pages to harvest | `make_btp_set.py --exclude-dir` (600 harvest pages disjoint from the 40 evaluation pages) + `harvest_lines.py --no-guard --line-out` (37,960 word strips, 1,993 whole lines, 496 matched pages, 18% of words decoded wrong); the v11 recipe (seed 2) plus both files at real weight 3 (1.45M windows an epoch, 8 epochs, 649 min, held real 85.4%); judge `linechoice13` (77.3%); nine evaluations | Against the live rows, char / word: **Legal Reports 74.7 / 55.5 → 77.7 / 60.2** (recall 62.4 → 67.0; the LSTM 76.8 / 64.5 — its characters passed, its words 4.3 ahead); SROIE 65.0 / 35.0 → 64.9 / 34.7; FUNSD 52.3 / 29.0 → 52.4 / 29.1; dev-8 97.4 / 94.6 → 97.4 / 94.5; broad-30 95.1 / 91.1 → 95.1 / 91.0; **legal-8 94.0 / 90.1 → 93.7 / 89.5**; modern 93.9 / 90.0 → 94.0 / 90.1; business 97.7 / 95.6 → 97.7 / 95.6 (templated receipts 97.9 / 93.7 → 98.0 / 95.3); **blocks broad-30 98.4 / 95.8 → 97.4 / 94.9**. The new domain gains what the receipts gained and the standard sets hold, except the two that keep paying for every line-model change: the typewriter set (−0.6 word) and the bare letter blocks (−0.9). The blocks' loss is the larger and the odder — the Legal Reports are typed office pages, not letter paragraphs — and on one seed it cannot be told from the recipe's own spread (the two v11 seeds differed by 0.4 word on the blocks). Not adopted on one seed; a second seed is the two-seed door if the Legal Reports are wanted in the default profile (12 h of GPU); otherwise the harvest stands as data, the first from a human-transcribed public archive, and the row records that the corpus works: an untrained-on domain moved 4.7 words on 600 harvested pages. |
| ✅ Legal Reports run, second seed (`seq_line_v13b`, seed 3) — every standard set within its range, the Legal Reports +1.4 word, the receipts +0.9; ADOPTED as the standard profile's reader and judge; seed 1's block and typewriter losses were the draw (2026-09-21) | Two-seed door for the v13 recipe; the owner asked for the seed with an early look and the option to abort | The v13 chain with `--seed 3` (649 min, held real 84.3%); an epoch-4 checkpoint read mid-run on legal-8, the letter blocks and the Legal Reports (92.7 / 87.1, 98.0 / 94.7, 73.5 / 53.8 — a half-trained model under another model's judge; no abort); judge `linechoice13b` (78.4%); the nine evaluations | Seed 3 against the live rows, char / word: Legal Reports 74.7 / 55.5 → **75.6 / 56.9** (recall 63.9); SROIE 65.0 / 35.0 → **66.0 / 35.9**; FUNSD 52.3 / 29.0 → 53.6 / 29.3; dev-8 97.4 / 94.6 → 97.2 / 94.5; broad-30 95.1 / 91.1 → 95.2 / 91.1; legal-8 94.0 / 90.1 → 94.2 / 89.8; modern 93.9 / 90.0 → 94.0 / **90.4**; business 97.7 / 95.6 → 97.6 / 95.7 (templated receipts 97.9 / 93.7 → **98.3 / 95.4**); blocks broad-30 98.4 / 95.8 → 98.3 / 95.6. The two seeds: Legal Reports +4.7 / +1.4 word, legal-8 −0.6 / −0.3, blocks −0.9 / −0.2 — the standard sets' moves on seed 1 were the draw, and the new set's own seed spread is 3.3 words, wider than any standard set's, as a 40-page set of long typescript pages read at 57% will be. Seed 3 passes the rule as written (legal-8 −0.3 at its range, blocks −0.2 inside 0.4, dev-8 −0.1 inside 0.1) and gains on four real sets, so it is the live reader: `data/seq_line_en.npz` = v13b, `data/linechoice.npz` = linechoice13b, the previous pair kept as `seq_line_v11s2` / `linechoice11s2`. What it establishes: a human-transcribed public archive is a working harvest source for this engine, and the recipe carries a fourth real domain (letters, pleadings, receipts, typed office pages) with the first three holding. Under the adopted files: news-8 95.4 / 92.7 (live 95.4 / 92.5), mag-8 76.9 / 67.3 (77.0 / 68.5, measured only), blocks dev-8 98.4 / 96.3 (99.2 / 97.3 — the one row down by a word, on 1,312 words), blocks legal-8 96.8 / 95.9 (96.6 / 95.7); reader-only on the receipts **73.4 / 44.5** (72.1 / 42.6 before; the LSTM 64.0 / 40.3). The dev-8 identity check caught a config rewrite that had dropped the reader's mode and rule (six rows re-measured; a test now pins the profile's decoder identity). |
| 🔬 A batched window prefetch for the sequence scorer (`seq_prefetch`) — halves the torch decoder's time, which is still slower than numpy's; a fraction of a second in numpy; kept as an option, off (2026-09-21) | The profile of 2026-09-20: the torch backend read a letter in 28.8 s against numpy's 18.5 because the decoder handed the scorer one window at a time and paid a launch per window | `SeqTerms._seq_prefetch`: after a line is segmented, every segment's whole-span window is scored in ONE `log_probs` call (the scorer batches by width) and put in the line's window cache; the gap variants still score on demand. Measured on the profiled letter under both backends and by a dev-8 dump against the last identity dump | Letter, numpy: decode 3.23 + 3.18 → 3.08 + 3.08 s (total 17.4 → 17.5 s, noise); torch: decode 8.4 + 8.4 → 5.25 + 5.14 s (total 28.8 → 21.8 s). dev-8 97.2 / 94.5, the same mean, but SIX of the eight pages' texts differ from the identity dump: a padded batch and a single window are not bit-identical through the recurrent layers, and a handful of near-tie words flip either way — so this is a measured change, not an identity-preserving one, and the four-set run it would need buys 0.15 s a page in numpy. Not adopted: numpy stays the default backend and `seq_prefetch` stays off. What the torch number says: even with the segment windows batched, the reader's line calls (42 lines, chunked) and the variants' windows keep the GPU launch-bound; a GPU path that beats numpy on a page needs the whole page's windows in one or two calls, a restructuring of the decode loop rather than a prefetch. Recorded as the throughput leg's finding; the CPU path at 17.4 s stands. |
| ✅ The judge refitted with the page-level feature and the receipt pairs (`linechoice14s`) — real receipts +5.0 characters / +5.2 words (past the LSTM in both), legal-8 +0.7 word, Legal Reports +1.1 characters; ADOPTED as the live judge (2026-09-21) | The receipts census: 73% of their word errors were classic junk lines the judge kept; a judge that can tell a receipt roll from a letter should stop keeping them | `page_unendorsed_frac` (the share of the page's classic lines with no endorsed word) appended to the judge's features, computed once per page in `HybridDecode.run` and threaded through `_choose_line`; judges fitted without it load with zero weight (decisions unchanged, the compatibility that let the feature land before the refit). Pairs re-harvested under the live reader (`harvest_line_choice.py` on the four UNLV sets, 2,736 pairs, and 56 receipts, 545 pairs); two fits: `linechoice14` (UNLV only, 79.0% precision on a page-disjoint holdout) and `linechoice14s` (with the receipt pairs, 76.7%); both measured on SROIE, business by kind, the four sets and the Legal Reports | `14s` against the live rows, char / word: **SROIE 66.0 / 35.9 → 71.0 / 41.1** (recall 57.6 → 60.9; the LSTM 64.0 / 40.3 — passed in both columns under the judge's default, where reader-only decoding had been the only way); **legal-8 94.2 / 89.8 → 94.5 / 90.5**; Legal Reports 75.6 / 56.9 → **76.7 / 57.2**; dev-8 97.2 / 94.5 → 97.3 / 94.5; broad-30 95.2 / 91.1 → 95.2 / 91.2; business 97.6 / 95.7 identical (templated receipts 98.3 / 95.4 → 98.4 / 95.7); modern 94.0 / 90.4 → 93.9 / 90.2. `14` (no receipt pairs): SROIE 67.4 / 37.5, legal-8 89.8, the rest the same — the receipt pairs teach the page feature what a roll looks like. Adopted: the one page-level fact lets the judge weigh a classic line's lack of endorsement by the page it is on, and the typewriter set gains with the receipts because its pages share the shape (monospace, few lexicon words). Recorded against it: modern −0.2 word, at the edge of what a judge refit moves; and a fitted judge's holdout precision fell from 79.0 to 76.7 with the receipt pairs while every set improved — the holdout is UNLV-heavy and the receipts are where the new feature works. `data/linechoice.npz` = 14s; 13b kept. Under the adopted judge: dev-8 re-read 97.3 / 94.5 (identity), news-8 95.5 / 92.8, mag-8 76.7 / 66.8, FUNSD 53.6 / 29.3 → 55.3 / 30.0, blocks broad-30 98.3 / 95.6, dev-8 98.4 / 96.4, legal-8 96.9 / 96.0 — nothing else moved by more than a tenth or two. |
| 🔬 The Rumor Project's scans carry the wrong resolution tag — a 400-dpi page read under a 300 tag harvested 2 lines in 75 pages at 3.6 min a page; the set builder now infers dpi from the page width and resamples pages above 300 (2026-09-22) | The third By the People campaign (Rumor Project: typed WWII rumour-control office pages) harvested nothing usable where the Legal Reports had yielded thousands of lines | `scripts/make_btp_set.py`: dpi inferred as image width / `--page-width-in` (8.5), pages above `--max-dpi` (300) resampled and tagged; the measured chain re-run on one page | The images are ~3,400 px wide (400 dpi on letter paper) and were tagged 300, so every 300-dpi-declared threshold (despeckle area, x-height bands, the reader's 32-px strip normalisation) saw type a third larger than declared and the classic engine spent its time on a page it could not read. Re-tagged, one page reads at 24.1 / 32.9 (recall 80.9, precision 56.0) — still poor, and the reason is the next row. The Legal harvest2 and NAWSA shards were built under the same tag and are worth re-harvesting; the eight shards already in the v14 run stay as they are (a model that sees some over-scale strips is not harmed; the harvest just found fewer lines than it could have). Lesson recorded: a public archive's TIFF header is not a resolution measurement; the page width is. |
| 🔬 Where a dense typescript page's four minutes go — Python CTC loops: `prefix_beam_search` 89 s over 1,152 calls, `_beam_word_mode` 83 s, the scorer's `log_probs` 43 s (numpy GRU 32 s), `ctc_nll_batch` 31 s; 646 million function calls for one 216-s decode pass (2026-09-22) | The Rumor page took 3.6 min under the harvest; what the time is | `scripts/profile_page.py` on the re-tagged Rumor page (`935373.tif`), the neural profile | The classic recognizer and the reader's forwards are not the cost; the per-word beam is: 1,152 prefix-beam calls at 77 ms each (23.6 s of them in the Python loop body, 30 s in `add`/`lse` called 52 and 154 million times), 4,918 `_beam_word_mode` calls at 17 ms, and 5,659 `ctc_nll_batch` calls at 5 ms. On the letter (17.4 s) these same loops are a third of the time; on 176 lines of a page with a dark frame (next row) they are all of it. What it says for the throughput leg: the prefix beam's inner loop (`add`, `lse`) is the one place a vectorised or compiled inner step would pay across every page, ahead of any GPU work — the GPU path is launch-bound (2026-09-21 row) and the CTC loops never touch it. Measured only; recorded for the throughput leg's queue. |
| ✅ The Legal Reports evaluation set rebuilt at its measured resolution — the same 40 pages, dpi inferred from the page width and resampled to 300: neural **76.7 / 57.2 → 81.0 / 60.9**, the LSTM 76.8 / 64.5 → 78.8 / 66.3; ADOPTED as the set (2026-09-22) | The dpi-tag finding above: the Legal Reports are ~3,100–3,200 px wide, so a letter page is at ~365 dpi under a 300 tag, and their harvested x-heights (median 28.6 px) sit 22% above the UNLV legal harvest's (23.2) | `scripts/make_btp_set.py`'s inference applied to the existing draw (a retag copy: dpi = width / 8.5 in, LANCZOS resample to 300, truth copied); both engines re-measured on the same files; the old set kept as `eval_tag300` | +4.3 characters / +3.7 words for this engine and +2.0 / +1.8 for Tesseract's LSTM from the file header alone — every 300-dpi-declared threshold (despeckle area, x-height bands, the reader's 32-px normalisation, the classic engine's size gates) had seen type a fifth larger than declared. Against the LSTM the set now reads ahead by 2.2 characters and behind by 5.4 words (recall 66.6 vs 71.0): the character lead is real (typewriter strokes the reader knows) and the word deficit is the same layout-and-junk-line story as the receipts before the judge's page feature. The 600-page harvest that trained v13b and the 3,000-page expansion were built under the same tag; every harvest directory is re-tagged (`*_tag300` kept) and re-harvested on the training box for the next recipe. On the 100-page draw (`eval100`, disjoint from the harvest and the 40), corrected the same way: this engine 80.2 / 64.2 (recall 68.7, precision 68.6), the LSTM 81.6 / 70.8 (74.8, 79.8) — on the larger draw the LSTM leads in both columns, by 1.4 characters and 6.6 words, so the 40-page character lead is within the draw's spread and the honest statement is "level in characters, six or seven words behind". Lesson written into the builder's docstring: a public archive's TIFF header is not a resolution measurement; the page width is. |
| ❌ Every By the People shard at the full real weight (`seq_line_v14a`: the v13 recipe plus eight shards — 3,000 Legal Reports pages and 1,000 NAWSA pages, 208k word strips — at x3, trained on the CUDA box in 59 min, held real 87.2%) — real receipts −6.3 / −8.8, news-8 −1.2 / −2.2, dev-8 −0.2 / −1.0, business −0.1 / −1.0; legal-8 +0.4 word, Legal Reports +0.8 word, FUNSD +0.8 char; NOT adopted (2026-09-22) | The LoC expansion: more of the domain that had just paid (v13b) | The v14 chain: `train_seq.py --lines <UNLV, SROIE, FUNSD, Legal 600> <8 shards> --real-weight 3 --seed 3 --batch 32` on the box; judge `linechoice15s` re-harvested under it (with the receipt pairs, 79.9% decision accuracy on the holdout); every set; two controls on the receipts (v14a under the live judge, and reader-only) | Against the live rows, char / word: dev-8 97.3 / 94.5 → 97.1 / 93.5 (97.1 / 93.6 under the live judge — the reader's loss, not the refit's); broad-30 95.2 / 91.2 → 95.1 / 91.0; legal-8 94.5 / 90.5 → 94.5 / **90.9**; modern 93.9 / 90.2 → 94.0 / 89.9; business 97.6 / 95.7 → 97.5 / 94.7; **SROIE 71.0 / 41.1 → 64.7 / 32.3** (live judge 65.2 / 32.2; reader-only 73.4 / 44.5 → 67.4 / 36.0 — the reader itself lost the receipts); FUNSD 55.3 / 30.0 → 56.1 / 30.0; Legal Reports (corrected set, frame off) 81.0 / 60.9 → 80.1 / **61.7**; news-8 95.5 / 92.8 → 94.3 / 90.6; mag-8 76.7 / 66.8 → 76.2 / 65.8; blocks broad-30 98.3 / 95.6 → 98.3 / 95.3. The held-real number was the best any run has posted (87.2 vs v13b's 84.3) and it measured the wrong thing: the held set is drawn per file, so it is now half typescript. What happened to the mix: the eight shards at x3 doubled the real word pool (208k against the 168k of UNLV + the Legal 600), so the letters fell from about 70% of the real strips to under 40% and the receipts from 11% to 6%, and the reader followed the pool — the same lesson as the 2026-09-18 row that introduced `--lines-once`, forgotten for one run. Recorded against the shards as well: they were harvested under the wrong dpi tag (row above), so the strips are a fifth over scale before the reader's own normalisation. Next: `seq_line_v15a`, the same recipe with the shards under `--lines-once` (running); then the re-tagged re-harvest (in progress on the box). |
| ❌ The shards once an epoch (`seq_line_v15a`: v14a's recipe with the eight shards under `--lines-once`, box-trained, held real 86.6%) — the receipts collapse exactly as under v14a (SROIE 64.9 / 32.4), so the weight was not the cause; NOT adopted; a bisect is running (2026-09-22) | The v14a row's diagnosis: the shards at x3 had shifted the domain shares | The same chain with the shards taken once (1.87M training windows against 2.09M); judge `linechoice16s` (receipt pairs in, 78.8% decision accuracy); the twelve evaluations | Against the live rows, char / word: dev-8 97.2 / 94.2 (−0.1 / −0.3); broad-30 95.2 / 91.0 (−0.2 word); legal-8 94.2 / 90.2 (−0.3 / −0.3); modern 94.0 / 90.1; business 97.6 / 95.0 (−0.7 word); **SROIE 64.9 / 32.4** (live 71.0 / 41.1; v14a 64.7 / 32.3); FUNSD 56.4 / 30.0 (+1.1 char); Legal Reports 80.4 / 61.7 (live, frame on, 81.1 / 61.1); news-8 95.3 / 92.1 (−0.7 word); mag-8 77.2 / **67.7** (+0.9); blocks 98.3 / 95.7. The receipt number is the same to a tenth whether the shards weigh one or three, which rules the mix out and leaves the two things v14a and v15a share and v13b does not: the shards' presence at all (208k strips harvested under the wrong dpi tag), and the training machine (the CUDA box; v13b was trained on the laptop's MPS backend, same torch 2.14, same data by checksum). Bisect running: `seq_line_v13c` = v13b's command byte for byte on the box (its first two epochs reproduce v13b's losses, 0.289 and 0.268); if its reader-only receipts hold at 73 / 44 the shards are the cause, if they fall to 67 / 36 the box is. Lesson pending the bisect; recorded now: the held-real figure rose again (86.6) while the receipts fell 9 words — the per-file held draw is not a domain guard, and no adoption question is answered by it. |
| 🔬 Bisect: v13b's command byte for byte on the CUDA box (`seq_line_v13c`) — receipts reader-only 71.9 / 42.2 against v13b's 73.4 / 44.5, dev-8 97.2 / 94.2 against 97.3 / 94.5: seed-spread moves, not the collapse; the box is sound and the wrong-dpi shards are the cause (2026-09-22) | v14a and v15a collapsed the receipts by 9 words whether the shards weighed one or three; they shared the shards and the training machine | The same data (checksums equal), the same command, `--device cuda`; per-epoch losses reproduce v13b's to three decimals (0.289, 0.268, 0.254 …), held real 84.3% = v13b's; then reader-only SROIE, SROIE under the live judge, dev-8 under the live judge | Reader-only SROIE 71.9 / 42.2 (v13b 73.4 / 44.5; v14a 67.4 / 36.0); under the live judge 69.2 / 38.8 (live 71.0 / 41.1); dev-8 97.2 / 94.2 (live 97.3 / 94.5). A re-run of the adopted recipe on another card sits 1.5 / 2.3 under the adopted seed on the receipts and 0.3 word under on dev-8 — the size of the seed spread recorded on 2026-09-21 — while the shard runs sit 6 / 8.5 under. So: the 208k word strips harvested under the 300 tag from ~365- and ~400-dpi scans (a fifth to a third over scale, and matched under a classic read that saw type a third larger than declared) hurt the small-type receipts regardless of their weight; the box's CUDA path trains the recipe as the laptop's MPS path does. Conclusions written down: (1) a harvest is only as good as its dpi, and an over-scale harvest is not neutral, it is toxic to the smallest type in the mix; (2) the training machine is cleared for adoption runs, seeds compared as before; (3) next is `seq_line_v15b` — v13b's recipe with the re-tagged, frame-cleared re-harvest (the Legal 600 replaced by its corrected harvest, the 3,000-page expansion, NAWSA and the Rumor Project) under `--lines-once`, queued behind the re-harvest on the box. |
| ✅ The prefix beam's frame loop flattened — string-keyed prefixes, parent-linked frame chains, libm on Python floats, in-place entry updates: 3.9x faster on the function with bit-identical output over 1,260 random strips and the dev-8 dump; no measurable change on a clean page, because the function is a percent or two of a clean page's decode (2026-09-22) | The dense-typescript profile above: 89 of 216 s in `prefix_beam_search` — but that was the 176-junk-line page; once the frame is cleared the same page decodes in 4 s a pass | `recognize/ctc.py` `prefix_beam_search`: the same additions in the same order (the pruning sort is stable on insertion order, so ties fall the same way); `math.log/exp` checked bit-identical to numpy's scalar path on 50k pairs; prefixes as `str` (a tuple recomputes its hash on every lookup; a str caches it); frames and emissions as (t, lp, parent) chains unrolled for the winner; a first pass that only swapped the arithmetic bought 1.11x, the flattening 1.24x, the keys and chains the rest | Function: 42.6 s → 10.9 s over the 1,260 cases (beam 4 / 8 / 12, with and without a word bonus, strips of 20–1,500 frames), zero mismatches. Pipeline: dev-8 dump identical on all eight pages (97.3 / 94.5); the letter 13.8 → 14.0 s total, decode 3.47 → 3.43 s a pass; the Rumor page 17.8 → 18.2 s, decode 4.18 → 4.21 (noise). Kept: it is faster, tested and reads as before. What it says about the throughput leg: the Python CTC loops dominate only pathological pages; on a clean letter the two decode passes (about 3.4 s each) are spread over `_beam_word_mode`, `ctc_nll_batch` and the scorer's forwards, and the classic recognizer's 4–5 s is the largest single stage — the earlier conclusion (2026-09-21) stands, and the next real throughput gain is structural (page-level batching of the scorer, or the evidence kernel), not a tighter inner loop. |
| ✅ Receipts census → declined-line rule (`line_drop_declined`): a classic line the reader declines, with no endorsed word and an x-height under half the page's median, is dropped — SROIE +0.4 word, FUNSD +0.7, Legal Reports +0.8, everything else identical; ADOPTED in the neural profile (2026-09-22) | The receipts' remaining errors, by census under the live profile (60 pages, 6,663 truth words): 24% of truth words sit on truth lines no output line matches, 14% are near misses (one or two characters), 9% dropped; of the output's 6,641 words 20% sit on lines matching no truth line. A per-line provenance census (`line_keep_alt`) of the 2,373 output lines: 975 reader lines matched, 376 classic lines matched, 43 judge errors (39 kept classic wrongly, 4 the reader), 173 both engines wrong, and **458 classic lines (2,213 words) the reader never reached** — declined for want of geometry, a strip too narrow, nothing emitted or confidence under the floor — at x-heights of 1–6 px: dashed rules and noise read as punctuation and letter salad | `HybridDecode`: at the two decline points, drop the classic words when the line is unendorsed (no lexicon word, no numeric format) and its x-height is under `line_drop_declined` × the page's median x-height; `_unendorsed` helper; `lines_dropped` scalar. Two forms measured on eleven sets: without the x-height qualifier, then with it at 0.5; then a feature dump of every declined unendorsed classic line on legal-8, business, the Legal Reports and modern (x-height ratio, classic p_correct, alnum fraction, truth match) | Unqualified, char / word against live: legal-8 94.5 / 90.5 → 94.9 / 91.6; **Legal Reports 81.1 / 61.1 → 81.6 / 64.0**; FUNSD 55.3 / 30.0 → 55.4 / 31.3; SROIE 71.0 / 41.1 → 71.2 / 41.7; mag-8 66.8 → 67.3; blocks 95.6 → 95.7; but **business 95.7 → 95.3** (invoices' recall 96.8 → 95.5, purchase orders 98.2 → 97.4) and **modern 90.2 → 89.5**; dev-8, broad-30, news-8 identical. Qualified at 0.5: SROIE 71.1 / 41.5, FUNSD 55.5 / 30.7, Legal Reports 81.4 / 61.9; dev-8, legal-8, business, modern identical. The dump explains both: the invoices' declined lines are SKUs (`g5883-70`, `apple.com/bill`) at the page's x-height that the classic engine reads right and nothing endorses; the legal-8, modern and Legal Reports lines the unqualified form removes are margin line numbers and page numbers (`1`, `4`, `16`, `70`) that the truth conventions omit — a truth-convention gain, not a recognition one, and not chased; the separable junk is the specks and dashes at 0.04–0.26 of the page's x-height with p_correct near zero, which the qualifier isolates. What the census says about the receipts' remaining 59 words in a hundred: the largest bucket is line-level — a quarter of truth lines have no output line within 35% edit distance (both engines wrong on a degraded line, or the line never segmented), the judge decides only 43 of 2,373 lines wrongly, and the near misses (`total→tolal`, `gst→ust`, `johor→iohor`) are the reader's character errors on small type; so the receipts' next lever is the line reader on small degraded type (a harvest at the correct scale, RESEARCH above) and the line finder, not the judge. |
| ❌ The corrected re-harvest (`seq_line_v15b`: v13b's recipe, the Legal 600 replaced by its corrected harvest, five corrected Legal shards and the Rumor Project under `--lines-once`; 1.69M windows, box, held real 86.9%) — receipts reader-only **67.1 / 35.9**, the same collapse as v14a (67.4 / 36.0) and v15a; dev-8 under the live judge 97.2 / 94.4; NOT adopted; the dpi was not the cause either (2026-09-23) | The bisect of 2026-09-22 cleared the box and left the shards; the shards' one known fault was their dpi | The chain as written in NETWORKS.md; the quick verdict (reader-only SROIE, dev-8 under the live judge) taken before the judge refit, and the rest of the chain stopped on it | Three runs now share the collapse — shards at x3 and the wrong dpi (v14a), at x1 and the wrong dpi (v15a), at x1 and the right dpi (v15b) — and two runs without the shards do not (v13b, v13c). Weight and scale are ruled out; what remains is the shards' presence at 170–210k typewriter strips against the Legal 600's 34k that v13b carries without harm. The reading this suggests: typewriter and thermal-receipt print compete for the same monospace hypotheses, and 200k typewriter lines against 27k receipt lines settle the contest — a domain-competition effect that the held-real figure (per file, now half typewriter) rewards and the receipts pay for. Bisect on volume running: `seq_line_v15c` = v13b's command plus ONE corrected Legal shard (34k strips) under `--lines-once`; if the receipts hold, the lever is the receipts' own weight (a per-file weight in the trainer) or more receipt data, not less typescript. |
| 🔬 Volume bisect (`seq_line_v15c`: v13b's command plus ONE corrected Legal shard, 34k word strips, once an epoch) — receipts reader-only **71.9 / 41.4** (v13b 73.4 / 44.5, v13c 71.9 / 42.2: seed spread, no collapse), dev-8 97.2 / 94.3, **Legal Reports 84.1 / 66.8** under the live judge (live 81.4 / 61.9; the LSTM 78.8 / 66.3) — the collapse was volume; full chain running (2026-09-23) | Three runs with 170–210k typewriter strips collapsed the receipts at any weight and scale; two without them did not | v13b's recipe on the box with `rt_words/rt_full_btp_legal_shard00` under `--lines-once`; the quick verdict | One shard at the Legal 600's scale keeps the receipts where a v13b re-run puts them and buys the Legal Reports 2.7 characters and 4.9 words — past Tesseract's LSTM in both columns on the 40-page set. So the typewriter harvest pays, and pays by the page rather than the strip: what broke v14a/v15a/v15b was 200k typewriter strips against 27k receipt lines, two monospace domains contesting the same hypotheses with one outnumbering the other seven to one. The dial is now explicit — `train_seq.py --weight FILE=W` sets a file's share — and two follow-ups run: v15c through the standard chain (judge `linechoice18s` with the receipt pairs, the twelve evaluations) for adoption, and `v15d` = all five corrected Legal shards and the Rumor Project at a 0.2 share each (one shard's volume over six times the pages) to ask whether page diversity at fixed volume adds anything. |
| ❌ Page diversity at fixed volume (`seq_line_v15d`: v13b plus all five corrected Legal shards and both Rumor shards at a 0.2 share each — about one shard's volume over six times the pages) — receipts reader-only 68.9 / 37.2 (v15c, one whole shard, 71.9 / 41.4), Legal Reports 83.2 / 65.6 (v15c 84.1 / 66.8), dev-8 97.3 / 94.5; NOT adopted (2026-09-23) | If the shards hurt by volume, does spreading the same volume over more pages help the typescript set without costing the receipts? | The `--weight FILE=0.2` dial on twelve files under `--lines-once`; the quick verdict under the live judge | No on both counts: a fifth of every shard reads worse than one whole shard on the receipts (−3 characters, −4 words reader-only) and on the Legal Reports (−0.9 / −1.2). The Rumor Project's strips (23k at a fifth ≈ 5k) are the one new domain in the mix and the likeliest cause of the receipt difference; the Legal Reports difference says a shard's pages are exchangeable — 34k strips from 500 pages teach the same as 34k from 2,500. Recorded: the volume result of v15c stands, the shards are not to be spread thin, and the receipts' share is the variable to move (v15e, running: SROIE lines at weight 5). |
| 🔬 v15c through the chain: under its refitted judge (`linechoice18s`) legal-8 −0.9 word, business −1.0, SROIE 69.4 / 39.4; under the LIVE judge legal-8 identical, SROIE 70.5 / 39.9, Legal Reports 84.1 / 66.8, the 100-page draw 82.5 / 69.2 (live 80.2 / 64.2; LSTM 81.6 / 70.8) — a refit under a new reader can be worse than the judge in hand; v15c itself a mixed result, not adopted (2026-09-23) | The standard chain refits the judge under each new reader; the receipts had asked whether that is always right | The twelve evaluations under `18s` (77.9% decision accuracy on its holdout, against 14s's 79.9), then legal-8 and SROIE controls under 14s | Under 18s, char / word: dev-8 97.3 / 94.4, legal-8 94.1 / 89.6, broad-30 95.3 / 91.4, modern 93.9 / 90.2, business 97.6 / 94.7, SROIE 69.4 / 39.4, FUNSD 54.8 / 31.5, Legal Reports 83.2 / 66.2, news-8 95.5 / 93.0, mag-8 77.1 / 68.7, blocks 98.2 / 95.5, the 100-page draw 82.5 / 69.2. Under 14s (controls): legal-8 94.5 / 90.5, SROIE 70.5 / 39.9, Legal Reports 84.1 / 66.8. The refit lost 0.9 word on legal-8 and 1.0 on business that the live judge does not lose, and gave the Legal Reports 0.9 less: its pairs were harvested under a reader whose classic-vs-reader margins differ from the ones the receipt feature was learned on, and the fit with fewer, more reader-favouring pairs (83.5% taken) generalises worse. Rule taken from it: a candidate reader is measured under the LIVE judge first, and a refit is adopted only if it beats the live judge on the sets a refit has moved before (legal-8, business, SROIE). v15c as a reader: Legal Reports +4.9 words, SROIE −1.6 — the recipe on the box sits under v13b's lucky seed on the receipts (v13c said the same), which is what v15e's receipt weight addresses. |
| 🔬 The receipts' share raised (`seq_line_v15e`: v15c's recipe with the SROIE lines at `--weight 5`, from the real weight of 3; seed 3) — under the live judge SROIE **72.6 / 44.1** (live 71.1 / 41.5; reader-only 74.3 / 46.1, the best any reader has posted), Legal Reports **83.2 / 65.6** (live 81.4 / 61.9), the 100-page draw pending; dev-8 97.4 / 94.2, legal-8 94.3 / 91.0, broad-30 95.2 / 91.1, modern 94.1 / 90.3, FUNSD 55.7 / 30.7, news-8 95.5 / 93.0, mag-8 76.8 / 67.1, blocks 98.3 / 95.7 (dev-8 blocks 96.0, legal-8 blocks 96.5); but business 97.4 / 94.8 — the templated receipts 95.7 → 91.8 words. Seed 2: dev-8 97.3 / 94.5, Legal Reports **85.3 / 68.3**, but SROIE 68.3 / 38.6 (reader-only 70.2 / 40.4). Held for a third seed (2026-09-23) | The dial built for the volume finding: one variable, the receipts' weight, moved | Two seeds so far, each through the quick verdict under the live judge, seed 3 through every set; the templated receipts diagnosed reader-only on their twelve pages (v13b 90.3 words, v15e 86.6) | The standard sets hold on both seeds (seed 3: dev-8 −0.3 word, the rest within a tenth or two or up; seed 2: dev-8 identical). The Legal Reports gain is robust: +3.7 and +6.4 words on the two seeds, past Tesseract's LSTM (66.3) in both columns on both. The receipts are not: +2.6 words on seed 3, −2.9 on seed 2 — a six-word swing on a 60-page set read at 42%, wider than the Legal Reports' own spread, and the templated (synthetic) receipts pay 3.9 words on seed 3 for the real ones' gain, in the reader itself: weighting real thermal print up pulls the reader off clean rendered monospace. What the rule says: adopt when two seeds win; the Legal Reports have their two, the receipts have one, so seed 1 is running. Recorded either way: the per-file weight moves the domain it names, the receipts' own measurement is the least stable in the suite, and the templated receipts are a synthetic set — a real gain against a synthetic loss is the trade the classic profile keeps as the reference for. |
| ❌ v15e's third seed and the refit judge — seed 1: SROIE 66.7 / 36.7 (reader-only 68.4 / 40.2), Legal Reports 83.3 / 65.1, dev-8 97.2 / 94.6; the refit `linechoice19s` (79.8% decision accuracy) under seed 3 level or worse on all four check sets (SROIE 72.3 / 43.6, legal-8 90.7, business 94.6, Legal Reports 83.0 / 65.5); v15e NOT adopted under the two-seed rule (2026-09-23) | The rule asks for two winning seeds; the receipts had one | The third seed through the quick verdict under the live judge; the refit through legal-8, business, SROIE and the Legal Reports | Three seeds of one recipe, receipts under the live judge, words: **44.1, 38.6, 36.7** against the live 41.5 — one win, two losses of three to five words; Legal Reports words: 65.6, 68.3, 65.1 against 61.9 — three wins, every one past Tesseract's LSTM (66.3) in characters and two in words. So the Legal Reports gain is the recipe's and the receipts' movement is the seed's: a 60-page set read at 42 words in a hundred, where a line the judge keeps or drops moves the mean a word, has a seed spread of eight words, wider than any effect measured on it this month, and the live 41.5 is v13b's draw as much as v15e's 44.1 is seed 3's (v13c, the same recipe re-drawn, sat 2.3 words under v13b reader-only). Two consequences recorded: (1) the receipts cannot decide an adoption by one seed in either direction — a receipt claim needs the seed spread beside it from now on, and the seed spread is being measured on the live recipe (`seq_line_v13a`, its other seed); (2) the choice in front of the owner is a reader that wins the typewriter archive on every seed and holds the standard sets, at the receipts' seed lottery — seed 3 as the one draw that wins both, or no change. Not taken without the owner: adopting a one-in-three receipt result would be the same mistake as trusting v13b's. The refit judge, for the record, is now 0 for 3 against the live judge under new readers; the live judge's pairs were harvested under a reader whose margins the refits do not reproduce, and the procedure stays "live judge first". |
| 🔬 The live recipe's own receipt spread — v13b's three seeds under the live judge read SROIE at **41.5 / 40.0 / 39.2** words (v13b, v13a, v13c; reader-only 44.5 / 43.4 / 42.2): mean 40.2, spread 2.3; v15e's three at 44.1 / 38.6 / 36.7: mean 39.8, spread 7.4 (2026-09-23) | A receipt claim needs the seed spread beside it (row above); the live number had none | `seq_line_v13a` (seed 1, 2026-09-20) and `v13c` (the box re-run) on SROIE under `linechoice14s`, reader-only for v13a | The two recipes are level on the receipts on average (40.2 against 39.8 words) and v15e is three times as variable. Read together with the Legal Reports (v13b 61.9; v15e 65.6 / 68.3 / 65.1): the one corrected shard plus the receipt weight buys the typewriter archive four to six words at no average cost on the receipts, with a receipt lottery that seed 3 won and seeds 1–2 lost. The live 41.5 is the top of its own recipe's draw. What this changes in the record: "receipts −2.6 on seed 2" is a seed statement, not a recipe statement; the honest receipt row for any line model is a mean over seeds, and the suite's least stable set should not be the one that vetoes a recipe that every other set accepts. Recommendation to the owner written into the decision: adopt v15e seed 3 (the draw that wins both), or adopt on the Legal Reports evidence with the receipts reported as a three-seed mean; either way the receipts' next lever is variance — more receipt pages, or a receipt-specific stabiliser — not weight. |
| ❌ Model soups of seeds (`scripts/soup_models.py`: the weight mean of v13a/b/c, and of v15e's three seeds, all trained from `seq_en_v5s1`) — worse than every seed they average: v13 soup SROIE 66.0 / 31.7, dev-8 97.1 / 92.6, Legal Reports 78.4 / 58.3; v15e soup 67.1 / 34.1, 96.9 / 93.7, 81.0 / 61.8 (2026-09-23) | The owner held v13b and asked for receipt stability; the receipts swing eight words between seeds, and averaging seeds is the cheapest variance reducer when they share a basin (Wortsman et al., "Model soups", ICML 2022) | The uniform mean of the float weights of three seeds, class list and architecture checked equal; reader under the live judge and reader-only on SROIE, dev-8, the Legal Reports | Every number falls below the worst seed (v13 soup: receipts −7.5 words under the live 39.2–41.5 range, dev-8 −1.9): eight epochs from a word-window init at the recipe's learning rate carry each seed into its own basin, and the midpoint of three basins is a worse model than any of them — the soups paper's own condition (fine-tunes that stay near a shared pre-trained point) does not hold here. Kept: the script (it is correct for snapshots of ONE run). Next, the averages that stay inside one basin: `train_seq.py --save-epochs` (late epochs of one run, souped) and `--ema` (a Polyak average of the weights; Polyak & Juditsky 1992; Izmailov et al., "Averaging weights leads to wider optima", UAI 2018), on two seeds of the live recipe, to see whether either narrows the receipts' seed spread. |
| ❌ Within-run weight averages (`train_seq.py --save-epochs`, `--ema 0.9995`) on two fresh seeds of the live recipe (`seq_line_v16s3`, `v16s2`) — neither narrows the receipts: EMA moves the two seeds +1.1 and −1.3 words, the soup of epochs 6–8 costs 5.0 and 3.3; and the two fresh draws read the receipts at 37.6 / 38.3, so the live recipe's five draws are 41.5 / 40.0 / 39.2 / 37.6 / 38.3 — v13b is the best of five (2026-09-23) | The seed soups failed across basins; averages inside one run's basin were the next candidates | v13b's command on the box with snapshots and an EMA; each seed's final, EMA and late-epoch soup on SROIE (live judge, reader-only), dev-8, the Legal Reports; then a paired bootstrap (5,000 resamples of the 55 receipts every run read) | Char / word, final → EMA → late soup: seed 3 SROIE 68.9 / 37.6 → 68.9 / 38.7 → 64.0 / 32.6, Legal Reports 80.3 / 60.8 → 81.9 / 63.5 → 80.5 / 60.9; seed 2 SROIE 69.3 / 38.3 → 67.8 / 37.0 → 66.3 / 35.0, Legal Reports 82.9 / 64.4 → 82.2 / 63.8 → 83.2 / 65.6; dev-8 within 0.4 everywhere. The late soup averages in epochs the cosine schedule has not finished (6 and 7 at a third and a sixth of the peak rate); the EMA is a fraction of an epoch long and inherits the final seed's draw. The bootstrap says what kind of variance this is: page sampling gives a single mean a 2.5-word standard error, but v13b beats its sibling seeds by +1.4 [0.2, 2.7], +2.2 [1.1, 3.3], +4.2 [3.0, 5.4] and +3.2 [1.5, 5.5] words — the seeds genuinely differ, each moving a receipt by a median 7.6 words. The variance is in the models, not the measurement, and weight averaging does not reach it. Next: an output ensemble (`SeqEnsemble`, a reader path `a.npz+b.npz+c.npz`, frame posteriors averaged in probability; Hansen & Salamon 1990), which keeps each member's basin. |
| 🔬 Output ensembles of seeds (`SeqEnsemble`) — reader-only they read the receipts at or above their members' mean (live recipe 43.8 against 44.5 / 43.4 / 42.2; v15e 42.8 against 46.1 / 40.4 / 40.2), but under the live judge about two words under it (38.6 against a member mean of 40.2; 37.2 against 39.8); standard sets hold or gain a few tenths; the letter 13.8 → 15.4 s (2026-09-23) | The variance is in the models (row above); an output average keeps each member's basin | Three seeds per ensemble, frame posteriors averaged in probability; the eight-set quick board under `linechoice14s`, and reader-only on SROIE. (A first run of the live-recipe ensemble scored the v15e ensemble twice: macOS's bash 3.2 has no associative arrays, so `${M[e13]}` and `${M[e15]}` both resolved to the last value; rerun with plain variables.) | Live-recipe ensemble, char / word: SROIE 68.9 / 38.6 (reader-only 73.5 / 43.8), dev-8 97.3 / 94.5, legal-8 94.4 / 90.6, broad-30 95.3 / 91.3, modern 94.1 / 90.5, business 97.5 / 95.7, Legal Reports 81.2 / 62.7. v15e ensemble: SROIE 67.0 / 37.2 (reader-only 71.7 / 42.8), dev-8 97.3 / 94.5, legal-8 94.5 / 90.9, broad-30 95.3 / 91.3, modern 94.1 / 90.2, business 97.7 / 95.3, Legal Reports 83.0 / 65.5. What an ensemble buys is the members' average, delivered every time rather than by lottery — the stability asked for — and it does so reader-only. The two words lost under the judge are a calibration effect: an averaged posterior is flatter, so the reader's confidence and CTC likelihood features shift down and a judge fitted on single-model features keeps more classic lines; this is the one case where a refit has a stated reason to beat the live judge, and a judge fitted on each ensemble's own pairs is being measured. |
| ✅ A judge fitted on each ensemble's own pairs recovers the calibration loss — live-recipe ensemble + `linechoice_e13s`: SROIE 71.4 / 41.1, dev-8 97.3 / 94.5, legal-8 94.4 / 90.7, broad-30 95.3 / 91.4, modern 94.0 / 90.3, business 97.5 / 95.5, Legal Reports **83.1 / 64.8**; v15e ensemble + `linechoice_e15s` (81.5% decision accuracy, the best any judge has fitted): SROIE 70.3 / 40.1, dev-8 97.3 / 94.5, legal-8 94.6 / **91.1**, broad-30 95.3 / 91.4, modern 93.8 / 89.9, business 97.6 / 95.0, Legal Reports **85.6 / 69.0** (past the LSTM's 78.8 / 66.3 in both columns); decision put to the owner (2026-09-23) | Ensembles lost two receipt words to the live judge by calibration (row above) | Pairs harvested under each three-model reader on the usual five sources, fitted with the receipt pairs, measured on seven sets | The refit is the first to beat the live judge under a new reader, and for the stated reason: it moves the live-recipe ensemble's receipts 38.6 → 41.1 and v15e's 37.2 → 40.1. The live-recipe ensemble now sits within a few tenths of the live model everywhere and gains the Legal Reports 2.9 words — v13b's level, which was the best of five draws, delivered as a fixed combination with no seed lottery. The v15e ensemble carries the typewriter gain stably (+7.1 words on the Legal Reports, +0.6 on legal-8) at receipts −1.4 (inside that set's seed noise) and business −0.7 (outside it). Cost of either: three reader forwards, the letter 13.8 → 15.4 s. |

| 🔬 CORD v2 as a second receipt corpus — photographed receipts (1,000; Park et al., NeurIPS DI workshop 2019; CC-BY-4.0): `scripts/fetch_cord.py` unpacks the Hugging Face parquet into image + row JSON; `make_external_sets.py --cord` writes the official test 100 as `data/ext/cord/eval` (full photos) and `evalcrop` (each receipt cut to its truth rows plus 8%), train + validation 900 as the harvest; `harvest_boxes.py --cord` cuts 6,314 row strips (`data/linesfull_cord.npz`). Baselines on the cropped set, char / word (recall): live 33.5 / 1.4 (28.4), reader-only 34.2 / 4.8 (27.1), v13b 31.3 / −0.5 (27.6), Tesseract LSTM **46.7 / 21.1 (44.1)**; on the full photos everything fails (live recall 20.6, LSTM 35.2) (2026-09-23) | SROIE's 566 harvest receipts are the only real receipt data and its seed spread is eight words; the owner asked for more receipt pages | Only the receipt body is annotated (the header is blurred for privacy), so recall is the honest column; the photos span 864–4,096 px tall under one tag. The full frames put the receipt on fabric, in a hand, beside other paper: texture reads as dozens of junk lines and the type-size estimate reads the texture (5 px on a page whose x-height is ~40) — finding the document in a photo is its own problem, measured and not chased here; the crop is to CORD what the blocks metric is to UNLV. One fault found on the way: the frame rule at 0.5 took shaded paper in a photo for frame and erased the left half of receipts before recognition, and the first harvest (5,800 strips) carried rows with erased words under full labels — discarded and re-harvested at 0.3 (next row), which also recovered 514 strips. Photographed receipts are the widest gap to Tesseract on the board (−16 recall); the CORD strips are in the v17 recipes. |

At 0.3: Rumor page 96.2 / 86.1 → **97.3 / 90.2**; SROIE 70.3 / 40.1 → 70.3 / 40.2; Legal Reports identical; no UNLV, modern, business or FUNSD page carries a frame even at 0.5, so those sets are identical by construction; classic dev-8 reproduces (95.2 / 89.3) (2026-09-23) | The CORD inspection above | The frame share at 0.5 / 0.35 / 0.25 / 0.15 on a Rumor, a CORD, a SROIE and a Legal Reports page; the framed sets re-measured at 0.3 | Adopted. The rule's darkness threshold now sits between the two populations it has met (black scanner lids and dark tables below 0.2, shaded paper at 0.5), with the three-edge qualifier unchanged. |

Read: 6,314 photographed receipt rows close a quarter of the photo-receipt gap to Tesseract (recall +3.2 of 15.7) and cost the scanned receipts two characters while their recall rises — two receipt regimes sharing one weight; the NAWSA/Rumor mix at half a shard each dilutes the Legal Reports' own shard rather than adding a domain, so the typewriter lever is the Legal Reports shard alone.
